### Added
- **[Ingestion]** **Scan a page into a library from the Lock Screen.** Row "Scan a page into a library from the Lock Screen" (5.7 since 2026-10-08); the owner named `project.pbxproj` and the entitlements on 2026-10-17 and signed in to Xcode the same day. The 5.7 "Scan Document" control opens the app's scanner after unlocking; this adds one that doesn't need unlocking. A locked camera capture extension, `OpenIntelligenceCapture` (`Gunndamental.OpenIntelligence.Capture`, ExtensionKit, `com.apple.securecapture`, iOS only), shows Apple's `UIImagePickerController` for photos, which handles the hardware buttons as a capture extension must; Use Photo writes a JPEG to the session's folder (`LockedCameraCaptureSession.sessionContentURL`, in the app's own data container), asks the system to open the app (`openApplication`, which asks to unlock first), and shows a new camera with a line saying how many pages wait for the app; Cancel shows a new camera too (`CaptureScreen`). Its Info.plist carries the app's camera text; Apple says the extension inherits the app's camera permission. The control, "Scan into Library" (`ScanIntoLibraryControl`), runs `ScanIntoLibraryIntent`, a `CameraCaptureIntent` compiled into the app, the widget extension and the capture extension, as Apple's guide asks ("Creating a camera experience for the Lock Screen", developer.apple.com, read 2026-10-17); the guide has the control launch the extension when the device is locked, and when it isn't the intent runs in the app, which opens its own scanner then, as "Scan Document" does (`ScanIntoLibraryIntent.openCaptureInApp`). When the app becomes active it copies each photo from `LockedCameraCaptureManager.sessionContentURLs` into the imported-documents folder, removes it from the session folder, hands a folder whose photos were all stored back to the system (`invalidateSessionContent(at:)`), and hands the copies to the Documents screen's plan check and import review (`LockedCaptureImport`, `.importFiles`), one run at a time across windows. It takes only as many as the plan has room for (`ContentView.documentRoom`): the Documents screen removes a handed-over file it can't add, and the camera's copy is the only one, so with a full plan a page waits in the camera's folder and the app says so once (a review found the loss the same day). `[evidence_level: test_verified (LockedCaptureImportTests: stored once, in name order, removed from the session folder, a folder handed back only when all its photos were stored, kept when it can't be stored, only what the plan has room for taken; on 2026-10-17, on the tree of five commits made together (the last passage, the sale's price by territory, web links, the Lock Screen capture and shares while the plan is full): DEVICE BUILD SUCCEEDED with signing (-allowProvisioningUpdates), TEST BUILD SUCCEEDED, a signed Mac BUILD SUCCEEDED, Executed 1186 tests, with 7 tests skipped and 0 failures, and the 4 UI tests passed (one alone, after it failed to launch the app in the full run); the suite and the UI tests ran after the test simulator's app data was reset, because a share-inbox check had left its library over the free plan's limit), build_verified with signing for the iPhone (the extension embedded under Extensions with its own App ID); confidence: low until it runs, because the Simulator has no camera; closes when a page taken from the Lock Screen control lands in the open library on a device; evidence_source: OpenIntelligenceCapture/, ScanIntoLibraryIntent.swift, LockedCaptureImport.swift, ContentView.swift, IngestionLiveActivityWidget.swift]`
- **[General]** **A web link opens the app where it's installed, and the App Store where it isn't.** Row "Web links to the app open it, or the App Store when it is not installed" (5.7 since 2026-10-08); the owner named the entitlements on 2026-10-17. Every link the app has (`AppLink`) has a web form, `https://gunzino.me/openintelligence/open?to=document/<id>` (`AppLink.webURL(for:)`), and `AppLink.destination(for:)` reads one as the app link it carries, so the handler the app already had takes it: SwiftUI hands a web link that opens the app to `onOpenURL` as the URL (developer.apple.com, read 2026-10-17). `OpenIntelligence.entitlements` carries `com.apple.developer.associated-domains` with `applinks:gunzino.me`. gunzino.me serves the association file naming the app for that one path and the page such a link shows where the app isn't installed, which offers the App Store (Gunzino `5c10b42`, published at the owner's word on 2026-10-17; that day the site served the file with no redirect and Apple's association CDN, `app-site-association.cdn-apple.com/a/v1/gunzino.me`, already served it). Only https on gunzino.me counts (the entitlement names that host alone), and `to` can't carry another address. `[evidence_level: test_verified (AppLinkTests: every destination's web link reads back; other addresses don't; on 2026-10-17, on the tree of five commits made together (the last passage, the sale's price by territory, web links, the Lock Screen capture and shares while the plan is full): DEVICE BUILD SUCCEEDED with signing (-allowProvisioningUpdates), TEST BUILD SUCCEEDED, a signed Mac BUILD SUCCEEDED, Executed 1186 tests, with 7 tests skipped and 0 failures, and the 4 UI tests passed (one alone, after it failed to launch the app in the full run); the suite and the UI tests ran after the test simulator's app data was reset, because a share-inbox check had left its library over the free plan's limit), not opened on a device, which closes the row; confidence: medium; evidence_source: AppNavigationRequest.swift AppLink; OpenIntelligence.entitlements]`

A document card's menu and a library's menu have Copy Link, which copies the web form of the item's link (`AppLink.webURL(for: .document(id))`, `.library(id)`), so Notes, Mail and Messages show it as a link; it opens the item on the person's devices that have the app, and the App Store page where the app isn't installed. - **[Ingestion]** **OpenIntelligence is in the share sheet on iPhone and iPad.** Row "Web Clipper / Share Extension" (5.7 since 2026-10-08); the owner named `project.pbxproj` and the entitlements on 2026-10-17 and signed in to Xcode the same day. A new extension target, `OpenIntelligenceShare` (`Gunndamental.OpenIntelligence.Share`, iOS only, embedded with `platformFilter = ios` like the widget extension), offers itself for a web address (`NSExtensionActivationSupportsWebURLWithMaxCount` 1) and for text; a file still goes in with Open in. `ShareViewController` shows what was shared and, on Add, writes each item to the app group's `ShareInbox` folder (`SharedInbox`, compiled into both targets): a share extension can't reach the app's files or open the app. When the app becomes active, `ContentView` takes the items one run at a time and stores each through the Paste control's staging (`SharedInboxImport`, `PastedContentStaging.stage`); an item leaves the inbox only once it is stored (`SharedInbox.claimAll`, `finish`), one that couldn't be, such as a page while offline, goes back to be tried the next time the app is in front, up to three tries (`putBack`), and items a run left half taken because the app ended are taken again, the one it was storing with that try counted and the ones waiting behind it as they were, so one that ends the app each time is let go after the third, and the app says so: a web address is downloaded the way "Save a Web Page" does it and text is saved as a note, then the Documents screen runs its plan check and import review (`.importFiles`); what was stored goes to the Documents screen at once, and what could not be stored is said in an alert with the Paste control's wording afterwards (a reviewer found that waiting for the alert left stored files to be swept if the app closed while it was up). The extension leaves a file to Open in: text from a provider with a file address or a file name isn't taken, and text over 200,000 characters gets a message of its own, or a line in the sheet when other items are kept. The privacy policy names the share sheet among the ways to give a web address. `[evidence_level: test_verified (SharedInboxTests: claimed oldest first and gone only when finished; a failed item tried again, then let go; a half-taken run recovered, with a try counted for the item it was storing; an unreadable file removed; read as Paste reads; in the iOS 27 Simulator on 2026-10-17, an item left in the inbox was taken and imported on a cold launch (within 8 s) and on a return from the background (within 3 s); on 2026-10-17, on the tree of five commits made together (signing, the share sheet, the tie-breaks, the chunker and the listing scene): DEVICE BUILD SUCCEEDED with signing (-allowProvisioningUpdates), TEST BUILD SUCCEEDED, Mac BUILD SUCCEEDED, UI tests Executed 4 tests, with 0 failures, and Executed 1179 tests, with 6 tests skipped and 0 failures), build_verified with signing for the iPhone; confidence: medium; not shared from Safari on a device yet, which closes the row; evidence_source: OpenIntelligenceShare/ShareViewController.swift, OpenIntelligence/App/SharedInbox.swift, SharedInboxImport.swift, ContentView.swift, project.pbxproj]`

- **[UI]** **A cited passage opens on its PDF page, with the passage highlighted.** The owner's word, 2026-10-16: an answer named the right figure and page from an instructions-for-use PDF, and checking it against the page meant opening the PDF and finding the page by hand, because a citation opened the document's details, whose Quick Look preview starts at page 1 and cannot be sent to a page (`CitationSourceDocument`, the comment at `DocumentDetailsView`'s toolbar). `SourcePageView` opens the PDF at the passage's page in a PDFKit view, finds the passage in that page's text and highlights it (`PassageLocator`: its sentences of four words or more, matched as words in order with any whitespace between them and without regard to case, each by its first 12 words and the last one whole when the page holds it whole, from the first sentence found to the end of the last; a passage cut short with "..." is matched up to the cut), then scrolls to it once the view has a size (`RevealingPDFView.show`). A page with no text layer opens at the page without a highlight, and a page number past the end opens the last page. A saved conversation keeps each passage cut to 600 characters (`ChatMessage`), so the page reads the passage's whole text again by its id from its library (`LibraryDocumentLookup.passageText`, `VectorDatabase.chunks(withIDs:)`) and highlights again when it arrives; a library or passage that is gone leaves the saved copy's highlight. It opens from a citation tapped in an answer, from the page under a Fact Check claim's quote, and from a Page button on each source in Response Details that is a PDF on this device with a page number. Anything else opens the document's details as before, and the details stay one tap away from the page (Document Details). Page numbers count from 1, as the import records them (`pageIndex + 1`). From a refuting review the same day: the claim card's header is its button with the card's padding inside it, so a one-line claim is more than 44 points tall, and a tap elsewhere on an open card closes it as the whole card did through 5.6 instead of reaching the message's own tap; each quote is a button of its own, at least 44 points tall, with a VoiceOver hint that says what it opens (its page or the document's details), in the accent when it opens something and gray when it does not; the Page button's sheet sits outside the `if` that draws the button, so a redraw cannot close an open page; and on the Mac the sheet has a size, because a PDF view hosted in SwiftUI with no frame measured 0 by 0. A probe on macOS 27 (2026-10-16, the same view in a SwiftUI window) found AppKit calls the view's `layout()` once it has its size, after which the cited page was current and the passage highlighted, and on a 500-page PDF opening the file, reading the page's text and finding the passage took 0.2 to 0.5 ms and 1.4 to 1.6 ms. PDFKit's calls were read in the iOS 27 and macOS 27 SDK headers on 2026-10-16 (`goToPage:`, `goToSelection:`, `highlightedSelections`, `PDFPage.string`, `selectionForRange:`). `[evidence_level: test_verified (SourcePageViewTests: PassageLocatorTests, seven, and PassageRevealTests, five, three on a PDF drawn in the test, one that hosts the view in a window and finds page 3 shown with the passage highlighted once it is laid out, and one that highlights again when a whole copy of a cut passage arrives; CitationSourceDocumentTests, which documents open a page), measured (the macOS probe), observed (the owner opened a cited PDF at its page with the passage highlighted on his iPhone, 2026-10-16, on the build before the review's changes); not yet seen on the Mac or in a conversation reopened after a relaunch; confidence: medium until it is; evidence_source: SourcePageView.swift, CitationSourceDocument.swift, GroundedAnswerView.swift, ResponseDetailsView.swift]`
- **[UI]** **"Your Library" widget.** `LibraryWidget` (Home Screen small and medium, Lock Screen rectangular) shows the open library and its document count, and on the medium size the last question in that library with the start of its answer, marked `.privacySensitive()` (WidgetKit hides it on a locked device when the person has chosen to hide private widget data; developer.apple.com, "Creating a widget extension", read 2026-10-15). The app writes a `WidgetSnapshot` (`widget_snapshot.json`) to the app group `group.Gunndamental.OpenIntelligence`: the open library and its count from `ContentView` (once an import ends, not for every document it adds), from the Focus filter when it moves the open library with no screen open (`ContainerService.applyFocusFilter`), and the last answer from `ChatScreen.recordForLibraryWidget` after the conversation is saved. Only a message with an answer's metadata counts, so an error, a stopped answer or the background notice is not written. The answer carries its library, conversation and message ids, and it leaves the widget when another library opens, when the conversation is cleared (`ChatScreen.clearChat`) or deleted (`ThreadSidebarView`), when the answer is hidden or reported and hidden (`MessageBubbleV2`), and when sync removes the conversation after it was deleted on another device (`ContentView.refreshSharedWorkspaceIfNeeded`, `WidgetSnapshot.forgetAnswer(unlessConversationExists:)`). The app asks WidgetKit to redraw only when the file changed, and writes nothing on the Mac, where no widget extension is built. The widget's code is in `IngestionLiveActivityWidget.swift` because the extension target lists its files in `project.pbxproj`. **Offered from 2026-10-17:** it waited until the App IDs carried the app group, which needed the owner's account in Xcode; he signed in that day, a device build with `-allowProvisioningUpdates` registered the group on the app's and the extension's App IDs and made their profiles (the signed app and widget extension carry `com.apple.security.application-groups`, read with `codesign -d --entitlements`), `OpenIntelligenceLiveActivitiesBundle` lists `LibraryWidget`, and What's New has its line. Seen in the iOS 27 Simulator on 2026-10-15 with the group added to a scratch build: the app wrote the library and its 2 documents, then the question and the start of its answer; both sizes drew them on the Home Screen; tapping the widget opened Chat; Clear Chat took the answer off at once. Catalogue row "Widgets". `[evidence_level: test_verified (WidgetSnapshotTests), build_verified, measured in the iOS 27 Simulator with the group in a scratch build (2026-10-15), measured for signing (a device build with -allowProvisioningUpdates and `codesign -d --entitlements` on the app and the widget extension, 2026-10-17); confidence: medium; not on a device; evidence_source: OpenIntelligence/App/WidgetSnapshot.swift; OpenIntelligenceLiveActivities/IngestionLiveActivityWidget.swift]`
- **[UI]** **Swipe a document to delete it (iOS and macOS 27).** The library's cards sit in a `ScrollView`, where `swipeActions` did nothing until iOS and macOS 27 added `swipeActionsContainer()`, which gives a scroll view's rows the coordination a List's have: one row open at a time, closed by scrolling or a tap outside it (developer.apple.com, swipeActionsContainer(), read 2026-10-15). `DocumentLibraryView` puts it on the list and a Delete swipe on each card (`DocumentSwipeToDelete`, `SwipeActionsContainerOn27`). A document only this device has is deleted at once, with the Undo bar; one in an iCloud library asks first with the menu's message (`ModernDocumentCard.sharedDeleteMessage`). Libraries already reorder in the library row (27), and conversations already swipe to delete. Catalogue row "Reorder and swipe in any list", built. Tried in the iOS 27 Simulator on 2026-10-15: a swipe on a sample document showed Delete, Delete took it out of the library with the Undo bar, and Undo, tapped inside the seconds, put it back. `[evidence_level: measured in the Simulator, build_verified; confidence: medium; not tried on a device or on a Mac trackpad; evidence_source: DocumentLibraryView.swift; DocumentCard.swift]`
- **[Orchestration]** **Amounts of money, dates and measurements are checked with Apple's data detector.** `DetectedValueCheck` reads an answer's amounts, dates and measurements with `dataDetectorMatches` (DataDetection) and finds those its passages contradict: an amount the passages state as money only in another currency, a date (written with its year) in a month the passages give other days of and never this one, or a value the passages state only with other units. Gate C fails on one, and a claim with one is Partial (`runGateB`); in a source-only answer the claim is unsupported (`SourceOnlyAnswerService`). The value check before it (`NumericValueExtractor`) matched "€1,200" against "$1,200", "35 kPa" against "35 psi", and "March 15" against any 15. Measured on macOS 27 on 2026-10-15 (a scratch driver over 17 cases): the detector reads "1.200,50 EUR", "$3.4M", "2.5 billion dollars", "due 15.03.2024" (an end date only), date ranges with both days, psi, kPa, gallons, liters, °F, °C, m and mm; it reads no money in "Deposit (USD): 1200" and no unit in "5 mg", "25 Nm", "120 V" or "15 A", which stay with the value check; it makes "next Monday" and "the 5th of March" dates relative to today, so a date without a year is not compared; and it reads "05/03/2024" as May 3, so a date is compared only with days of its own month. Catalogue row "Dates, money and measurements detector", built. `[evidence_level: measured on macOS 27 for the detector's readings, test_verified (DetectedValueCheckTests) in the iOS 27 Simulator; confidence: medium; not run on an answer on a device; evidence_source: DetectedValueCheck.swift; VerificationGateService.swift runGateB, runGateC, evaluateClaim; SourceOnlyAnswerService.swift]`
- **[Infrastructure]** **Background GPU Access.** `OpenIntelligence.entitlements` gains `com.apple.developer.background-tasks.continued-processing.gpu`, added at the owner's word on 2026-10-10. A background import asks the system for the GPU when the system offers it (`BackgroundTaskService`, `request.requiredResources = .gpu`), and the system offers it only with this capability: on the owner's iPhone the task's status file read `backgroundGPUSupported = false` on 2026-10-10 and background imports stopped within seconds, while the Core AI embedder runs on the GPU. A development build signed with the entitlement succeeded with Xcode updating the profile, the signed app carries the key, and it is installed on the owner's iPhone. Not yet seen: a background import continuing with it, and App Store signing through Xcode Cloud with the key in the profile (the first 5.7 cloud build is that test). `[evidence_level: build_verified (device build with -allowProvisioningUpdates, 16:24 PT; codesign shows the key); the behaviour it is for has not been observed, confidence: medium, evidence_source: OpenIntelligence.entitlements, BackgroundTaskService.swift]`
- **[Infrastructure]** **The app records its own hangs, slow launches and background terminations on the device.** `MetricDiagnostics` (`OpenIntelligence/App/MetricDiagnostics.swift`) subscribes to MetricKit's iOS 27 Swift API (`MetricManager`, its diagnostic and metric report sequences): each report becomes "[MetricKit]" lines in the pipeline log and one JSON line in `metric_reports.jsonl` in the local cache folder, newest 200 kept. Nothing leaves the device. It starts at launch, not under tests, and does nothing before iOS 27. Written by a delegated agent from the SDK's interface file and reviewed here. `[evidence_level: build_verified on device and Mac builds; test_verified for the formatting and trimming helpers (MetricDiagnosticsTests); no report has been received on a device yet (MetricKit delivers them on its own schedule), confidence: low until one arrives, evidence_source: MetricDiagnostics.swift]`
- **[Orchestration]** **Two system model tools from iOS 27 are wired in, switched off.** `SystemModelTools` in `FoundationModelToolRegistry.swift` can add `OCRTool` (reads text from an image) and `SpotlightSearchTool` (configured for the app's own Core Spotlight index only, at most 8 results, never the files source) to what `createTools` returns. Each is added only when its switch is set (`systemTools.ocr.enabled`, `systemTools.spotlightSearch.enabled` in `UserDefaults`, both off when unset), because a tool's description takes room in the model's window on every request and neither has been tried on a device with this app's questions. With both off `createTools` returns what it returned before. There is no Settings control for them yet. `[evidence_level: build_verified; test_verified for the switches (SystemToolsAndScanTests); neither tool has been called on a device, confidence: low for behaviour, evidence_source: FoundationModelToolRegistry.swift; _CoreSpotlight_FoundationModels and _Vision_FoundationModels interfaces in the iOS 27 SDK]`
- **[Ingestion]** **JSON, JSON Lines and NDJSON import as records.** Through 5.6 a `.json` file took the code-file lane and `.jsonl` and `.ndjson`, as unknown extensions, took the plain-text lane: each came in as its text as written, cut wherever the chunker's word window fell. `.jsonl` could not be chosen in either picker (its system type is dynamic and conforms only to `public.data`, measured on macOS 27); `.ndjson` could, as `public.text` (roadmap row 3f449a74d54f8199b1fce61c8ff6af0d). `JSONRecordExtractor` reads one record per line of JSON Lines, per element of a top-level array, and per object or list member of a top-level object, and writes each as a `Record N of T` header with one `path: value` line per value. It has its own reader (`JSONValue.parse`), because `JSONSerialization` loses three things a stored passage should keep: members stay in the file's order, a number keeps the file's digits (`12.50` is not rewritten `12.5`), and members with the same name are all kept. A null or empty member is written `null`, `""`, `[]` or `{}`, so its name is not lost. A line that is not JSON is kept as a record holding the line as written, and the count is logged. `processDocument` cuts one passage per record out of the cleaned text; when the headers do not all survive cleanup it logs a warning and chunks the text as prose, so the fallback loses boundaries and no text. A record over the embedding limit is cut at its own field lines, and every part starts with `Record N of T, part K of M`. A leading byte-order mark is taken off, and JSON Lines is split at line feeds only, since JSON allows other separator characters raw inside a string. A file that does not parse, a JSON Lines file over 64 MB and a `.json` file over 16 MB take the text lane as before; the 16 MB figure is a guess at what a phone can hold as one parsed tree and has not been measured. `.jsonl` and `.ndjson` are stored as `DocumentType.json`, so no stored type changes. Both pickers name the two extensions, and the import review no longer calls `.json` reduced-fidelity; a `.json` that falls back to the text lane says so only in the log. `[evidence_level: test_verified, confidence: high; evidence_source: JSONRecordExtractorTests (15 cases) and five IngestionFormatCoverageTests cases that drive real files through processDocument, one of them a single record far over the embedding limit; the full suite on an iOS 27.0 simulator on 2026-10-09 from a copy of the tree made outside iCloud: 670 tests, 0 failures, 3 skipped (the two EmbeddingProviderAgreementTests and LayoutReadingOrderTests' interleaved-stream case, which skip themselves)]` Not seen on a device: no `.jsonl` has been picked in the iOS document picker and no question has been asked against an imported record.
- **[Shortcuts]** **Four new actions: Find Passages, Add to Library, Create Library and Set Active Library.** They live in new files under `Services/Agentic/`. "Find Passages" (`SearchPassagesIntent`) returns the passages `searchDocumentsRaw` finds in the active library, without generating an answer, and takes the tenth App Shortcut slot. "Add to Library" (`AddToLibraryIntent`) takes a file, a link or text and an optional library; each is stored in the imported-documents folder first and then queued, and it returns the stored file names. When one of several things cannot be stored, the rest are still queued and the action throws a message naming both; text is always saved under a text extension. The import runs after the action returns, so a plan's document limit or an unreadable file is reported in the app and not to the shortcut. "Create Library" and "Set Active Library" (`LibraryIntents.swift`) call `ContainerService`, whose library limit applies, and return the library (rows 3f449a74d54f818fa388ed8c60f3d280, 3f449a74d54f815ab796eb064a525ed3, 3f449a74d54f8193bd4be75cb1c301c1). `[evidence_level: build_verified, confidence: high; evidence_source: SearchPassagesIntent.swift, AddToLibraryIntent.swift, LibraryIntents.swift, IntentSupport.swift; OIEntityMappingTests covers the file naming for saved text only; the built metadata (`Metadata.appintents/extract.actionsdata` in the iOS simulator product of 2026-10-09) lists 22 actions where 5.6 listed 16, 15 of them with an output type where 5.6 had none, 5 item types where 5.6 had 2, one enum, and 10 App Shortcuts with 27 phrases where 5.6 had 9 with 23]` None of the four has been run from Shortcuts or Siri, on a simulator or a device.

Links, Spotlight results, files handed over and actions all post to `AppNavigationRequest`, a queue that delivers its contents after each change. `ContentView.route` acts on the oldest request: it sets the tab and the active library and removes a request that ends there; a request whose last step belongs to a screen is left for that screen (`DocumentLibraryView` the file picker and handed-over files, `ChatScreen` a new conversation and the camera, which is Apple's document scanner since 2026-10-14). - **[Indexing]** **The Core AI embedding model is prepared a few seconds after launch when the system's cache has no entry for it.** Core AI specializes a model for the device the first time it is loaded and keeps the result in `AIModelCache`; Apple's article says the entry does not survive a system update. `CoreAISentenceEmbeddingProvider` loads on first use, so the first import or question after an install or an update paid for that. `EmbeddingModelWarmup` asks the cache four seconds after launch and calls `AIModel.specialize(contentsOf:)` at utility priority only when the entry is missing, the app is in front, the system has not asked apps to scale back, and neither a test run nor the validation harness is under way. Nothing coordinates it with the provider: if the provider is created in those seconds, both prepare the model once. A library kept on the Core ML provider gains nothing (row 3f449a74d54f81fd9bede90d7a98dd0e). How long the first preparation takes on an iPhone, which is the case this is for, is not measured; the warm-up logs its duration so a device can show it.

The library and the document have to exist on the receiving device, so they carry over for libraries that sync through iCloud; a local-only library's activity opens the screen in the library that is open there. `Info.plist`, named by the owner for 5.7, gains `NSUserActivityTypes` with the three types. - **[Shortcuts]** **The ask actions can be stopped by the system and, on 27, run past 30 seconds in the background with progress.** "Ask My Documents", "Ask a Library", "Ask About a Document", "Summarize a Document" and "Compare Two Documents" adopt Apple's `CancellableIntent` (iOS and macOS 26.4) and `LongRunningIntent` (27), declared in extensions marked with those versions, and run their question through `AskActionRunner.run`: `withIntentCancellationHandler` on 26.4, `performBackgroundTask` on 27, as through 5.6 before 26.4. The question runs in a task of its own, which a cancellation of the action's own task reaches too (`withTaskCancellationHandler`, all that stops an action before 26.4); when the system stops the action (no progress for about 30 seconds in the background, or a person stopping it in Siri, a Live Activity or Shortcuts) that task is cancelled with the engine's generation (`cancelActiveGeneration`), the action fails with a sentence that says which it was, and the pipeline trace records `[Shortcuts] Ask stopped by the system (timeout|cancelled)`. On 27 the action's progress follows the engine's status line and a two-second tick that keeps changing for as long as the question runs, because the system ends a long-running action whose progress stops; Apple's notes say the system shows that progress as a Live Activity (rows 3f449a74d54f812aadb2d2db3578b35a, 3f449a74d54f8139a380e1fa1cde5c53). Imports from a shortcut are not covered here. A finished action's bar stays full: after `AskControl.finish` a tick or a status changes nothing, and the tick stops on a cancelled sleep; the first version of this change let the last tick grow the total and drop a finished bar to about 91% (second review, 2026-10-14). `[evidence_level: test_verified for AskControl (AskControlTests: one stop runs every handler once and keeps the first reason, progress keeps moving and fills only with the answer, a finished bar stays full), build_verified for iPhone and Mac; confidence: medium; no action was stopped or backgrounded on a device, which is what closes both rows; evidence_source: RAGAppIntents.swift AskControl, AskActionRunner.run; the iOS 27 SDK's AppIntents interface and its documentation]`

- **[UI]** **A long answer shows a Live Activity.** `QueryLiveActivityAttributes` was declared before 5.7 and never started (row 3f449a74d54f8171861cdb4a650b8432). `QueryLiveActivityService` starts one when a question is still running ten seconds after it began, so Deep Think, Maximum and a long Standard answer get one and a quick answer does not (the begin call carries no mode, so time is the test); it shows the title, step and fraction the background task already reports, at most every 1.5 seconds, and ends with "Answer ready" or "Stopped", kept five minutes. ActivityKit starts a Live Activity only for an app in front (`ActivityAuthorizationError.visibility`), so the ten-second start is made only while the app is active, and one is also started when the app is about to leave the front (`UIApplication.willResignActiveNotification`) with a question still running, so locking the phone or opening another app within the ten seconds still gets one; one left by an earlier launch is ended at launch (second review, 2026-10-14; whether a start made at that moment succeeds has not been observed). - **[UI]** **The long answer's Live Activity has a Stop button.** `StopLongAnswerIntent`, a `LiveActivityIntent` declared beside `QueryLiveActivityAttributes` (which the app and the widget extension both compile, and the engine framework does not), draws a Stop button on the Lock Screen view and in the Dynamic Island's expanded view; the system runs its `perform()` in the app, which posts `Notification.Name.stopLongAnswer` with the Live Activity's `sessionID`, and `ChatScreen` stops the answer with its own `stopGeneration()`, keeping the text already written, only when `QueryLiveActivityService.isRunningActivity(sessionID:)` says that Live Activity is the running answer's. An ended Live Activity stays up five minutes, so a Stop tapped on one cannot stop a newer answer. `QueryLiveActivityAttributes.ContentState` gains an optional `finished`, set when the answer ends, which takes the button away; the watch's small layout has no button. iPhone and iPad. `[evidence_level: code_verified, confidence: medium, evidence_source: IngestionLiveActivityAttributes.swift, QueryLiveActivityService.swift, IngestionLiveActivityWidget.swift, ChatScreen.swift; not yet seen on a device]`

They are `SwiftUI.Tab(_:systemImage:value:)` now (the view's own `Tab` enum is the selection type), the Chat badge rides on its tab, and on iOS `.tabBarMinimizeBehavior(.onScrollDown)` lets the bar shrink while a conversation or a library scrolls. The iPad sidebar (`sidebarAdaptable`), the bottom accessory, sheets and navigation bars are still open on the row. `[evidence_level: build_verified, confidence: medium; not seen on a device]`
- **[Infrastructure]** **A deleted library stays deleted.** Two ways brought one back (roadmap row "Deleted library comes back"). Every window on the Mac, and the SDK engine, holds its own `ContainerService` list, and a window that had not done the delete wrote the library back with its next save. And `LibraryDeletion` reloads the list from disk right after `deleteContainer`, whose write was queued (`listWriteQueue`), so the reload could read the list from before the delete and the next save wrote it back. `ContainerService` now keeps the ids of libraries deleted on the device (`deletedContainerIds()`, the newest 500 under `deletedContainerIds` in the device's settings; a library id is never reused), leaves them out of every save and every load (a load that left one out saves the list again), and `loadContainers` waits for the writes queued before it (an empty `sync` on the serial write queue; the app has no file presenter, so the wait cannot deadlock on the main thread). A library that came back came back empty: its documents were already kept deleted. Not across devices: a library deleted on one device and still on another stays on the other. `[evidence_level: test_verified (DeletedLibraryTests: two ContainerService objects for two windows, and a reload right after a delete), confidence: medium; not seen with two Mac windows]`
- **[Ingestion]** **A Pause button for the import queue.** `RAGService.pauseIngestionQueue` stops the running import the way the end of the app's background time does (`cancelIngestionTaskAndSetItAside`, no cancellation recorded, so a file imported in pieces keeps its checkpoint), sets every unfinished item to `.paused` with a detail that says whether it continues from where it stopped (`restoredIngestionProgress` found a checkpoint) or starts over, and sets `ingestionPausedByPerson`, which `handleContinuedIngestionExpiration` honours so the stopped loop's exit doesn't queue the items again. The queue then waits as it does after a restart: `IngestionQueueOverlay` shows its Continue and Discard, with "Uploads paused" wording when the person paused it, and a pause button in its header while an import runs (Chat and Documents pass `onPause`). Continue logs "Continued after a pause". Closes when a large PDF paused part-way on a device continues from its last piece. `[evidence_level: test_verified (PauseIngestionTests), confidence: medium]`
- **[Ingestion]** **The text of an import that never finished is removed once a launch.** `RAGService.removeTextOfUnfinishedImports`, after the persisted queue is restored and only with no import queued or running, removes the keyword store's text for every document id the store holds and the document list read from disk does not (`getAllDocumentIds`, `delete(for:)`; `SQLiteFullTextService.swift` unchanged). An empty or unreadable list removes nothing. The store's file shrinks only through Optimize Database. `[evidence_level: test_verified (UnfinishedImportTextTests), confidence: medium]`
- **[Ingestion]** **Text set sideways along a page's edge is left out.** `LayoutAwareExtractor.isMarginStrip` drops a box more than three times taller than wide that sits within 6 percent of the left or right edge or runs over half the page's height, in the Vision path and the PDF text-layer path. Measured with Vision on the journal article of the roadmap row on 2026-10-14 (macOS 27, the app's 5x render): the download notice down the right edge of pages 2 to 8 was 0.011 to 0.013 wide and 0.958 tall, at x 0.968 to 0.981, and it was read in at confidence 1.0; two rotated figure labels (0.015 by 0.042, 0.013 by 0.094) and every body line stay. The row's device capture, "IN Pychatry an" at x 0.08 to 0.25, is the journal's horizontal logo at the top of page 1 on the Mac, so the logo becoming a heading is still open. `[evidence_level: measured, test_verified (LayoutMarginStripTests); confidence: medium]`
- **[Ingestion]** **Apple's data detector reads the whole document, not only table cells.** `extractDetectedData` had one caller, `parseTable`, so the addresses, phone numbers, dates and amounts the 5.3 notes describe as recognised "when a page is read" were found only in table cells, and a page read from its text layer never reached the detector (roadmap row "Entities come from table cells only"). `StructuredDocumentParser.detectedEntities(inText:characterLimit:)` (`@concurrent`) runs `dataDetectorMatches` (iOS and macOS 26) on the document's chunkable text, the first 1,000,000 characters, plus the regex sweep for missing types, and `DocumentProcessor.mergeDetectedEntities` adds them to the cells' entities without repeats, which the library's vocabulary learns as before (`ContainerVocabularyService.learnFromDetectedEntities`), so query expansion now knows them. Measured on the Mac on 2026-10-14: 0.7 to 1.1 million characters a second. `[evidence_level: measured, test_verified (TextEntityDetectionTests), confidence: medium; not run on a device, and retrieval with the larger vocabulary not benchmarked]`
- **[Orchestration]** **Deep Think keeps its synthesis when research's short answer says something new.** At the call site where `executeRecursiveResearch`'s answer replaces the chain's synthesis, `researchMayReplaceSynthesis(question:synthesis:research:)` must also pass: a synthesis of 400 characters or more gives way to an answer under a quarter of its length only when 60 percent or more of the answer's content words beyond the question's own (`answerWords`: three letters or more or a number, no stop words, "nine" counted as "9") appear in the synthesis. On 2026-09-23 a 3,372-character synthesis was replaced by "OpenIntelligence handles file types such as notes, warnings, exceptions, and documents.", which is wrong; `shouldAcceptReplacement` let it through because the synthesis carried no citation markers. A rejected replacement is logged. `shouldAcceptReplacement` and its three other call sites are unchanged. `[evidence_level: test_verified (AnswerReplacementGuardTests, the two 2026-09-23 answers against written syntheses), confidence: medium; not run on a Deep Think benchmark]`
- **[Orchestration]** **Deep Think and Maximum answers run the verification gates.** Every mode was listed as verifying (`RAGQualityMode.usesVerificationGates`, and Settings' Verify) while `executeAgenticQuery` returned before the gates, so a Deep Think or Maximum answer read Not Checked unless the source-only check ran (roadmap row "The Verified badge shows for answers whose checks failed or never ran, and Deep Think and Maximum run no verification gates", release test 2). When the source-only check did not run, `verifyAgenticAnswer` runs `VerificationGateService.verify` with Standard's confidence policy on the passages the reasoning ended with, with the answer's and the question's vectors and the passages' stored vectors, and the status string reads `verified:NN%` or `unverified:<gates>` (`verificationSummary(for:)`, Standard's format), so the badge reads Verified or Unverified and the failed gates are listed as warnings. The answer is left as written; a failed check does not abstain (`Docs/ai/DECISIONS.md`, 2026-10-14). Closes when a Deep Think answer on a device shows the result of its gates. `[evidence_level: build_verified, confidence: medium; the Simulator has no model, so no Deep Think answer ran]`
- **[Orchestration]** **An extracted value stands in for the answer only when it is about what was asked.** `highPrecisionLookupOverrideAnswer`, which all five places that let `SpecificationExtractor`'s value replace or pre-empt the model's answer go through, now also requires `extractedValueAnswersQuestion`: the value's label, or the sentence of the line that holds it (`valueLine`), must share a word with the question's subject. The subject is the question's words of three letters or more without `genericValueWords` (question words, quantity words, units, function words and the verbs a question asks with), with the extractor's synonyms added (`SpecificationExtractor.subjectSynonyms`, which the extractor now reads too); `lookupWords` drops a plural's final "s" only from a word of five letters or more and not after "ss", "us" or "is". A question with no subject words left is not checked; a value that fails is not used, and the model answers as it would without one. On the passages of the 2026-09-26 lock probe (`Docs/AuditArtifacts/RAGArchitectureAudit_2026-09-26/swift/`), run through the real extractor, the three wrong locks (the engine oil's 4.5 L for two fuel-tank questions and its 4.8 US qt for coolant) are refused and the seven right ones are kept. Corrected 2026-10-15, after a refuting review of d78ce2f: the first version judged a value by the extractor's label or its `surroundingContext`. On this path the label is always empty, and the context, cut into sentences at every ".", never held a value with a decimal point and fell back to 50 characters each side, which reached the neighbouring lines' labels; so the probe's wrong locks still passed, while the tests, written with a label, passed too. "does" and "this" had also been folded to "doe" and "thi" and stayed in every subject. And the entry's second example was wrong: on 2026-09-26 nothing locked on the lease, the model wrote "30 days" itself, and the extractor has no unit for days. The extractor's scoring is unchanged. `[evidence_level: test_verified (ExtractedValueRelevanceTests, which run SpecificationExtractor on the probe's passages), confidence: medium; the false-lock rate on real libraries is not measured]`

Structure-aware chunking still runs on the main actor (roadmap row "Import passes on the main thread"). `[evidence_level: code_verified, confidence: high]`
- **[Ingestion]** **The stage ledger counts off the main thread, without a substring per word.** The four readings of `IngestionStageLedger` in `processDocument` (extraction, chunked, sanitized, token-limited) counted every passage's characters and words on the main actor, and the words by `split(whereSeparator:)`: about 430 ms a reading for 8 MB of text on an M3 Pro built `-O`, about 1.7 s an import, and 1.4 million substrings each time. `IngestionStageLedger.counts(of:)` and `wordCount(of:)` are `nonisolated`, count the runs of non-whitespace characters without making substrings, and `DocumentProcessor.ledgerCounts` runs them on the concurrent pool (`@concurrent`); `DocumentProcessor.wordCount(of:)`, the Words figure, counts the same way. The counts are the split's (`testWordCountIsTheCountSplittingGives`, `testCountsAreWhatRecordingFromTheTextsGives`). The category check stays: it counts the whole text once, 15 ms for 8 MB on the same Mac, and reads at most 12,000 characters. It logs a warning with how many were left out whenever the ceiling binds. The ranking is unchanged. - **[UI]** **A question can be spoken into the chat field on iPhone, iPad and Mac.** Settings described the app's speech feature as "Audio transcription & voice input", and the microphone permission text said "Record voice queries or dictate text", but through 5.6 the app had no microphone code (backlog row a60d2bf8ab4f428194d7d373c7c8b347, found by the triage of 2026-10-14). The field has a microphone button now. `VoiceDictation` asks for the microphone (`AVAudioApplication.requestRecordPermission`), runs Apple's `DictationTranscriber` with the `progressiveShortDictation` preset on the device (the system installs its model through `AssetInventory` the first time), feeds it the microphone through `AVAudioEngine`, converted to the format `SpeechAnalyzer.bestAvailableAudioFormat` gives, and puts the words after what the field held as they are recognised; a second tap or sending stops it, and nothing is sent by itself. A stop that comes while dictation is still preparing (sending, or leaving the chat, during the permission prompt or the model's first install) ends it before the microphone starts, and stopping waits at most three seconds for the last words before it cancels the analysis, so the button cannot stay on Stop. - **[Shortcuts]** **Visual Intelligence lists matching documents.** `VisualDocumentSearchQuery`, an `IntentValueQuery` over `SemanticContentDescriptor` (iOS 26, macOS 27; an app may have one, and this is the app's only one), reads the picture's text on the device with Vision's `RecognizeTextRequest`, adds the system's labels, keeps at most 12 words (`VisualSearchTerms.from`: words with both digits and letters first, such as a model number, then the longest, then the labels, with no common words), and returns at most 100 documents: those whose name holds a word, then those whose passages match in the keyword index across every library (`SQLiteFullTextService.searchChunks`, which tries every word and then any word). A result opens with `OpenDocumentIntent`. "More results" is `VisualDocumentSearchResultsIntent` under `.visualIntelligence.semanticContentSearch`; its one parameter, `semanticContent`, and that it opens the app are read from Xcode 27's own schema database (`AppIntentSchemas.sqlite`, `ShowVisualSearchResultsInAppIntent`), and it opens the library's search with the same words (`.searchFor`). It lives in `Features/Camera`, which the engine framework does not compile. The iOS Simulator's SDK has no Visual Intelligence framework (the device and Mac SDKs do, measured 2026-10-14), so the query compiles for a device and the Mac only, and the word choice sits outside its guard, where the suite runs it. - **[Shortcuts]** **"Delete Documents".** A `DeleteIntent` that asks first (`requestConfirmation`, naming the documents and saying their passages and Spotlight entries go too) and deletes through `RAGService.removeDocument`. It deletes nothing unless the engine's list of documents holds every one asked for (`DocumentDeletion.documents`, up to three seconds for an engine made for the action, which reads its list a moment after it is made): `removeDocument` works from the engine's own record of each document, and a document no longer there stops the action before anything is deleted. A second review found that a save wrote back every document the engine held, even one recorded as deleted, so an engine that had read the list before a delete (the app's, opened during the action, or another Mac window) put the document back at its next save; `saveDocumentsToDisk` now leaves out documents recorded as deleted, whose ids never come back. Corrected 2026-10-15: run in the open app it can be undone for a few seconds (`UndoableIntent`; "A deletion can be undone for a few seconds", below). `[evidence_level: test_verified for the question and the refusal (DocumentDeletionTests), build_verified; confidence: medium; not run from Shortcuts on a device; evidence_source: DeleteIntents.swift; RAGService.swift saveDocumentsToDisk]`
- **[UI]** **A deletion can be undone for a few seconds.** Deleting a document in the app (`ModernDocumentCard`) calls `RAGService.scheduleRemoval`, which takes the documents out of `documents` at once, with their passages out of `totalChunksStored` and their library's figures, and runs `removeDocument` when `PendingDocumentRemoval.undoWindow` (6 s) ends; `undoRemoval` puts them back where they were in the list. The library shows an Undo bar for the newest pending removal (`DocumentLibraryView.pendingRemovalBar`), and the screen's undo manager gets an entry, which holds its own target and is removed when the removal runs. Until then nothing is removed: passages, text, Spotlight entries, the file and the iCloud copy are as they were; a reload of the list leaves the documents out (`applyLoadedDocuments`), searches leave their passages out (`passagesFromKnownDocuments`), and Shortcuts and Siri no longer list them (`OIDocumentEntityQuery.loadDocumentRecords`), while Spotlight still finds them. A pending removal runs when the app goes to the background, after the vector stores are flushed and with a second flush after it (`ContentView`); before a library is deleted or emptied and before Clear All (`commitPendingRemovals`, which also waits for a removal already running); and at the next launch when the app ended in those seconds (`removeDocumentsLeftPending`), the ids being kept per run of the app in `pending_document_removals.json` (`PendingDocumentRemovalLedger`, on the device only; iCloud Sync copies named files). A ledger that cannot be written after an undo is deleted, so a failure leaves a document in place rather than removing one that was put back. A rebuild skips a document whose removal is pending, which it would otherwise import again under a new id. The timer holds the engine until it fires, so a Mac window closed in those seconds still runs its removal. Delete Everywhere's iCloud delete runs with the removal. `removeDocument` now subtracts a document's passages only when the document was still in the list. "Delete Documents" adopts `UndoableIntent`: run in the open app, on the app's own engine (`isTheAppsEngine`, set by `ContentView`, since `activePresentedInstance` is also taken by an engine an action builds with the app closed), it schedules the same removal with the undo manager the system hands it and says the deletion can be undone; otherwise it removes at once. A refuting review the same day found the eight problems these last fixes answer. - **[Shortcuts]** **Documents and libraries are the same item on every device.** `OIDocumentEntity` and `OILibraryEntity` adopt `SyncableEntity` (iOS and macOS 27), which tells the system an item's identifier is the same across a person's devices, so Siri can carry a reference to one from a device to another. Apple's documentation says an entity whose identifier is already consistent across devices, its example a UUID, adopts it with no other change (developer.apple.com, read 2026-10-14); a document's and a library's UUID is made with the item and the shared workspace carries it unchanged. Conversations are left out while how they sync is an open decision. - **[Infrastructure]** **The system's diagnostics say whether an import the person started or a chat question was running.** `AppStateReporting` reports two StateReporting domains (iOS and macOS 27): `com.openintelligence.import` ("Importing") and `com.openintelligence.answer` ("Standard", "Deep Think" or "Maximum", the chat's mode setting), set and cleared from the begin and complete hooks of an import the person started and of a question asked in the chat (an import stopped, dismissed or cleared ends its state through the hook that ends its Live Activity), a few times an import or question, within Apple's rate limit. A question from Siri or Shortcuts and an automatic rebuild report nothing. Labels are fixed and carry no metadata, and no document or question is in one. `MetricDiagnostics` makes its `MetricManager` with those two domains enabled, so MetricKit's reports split by them, and its one-line summary of a diagnostic names the states active up to it (" during answer Deep Think"). It answers the question the large library raised: which work the app was doing when the system ended it for memory. Catalogue row "Metrics per app state". `[evidence_level: test_verified for the labels (AppStateReportingTests), build_verified; confidence: medium; no report has been delivered on a device; evidence_source: AppStateReporting.swift; MetricDiagnostics.swift; developer.apple.com "Getting started with StateReporting", read 2026-10-14]`
- **[Shortcuts]** **Siri's answer card has a button for each document the answer rests on.** The five ask actions returned a still card with the question, the answer and the counts of passages and documents (`RAGResponseSnippetView`). They now return `AnswerCardSnippetIntent`, a `SnippetIntent` (iOS and macOS 26) whose card shows the question, the answer without Markdown marks and cut at a word near 600 characters, and up to three buttons, each running `OpenDocumentIntent`: one per document of the passages the answer's evidence list names, in that list's order (`StructuredAnswer.evidence`, which the chat's citation links resolve against, narrowed to the passages the accepted claims cite when they cite any), or, for an answer with no evidence list, per document of its retrieved passages in the order they were retrieved (`AskActionRunner.sourceDocumentIds`). The documents are taken from the engine's lists already in memory, so making the card reads no file. A review on 2026-10-14 found the first version took the retrieved passages' order while this entry called its documents cited, and decoded the whole documents file on the main actor to make the card; both were changed before it was committed. Apple's guide says the system may create and perform a snippet intent many times while its card is up and asks for no side effects and the least data ("Displaying static and interactive snippets", developer.apple.com, read 2026-10-14), so it only builds the view, and it is handed the answer already cut. The ten App Shortcuts are unchanged. For a Standard answer from a whole library the card also has a Think Deeper button (`ThinkDeeperIntent`, added the same day and not listed in Shortcuts), which opens the app on the library the question ran in, switches the chat to Deep Think as the chat's own "Switch to Deep Think" does, and sends the question (`AppDestination.ask(_:sendsAtOnce:inDeepThink:libraryId:)`); Deep Think runs for minutes, so its answer arrives in the chat, not in the card, and the chat stays in Deep Think afterwards. While an answer is still being written in the chat, the button changes nothing and says so. A Deep Think or Maximum answer has no such button, nor does a question kept to chosen documents, which the chat would ask of the whole library (`AskActionRunner.offersThinkDeeper`). A card for a question asked of a whole library also has an Ask a Follow-up button (`AskFollowUpIntent`, not listed in Shortcuts; `offersFollowUp`), which opens the app on that library in a new conversation that starts with the question and the answer as the card showed it (cut at a word near 600 characters, without its sources), saved as that library's chat history (`AppDestination.continueAnswer`). The engine gives the next question the conversation so far, from the library's conversation memory when that holds turns and from the chat history when it does not; Siri's question normally reached the memory already, since the engine records an answer there when it finishes one (`RAGService.finalizeResponse`), and the turn is added when the memory's latest turn is another one (`ConversationMemoryService.addTurn`). So a question such as "and for pets?" is asked as a follow-up, where the settings for conversation memory and question rewriting are on, as they are by default. An answer still being written in the chat is left to finish, with a note. Both buttons' actions declare `supportedModes = .foreground(.immediate)` (iOS and macOS 26), so the app comes forward before they post their request. A review the same day found the first version of both buttons asked in the active library whatever library the question ran in, offered a follow-up after a question kept to chosen documents, and let Think Deeper switch the mode under an answer being written; all three were changed the same day. - **[Shortcuts]** **"Check a Claim".** A new action that checks a sentence against the active library without writing an answer: the six passages that match it best are found as "Find Passages" finds them (`searchDocumentsRaw`, trimmed to six), and `VerificationGateService.verify` runs on them with the sentence as its one claim (`ClaimCheck.check`). Its evidence-coverage verdict is returned as "Supported", "Partly supported" or "Not supported" ("Not found" when nothing matches), so a shortcut can test it, and the spoken line names the passage the verdict rests on and says when the number gate found a number that does not match. These are the coverage and number checks a Standard answer gets. The search embeds the sentence and the gate takes its key words from Apple's noun tagger, but no language model judges it, and the check that compares meanings (Gate E) has no vectors to work with here. A second review found two faults in the first version, both fixed before it was committed: the sentence was also passed as the check's question, and the check counts a word in the question as found, so nearly any passage held it up at least partly (the question is now empty); and word overlap cannot tell "pets are allowed" from "pets are not allowed", so when the passage's matching sentence and the claim disagree about a negation a Supported verdict drops to Partly supported and the line says to read the passage. The suite then found a third: the gate takes a sentence's key words from Apple's noun tagger and counts a sentence it finds no nouns in as covered, and in the iOS Simulator an unrelated passage came back Partly supported where the Mac's tagger finds the nouns and the gate says Not supported [inferred: the simulator's tagger found none]; so a Partly supported verdict also needs half the sentence's content words in the passages (`ClaimCheck.contentWordCoverage`). A third review found two more, also fixed before it was committed: the negation check read the sentences after the matching one as well, so a "not" anywhere after it lowered a passage that said just what the claim said, and it counted the bounds and set phrases of a lease as negations ("no later than the 5th", "not less than 30 days", "Unit No. 4", "without limitation") while missing "non-refundable" and "prohibited". It now reads only the sentence that shares the most content words with the claim (`ClaimCheck.matchingSentence`), takes those phrases out first, and counts words starting with "non" and forms of "prohibit" and "forbid" (`ClaimCheck.hasNegation`). The gate itself is unchanged, since every answer goes through it; that is filed in the backlog. No App Shortcut slot is used. Catalogue row "Check a sentence against a library". `[evidence_level: test_verified for an unrelated, a matching, an opposite and a prohibiting passage and a "not" in the next sentence through the real check, and for the negation words and phrases, the labels and the line (ClaimCheckTests), build_verified; confidence: medium; not run from Shortcuts on a device; evidence_source: CheckClaimIntent.swift; VerificationGateService.swift verify, runGateB, isClaimCovered]`

- **[Shortcuts]** **The actions run through the system in tests, in a new UI-testing target.** `OpenIntelligenceUITests`, with a scheme of its own so the unit suite is unchanged, uses Apple's AppIntentsTesting (iOS 27), which sends an action by name to the running app through the App Intents infrastructure, so an action's own `perform()` runs with the context the system gives it. `ShortcutsSystemTests` adds a note with "Add to Library" and waits until the document lookup Siri and Shortcuts use to find a document by name lists it with its passages; then one test runs "Ask My Documents" itself, the long-running action the unit suite cannot call, and checks that the answer gives the note's figure and has passages, and the other runs "Open Document" with the item the lookup returned and checks that the app shows the note. The app is launched with a stand-in model, in Debug builds only (`UITestSupport`, switched on by the launch argument `--uitest-stand-in-model`; a release build has none of it), and a Debug-only action that Shortcuts does not list removes the tests' notes by the start of their names (eight characters or more). The tests do not wait on "Find Passages" or "Check a Claim": a library made in the Simulator holds all-zero vectors and a note alone in its library gets no keyword boost, so the similarity floor of 0.22 to 0.50 drops the note there, while the keyword search found it every second (measured 2026-10-14 from the app's trace). The owner approved a UI-testing target, with the project-file change it needs, on 2026-10-14. - **[Shortcuts]** **A question from Siri or Shortcuts that needs Private Cloud Compute brings the app forward and asks.** The five ask actions run in the background and may now come forward (`supportedModes` `[.background, .foreground(.dynamic)]`, in place of `openAppWhenRun = false`). When the system lets one come forward (`systemContext.currentMode.canContinueInForeground`), its question counts as one a person can be asked about, so the planner may choose Private Cloud Compute, and before anything is sent the consent gate calls `continueInForeground` and the same consent sheet opens in the app (`AskActionRunner.foregroundRequest`, `RAGService.bringAppForwardForConsent`). The chat shows the sheet only for its own engine, so a request from the engine an action builds for its question goes to `CloudConsentRelay`, and `ContentView` shows it, in one window only. A sheet no window shows within 15 seconds is given up: the question goes on without Private Cloud Compute and nothing is remembered, unlike Don't Allow. Through the first 5.7 builds, with consent not yet given, a background action never used Private Cloud Compute, and an action whose question reached the consent step while the app was in front (on the Mac, whenever the app was active) waited for good on a sheet nothing showed (read from the code, not observed). A refuting review the same evening found the first build of this wrong in four ways, corrected the same evening: no window could show the relay's request, because `@Published` announces a value before storing it and the claim read the stored one (the relay now keeps the request in `current` first, and a test claims from inside the announcement); the relay was chosen by the weak `activePresentedInstance` pointer, which could leave an action's request with nothing to show it or send a chat's own request there as well (an action's engine is now marked, `asksThroughRelay`); a choice already made was applied only after the app came forward, so Always Allow pulled the app forward for nothing and, on iPhone, a question from the background never used it (the stored choice now comes first and is read again at the start of each question and at each step, `refreshCloudConsentFromDefaults`, so a change in Settings counts at once); and a sheet on screen could be taken away by a newer request (the newer one is now given up). On the Mac an action's question now asks only when the app is in front, and a question whose sheet went unseen asks no more. The owner gave his word for wiring the actions to the consent step on 2026-10-14. Roadmap row 3f449a74d54f81fdaac9e3f22ba32c84. `[evidence_level: build_verified; test_verified for the relay and the engine's side (CloudConsentRelayTests); code_verified for an action coming forward, which needs the system's context and a device where Private Cloud Compute runs (the Simulator never selects it), confidence: medium until a device run, evidence_source: RAGService.swift ensureCloudConsentIfNeeded, cloudConsentDecision and makePostRetrievalModelPlan; RAGAppIntents.swift AskActionRunner.foregroundRequest; CloudConsentRelay.swift; ContentView.swift]` Not covered: a device, and Siri's own prompt to continue in the app.
- **[Indexing]** **Conversations sync through iCloud.** Conversations (Evidence Threads) were saved in `<Application Support>/EvidenceThreads/<library>/`, and iCloud sync reads and writes `<Application Support>/OpenIntelligence/EvidenceThreads/<library>/` (`WorkspaceSyncService.synchronizeEvidenceThreads`, whose local root is `OpenIntelligenceRuntimePaths.applicationSupportRoot()`), so a conversation stayed on the device it was had on, in a library set to iCloud Drive as in any other, while the store listing, the canonical doc (section 11) and the Atlas (section 15) said threads sync. `EvidenceThreadStore` now saves in `OpenIntelligenceRuntimePaths.baseDirectory()/EvidenceThreads`, beside the chat history and conversation memory that already synced, and the first store made after the update moves threads from both earlier folders (`EvidenceThreads` and `LocalCache/EvidenceThreads`), keeping their modification dates, which sync compares, and keeping the newer copy when a thread is in both places. A store pointed elsewhere, as by a benchmark run, takes no threads from the app's folders. Sync's merge copies the newer file each way and deletes nothing that is missing on one side, so the move loses nothing (`synchronizeEvidenceThreads`, read 2026-10-14), and a thread saved there now also starts a sync pass, since `coordinatedWriteData` posts `.localWorkspaceDidChange` for paths under that root. Roadmap row 3e649a74d54f811492b6d0d8f5e89f71, pulled into 5.7 under release test 2 (an advertised capability that did not work). A refuting review the same evening found that sync kept no record of deletions, so once threads synced, a deleted conversation came back from iCloud on the next pass and deleting could not bring a library under its conversation limit: `EvidenceThreadStore.deleteThread` now records the id in `deleted_threads.json` beside the threads folder, `WorkspaceSyncService.synchronizeEvidenceThreads` joins that list across devices (`mergeDeletedThreads`, as `deleted_documents.json` does for documents) and removes those conversations on both sides, and `listThreads` hides one still on its way out. A conversation syncs as one file and the newest copy wins, as the chat history does, so turns added on two devices before they sync are kept from one of them only. `[evidence_level: build_verified; test_verified (EvidenceThreadStoreTests: the app's store saves in the folder sync reads, the move keeps dates and the newer copy, a store elsewhere takes nothing); not observed on two devices, confidence: medium until a conversation made on one device shows on another, evidence_source: EvidenceThreadStore.swift; WorkspaceSyncService.swift synchronizeEvidenceThreads; OpenIntelligenceRuntimePaths.swift]`
- **[Infrastructure]** **A crash report the person can send.** When MetricKit hands the app a crash diagnostic after it closed unexpectedly (iOS 27 and macOS 27), `MetricDiagnostics` saves it as a file of its own in the local cache's `CrashReports` folder (`CrashReports`, newest ten kept), and the app offers it once, in the first window to ask (`takePending`): Email Report opens a message to the feedback address with a short summary in the person's own mail app, and Settings, About has Email the Last Crash Report and Share the Last Crash Report, which shares the whole file. A saved report holds the app and system versions, the device model, how the app ended (termination category and reason, exception type and code, signal, the exception's name and class), which of the app's states were running (`AppStateReporting`) and the call stack; it leaves out an Objective-C exception's message text and arguments, which can carry strings the app was handling, the memory region description, which can name a mapped file, and the region format, process id and signposts; a crash delivered twice is saved and offered once (the file's name carries a digest of its contents). Those three were added after a refuting review the same evening. Nothing is sent unless the person sends it. The roadmap row planned a crash reporter extension (CrashReportExtension, iOS 27); a new extension target needs a provisioning profile this Mac's Xcode cannot make without the owner's Apple account, and MetricKit already delivers the crash at the next launch, so it is built in the app. The in-app privacy policy says a crash report leaves only if the person emails or shares it. Roadmap row 3f449a74d54f81fa83cafeeb9cbf6344. `[evidence_level: build_verified; test_verified for saving, the one offer, keeping ten and the email (CrashReportsTests); no crash diagnostic has been delivered on a device yet, confidence: medium until one is, evidence_source: CrashReports.swift, MetricDiagnostics.swift, ContentView.swift, AboutView.swift]`
- **[UI]** **Libraries can be dragged into another order on a device.** On iOS and macOS 27 a library's chip in the library picker can be dragged to a new place (`reorderable()` on the chips and `reorderContainer(for:)` on their row, SwiftUI 27). The order is kept in this device's settings (`libraryDisplayOrder`), and the pickers, the menu commands for the next and previous library, and the Focus filter's choice of a first library follow it (`ContainerService.orderedContainers`, `pickableContainers`). The stored list keeps its own order: a second review on 2026-10-14 found that the workspace repair and sync put it back in creation order (`WorkspaceSyncService.mergeContainers`), and that its first library is the home of documents stored before libraries existed, so reordering the list itself would not have lasted and would have moved those documents' home. The order does not travel to other devices. A drop on one of the dragged chips changes nothing. Catalogue row "Reorder and swipe in any list", partly: conversations already swipe to delete, they have no rename or pin to put behind another swipe, and a swipe on a chat message would compete with selecting its text. `[evidence_level: test_verified for the order (LibraryOrderTests), build_verified; confidence: medium; not dragged on a device; evidence_source: ContainerService.swift orderedContainers, move, ordered, reordered; ContainerService+Focus.swift pickableContainers; ContainerPicker.swift LibraryReordering]`

A question is sent in the app's chat (`AppDestination.ask(_:sendsAtOnce: true)` and `ComposerDraft.sendsAtOnce`; while an answer is still being written it waits in the field), and the app comes forward, opening a window when none is open.
