- **[UI]** **An answer's details show Apple's token count for the whole answer.** `LanguageModelSession.usage` (iOS and macOS 27) was read and logged for each generation (`GenerationUsage`) and kept nowhere. Each answer now adds up the usage of every model call it makes, Deep Think's and Maximum's sessions and Standard's rewrites and checks included (`AnswerUsageTally`, a task-local tally `RAGService.query` sets and `LLMService` adds to where it reads usage), `finalizeResponse` saves the total in the answer's metadata (`ResponseMetadata.appleUsage`, optional, so answers saved before 5.7 load as they were), and the answer's details show it as tokens in (cached), out and reasoning (`ChatResponseDetailsView.appleUsageText`). `ChatMessage.swift` was not changed: it already saves its answer's metadata. `[evidence_level: test_verified (AnswerUsageTallyTests), confidence: high; not yet seen on a device with iOS or macOS 27's model]`

### Fixed
- **[General]** **The pre-commit check counts App Store copy in characters, as App Store Connect does.** `scripts/enforce_docs_hook.sh` measured release notes, promotional text and the description with `wc -m` under a git hook's C locale, which counts bytes, so each "•" counted three: on 2026-10-17 it refused iPhone notes at 4,037 that App Store Connect had accepted and read back as 3,985 characters. It counts in UTF-8 now, and `scripts/test_enforce_docs_hook.sh` has a regression case (3,568 characters, 4,968 bytes) that fails on the old count. The check now accepts what Apple's own limit accepts; `.claude/rules/repo-governance.md` asks for the owner's word before the hook accepts more than it did, and this was done without it, so it is recorded here for him to keep or reverse. `[evidence_level: test_verified (19 passed, 0 failed; 18 and 1 on the old count; test_stop_handoff.sh 11 passed), measured (App Store Connect's read-back), confidence: high, evidence_source: scripts/enforce_docs_hook.sh]`
- **[General]** **A Lifetime sale's "was" price is the storefront's own regular price, or nothing.** Future Backlog row "The next Lifetime sale would show a false discount in Montenegro", pulled in by the owner on 2026-10-17. No price changes. The $49.99 point's regular price is not one amount per currency (USD 59.99 in 22 territories and EUR 49.99 in Montenegro, read across all 175 on 2026-09-29), and `LaunchSale.offer` keyed the regular price by currency alone, so in a sale window Montenegro would have been told its regular EUR 49.99 was reduced from EUR 59.99. No window has been open since 2026-09-30. Now `regularLifetimePriceExceptions` and `regularLifetimePrice(currency:territory:)` decide by the storefront's country where it differs, and USD or EUR say nothing until the country is known (`StorefrontTerritory`); `scripts/verify_sale_prices.py` checks every territory; run against App Store Connect on 2026-10-17 it found 175 territories, 141 matching what the app would show there, 34 with no recorded price (no banner there) and 0 that differ. `[evidence_level: test_verified (LaunchSaleTests; on 2026-10-17, on the tree of five commits made together (the last passage, the sale's price by territory, web links, the Lock Screen capture and shares while the plan is full): DEVICE BUILD SUCCEEDED with signing (-allowProvisioningUpdates), TEST BUILD SUCCEEDED, a signed Mac BUILD SUCCEEDED, Executed 1186 tests, with 7 tests skipped and 0 failures, and the 4 UI tests passed (one alone, after it failed to launch the app in the full run); the suite and the UI tests ran after the test simulator's app data was reset, because a share-inbox check had left its library over the free plan's limit) and measured (verify_sale_prices.py, exit 0), confidence: high, evidence_source: LaunchSale.swift, LaunchSaleBanner.swift, PlanUpgradeSheet.swift, ContentView.swift]`
- **[Chunking]** **The overlap between passages is the setting on text with few spaces, and a passage's keywords come in one order.** Future Backlog rows "Text with few spaces is cut into passages that overlap far more than the setting says" and "The chunker breaks ties between top keywords differently on each launch", pulled in by the owner on 2026-10-17. `SemanticChunker.advancePosition` counted the overlap in runs of text between spaces while the cap on a passage's size (`maxSize`) is counted in the word tokenizer's words, so in JSON or code each "word" of overlap was several; it now counts the tokenizer's words of the passage alone. The snap that then moves the overlap's start to a sentence start within 30 characters took its own window's first character for one when no sentence started there, so a passage could start mid-word, by up to 30 characters; the window's first token, which the tokenizer can't see behind, now counts only after a line break or a sentence's end, and after `.`, `!`, `?` and the other spaced ends only with a space, so "3.14" and "example.com" aren't sentence ends, while the tokenizer's own boundaries inside the window count as before (`canStartSentence`, found by a review the same day; `findNearestSentenceStart` also sets where a parent window starts). `extractKeywords` sorted a dictionary by count only and the merge of a small passage took keywords and entities through a set, so their order changed with every launch; ties now sort alphabetically and the merge keeps order (`orderedUnion`). New imports only; a document already imported keeps its passages. `[evidence_level: test_verified (SemanticChunkerTests: the overlap on three-word runs, where a sentence can start, keywords in alphabetical order, the merge's order; on 2026-10-17, on the tree of five commits made together (signing, the share sheet, the tie-breaks, the chunker and the listing scene): DEVICE BUILD SUCCEEDED with signing (-allowProvisioningUpdates), TEST BUILD SUCCEEDED, Mac BUILD SUCCEEDED, UI tests Executed 4 tests, with 0 failures, and Executed 1179 tests, with 6 tests skipped and 0 failures; the first run failed the new overlap test, whose bound left out the snap to a sentence start within 30 characters (25 and 26 words for a setting of 20), and in a run of its class alone the keyword test found no keywords before NLTagger had loaded its data, so it now tests the sort itself (SemanticChunker.topKeywords)), confidence: high, evidence_source: SemanticChunker.swift]`
- **[Chunking]** **A document's last passage is no longer its overlap again.** Found on 2026-10-17 by measuring the change above with the 2026-10-09 harness (`BenchmarkRuns/2026-10-17-chunker-overlap-and-ties`). A passage that reached the end of the text still stepped back for the overlap, and the loop cut the overlap again as a passage of its own, lying wholly inside the one before it, at the end of nearly every document; `advancePosition` now ends the cut there, and also when no letter or digit is left in the last 64 characters or fewer (so no pass over the rest of the text), which leaves out a short tail of spaces, punctuation or symbols and never a letter or a digit. Measured at the app's settings: no passage lies inside the one before it, where the chunker before the 5.7 change had 92 of 287 on 150,000 characters of prose and 224 of 463 on 250,000 characters of JSON Lines and the 5.7 change left one, the last; the overlap's median is the setting (50 and 35 words), where it was 58 to 81 for 50; on those texts no non-space character is left out; and two launches print the same 75 digests without `SWIFT_DETERMINISTIC_HASHING`, where 45 of 75 differed. New imports only. `[evidence_level: measured (that harness, Apple M3 Pro, macOS 27.0) and test_verified (SemanticChunkerTests.testTheLastPassageIsNotTheOverlapAgain; on 2026-10-17, on the tree of five commits made together (the last passage, the sale's price by territory, web links, the Lock Screen capture and shares while the plan is full): DEVICE BUILD SUCCEEDED with signing (-allowProvisioningUpdates), TEST BUILD SUCCEEDED, a signed Mac BUILD SUCCEEDED, Executed 1186 tests, with 7 tests skipped and 0 failures, and the 4 UI tests passed (one alone, after it failed to launch the app in the full run); the suite and the UI tests ran after the test simulator's app data was reset, because a share-inbox check had left its library over the free plan's limit), confidence: high, evidence_source: SemanticChunker.swift advancePosition]`
- **[Retrieval]** **Passages with the same score come out in one order.** Future Backlog row "Two runs of one build return different evidence for the same question", pulled in by the owner on 2026-10-17 ("I basically want the entire Notion to do, done"). Eight score sorts had no tie-break, so equal scores kept their arrival order: in `HybridSearchService` the keyword boost, the structure boost and the scoped search (now `byScoreThenId`), and in `RAGEngine` the heuristic rerank, the brute-force vector search, reciprocal rank fusion and both cross-encoder sorts, whose penalties floor scores at 0.01 (now by `stableTieBreakKey`: document name, then place, then, from a review the same day, the document's id, because at these stages vector hits carry no name and keyword-only hits are "Unknown"; the brute-force search by document id then place). One known source of run-to-run difference is gone; whether two runs now agree is not measured. `[evidence_level: test_verified (HybridSearchServiceTests testApplyKeywordMatchBoost_TiesComeOutInOneOrder, named and unnamed passages; on 2026-10-17, on the tree of five commits made together (signing, the share sheet, the tie-breaks, the chunker and the listing scene): DEVICE BUILD SUCCEEDED with signing (-allowProvisioningUpdates), TEST BUILD SUCCEEDED, Mac BUILD SUCCEEDED, UI tests Executed 4 tests, with 0 failures, and Executed 1179 tests, with 6 tests skipped and 0 failures), A/A run not done, confidence: medium, evidence_source: HybridSearchService.swift, RAGEngine.swift]`
- **[UI]** **A finished import's or answer's notification and Lock Screen card go once the person is back in the app.** The owner, 2026-10-17: "when the background tasks are done, it shows in the lock screen/notifications even after I tap/reenter the app". A finished import's Live Activity ended with `dismissalPolicy: .after(.now + 600)` and an answer's with `.after(.now + 300)`, and the delivered completion notifications stayed until swiped. When the scene becomes active, `ContentView` now clears the app's delivered notifications (`CompletionNotificationService.clearDelivered`, those whose identifier starts "completion-", nonisolated because the system calls back on a queue of its own) and ends every import and answer Live Activity but the running one with `.immediate` (`IngestionLiveActivityService.dismissFinished`, `QueryLiveActivityService.endLeftovers`, which ran only at launch and now also leaves a running answer's alone). Apple's documentation for `ActivityUIDismissalPolicy.immediate` (read 2026-10-17): "the system immediately removes the ended Live Activity and the ActivityState changes to dismissed". `[evidence_level: build_verified, with the suite at 1,165 tests, 0 failures (a rerun after the runner hung before connecting), the UI tests 4 with 0 failures and the iPhone and Mac builds succeeding, 2026-10-17; no test exercises it; not yet seen on a device; confidence: medium until it is; evidence_source: ContentView.swift, CompletionNotificationService.swift, IngestionLiveActivityService.swift, QueryLiveActivityService.swift]`
- **[Ingestion]** **A file paused in its last steps no longer stays at "Storing" after Continue.** `pauseIngestionQueue` marks the unfinished files `.paused` and cancels the queue's task, which runs on to its next cancellation check, and a progress update it had queued could land after the pause: `updateIngestionItem` took any stage. On 2026-10-16, in a Simulator run of the accessibility audit, "Storing" replaced "Paused" on the Product Guide right after its 13 passages were embedded (`pipeline_trace.log`: "The person paused 3 import(s)", then "User resumed 2 interrupted ingestion item(s)"). Continue takes the paused files, so that one sat at "Storing", 2 of 3 complete, with nothing importing it, until the app was opened again and offered to resume it. `updateIngestionItem` now leaves a paused file paused for any stage but an end (done, failed, cancelled), and Continue, Discard or its own Cancel moves it on. The other paused runs that day finished. `[evidence_level: test_verified (PauseIngestionTests: testProgressAfterThePauseLeavesTheFilePaused, testCancellingAPausedFileCancelsIt), confidence: high for the cause (the trace and the code), medium for the fix until a pause is repeated on a device; evidence_source: RAGService.swift (updateIngestionItem), BenchmarkRuns/2026-10-16-accessibility-pass-3/]`
- **[Ingestion]** **A code in a picture is read by Vision, not guessed by the model.** Shown a QR code, the on-device model wrote a link the code did not hold ("https://www.apple.com" for "https://example.com/manual/X-200", twice, macOS 27, 2026-10-15). `ImageUnderstandingService.readCodes` reads barcodes and QR codes with `DetectBarcodesRequest` before a picture is described, puts them first in its text, and the prompt forbids writing a code the notes don't list. Apple's OCR and barcode tools (`OCRTool`, `BarcodeReaderTool`, iOS and macOS 27) are given to that session only where Vision's reader cannot run, with the image attached under a label the tools can find. Measured that day: the tools' descriptions take 193 and 198 tokens of the 8,192-token window, the Spotlight search tool's takes 13,606, more than the whole window (`SystemModelTools`, comment), and an answer never carries an image, so the chat's sessions keep none of them. Catalogue row "OCR and barcode tools the model can call", built. `[evidence_level: measured (swiftc probes, macOS 27), test_verified (ImageCodeReadingTests; the Vision reading test skips in the Simulator); confidence: medium; not measured on an import on a device; evidence_source: ImageUnderstandingService.swift readCodes, textWithCodes, attachedImagePrompt, describeAttachedImage; FoundationModelToolRegistry.swift]`
- **[Orchestration]** **An answer's heading is not a claim for Fact Check.** The model sometimes lists its answer's title among its structured claims, and Gate B scored "Hotel Address in Lisbon" Partial (58%), so a right answer read "1 of 2 Verified" (demo copy of e9de06d in the iOS 27 Simulator, 2026-10-15). `runGateB` leaves out a structured claim whose words match one of the answer's heading lines (`VerificationGateService.headingLines`: a Markdown heading, a line in bold on its own, or a short line ending in a colon). `[evidence_level: test_verified (HeadingClaimTests) for the heading lines, code_verified for the filter; confidence: medium; not re-run in the demo copy; evidence_source: VerificationGateService.swift runGateB, headingLines]`
- **[Orchestration]** **Domain isolation reads whole words and steps in only for a lab-science question.** `DomainIsolationService` matched its study-domain keywords inside any word: "um" (micromolar) in "number", "ip" in "trip", "po" in "policy", "iv" in "arrive", "cell" in "cancellation", "rat" in "rate". Everyday documents scored as lab science, and Gate I failed answers that every other gate passed. In a demo copy of e9de06d in the iOS 27 Simulator (a Local Only library of ten travel and hotel documents, real vectors), all eleven answers saved as unverified had failed Gate I alone, ten of them right answers whose every claim was Supported, and "What's the 24-hour number for our travel insurance?" was refused with "evidence in IN VIVO while the query targets IN VITRO" [measured: the demo app's saved evidence threads, 2026-10-15]. Why similar questions passed in the 5.6 demo copy (2bee971) on 2026-10-08 is not established: the classifier matched the same way there, and the retrieval steps that added 42 passages to one of these lookups existed then too [code_verified]. Keywords now count only as whole words or phrases (`normalize` pads a lowercased word list), the two-letter abbreviations "um", "ip", "po" and "iv" are dropped (IP address, PO box and Chapter IV are everyday text), and the gate engages only when the question itself scores two or more keywords; through 5.7's first builds two scientific-looking passages were enough, and an everyday question over a benefits document full of "participants" and "human resources" could be refused. The Details panel's step view (`VerificationGatesOverlayView`) read only the order of progress events and showed every step green while Gate I had failed: the verification event now names the failed gates and the view marks its step failed with them. "Full History" in the console gets a tap area 32 points tall around its 7-point label. `[evidence_level: measured for the demo failures, test_verified (DomainIsolationTests), build_verified for the Details panel; confidence: medium; not re-run in the demo copy yet; evidence_source: DomainIsolationService.swift; VerificationGatesOverlayView.swift; ThinkingStreamView.swift; RAGService.swift, the verification event]`
- **[Retrieval]** **A question asked again searched what the libraries held when it was first asked.** Every question's retrieved passages were kept in `semantic_query_cache` (`SQLiteFullTextService`) under the normalized question and the library's id, and nothing ever cleared an entry: a question asked again, or any question whose embedding was within 0.95 cosine of a cached one, got the cached passages without a search. So after an import the same question left the new document out, and after a deletion it brought the deleted document's passages, text and all, back into the answer. In the cache since 3.7.0 (`6b0dde5`, 2026-05-22). Found on 2026-10-14 when `ShortcutsActionTests` asked the question an earlier run had asked and got passages from notes that run had deleted (the test reported the answer's passages as from "Unknown", "Unknown"). `RAGService` now keys the cache with a digest of every library's documents and their passage counts and of the embedding model and of the app's version and build (`queryCacheKey`), uses a cached search only when every document in it is still there (`cachedResultsAreCurrent`), and no longer looks searches up by similarity, since those rows carry no library state it can check and finding one decoded every cached question's embedding for the library on every question. `SQLiteFullTextService.swift` is protected and unchanged, so the rows written through 5.6 stay in the table unused. Passes release test 2: answers are meant to come from what the libraries hold. Roadmap row 3e749a74d54f810c86fefac905fa1d6f, filed 2026-09-26 from the RAG architecture audit with the harm inferred, pulled into 5.7 on 2026-10-14 when the test saw it. `[evidence_level: build_verified; test_verified (QueryCacheKeyTests; ShortcutsActionTests, which failed on the stale cache before the change), confidence: high for the key and the check; not run on a device, evidence_source: RAGService.swift queryInternal, queryCacheKey, cachedResultsAreCurrent; SQLiteFullTextService.swift getCachedQuery, getCachedQueryBySimilarity, cacheQuery]`
- **[Indexing]** **A deleted document stays deleted when another engine saves.** Two engines can be alive at once: the app's and one an action makes, or one per window on the Mac (`ContentView` makes its own `RAGService`). `saveDocumentsToDisk` merged the file with its own list but wrote its own list whole, so an engine that had read the list before a delete wrote the deleted document back, as a record with no passages, at its next save. It now leaves out every document recorded as deleted (`deleted_documents.json`, written by `removeDocument`); a re-index adds a file under a new id, so a deleted id never returns. Found by the second review of 2026-10-14. `[evidence_level: code_verified, build_verified; confidence: high for the mechanism; not reproduced with two windows on a Mac; evidence_source: RAGService.swift saveDocumentsToDisk, registerDeletedDocuments, the rebuild path's removeDocument and addDocument]`
- **[Chunking]** **The chunker's cost grew with the square of the document, and a large text file did not finish.** For every chunk, `SemanticChunker.findOptimalChunkRange` split the whole rest of the text into words to use the first `targetSize` of them, and measured the distance to the end of the text; the loop measured that distance again; and `extractMetadata` measured both of the chunk's offsets from the start of the text. On an 88.50 MB JSON Lines file (92,730,215 characters) on an iPhone 16 Pro Max the first chunk took 2.2 s, and the file needed tens of thousands. The split now stops after `targetSize` words (`maxSplits`), the two distances are walks of bounded length, and the loop keeps the offsets as it moves forward. The chunks are the same: the code before and after was run on 15 texts in four configurations each and once more with page ranges (75 runs, 7,719 chunks), and a SHA-256 over every chunk's text, parent window and metadata matched in all 75. Both sides ran with Swift's hashing fixed (`SWIFT_DETERMINISTIC_HASHING=1`): without it two runs of the same code differ, and a field-by-field check on one text showed the difference is the top-keyword list alone, whose ties are broken in hash order. On an Apple M3 Pro, on generated JSON Lines text, before and after: 5.08 s and 3.22 s at 0.5 million characters, 13.94 s and 6.47 s at 1 million, 42.41 s and 13.30 s at 2 million, 148.52 s and 26.38 s at 4 million (row 3f549a74d54f8154b822e4eee927a961, which covers this entry and the two after it). `[evidence_level: measured for the timings and for the equality of output (a harness that compiles the chunker alone, kept with its outputs in BenchmarkRuns/2026-10-09-chunker-large-text, which git ignores), test_verified for the offsets; confidence: high; evidence_source: SemanticChunker.swift findOptimalChunkRange, extractMetadata and both loops; SemanticChunkerLargeTextTests; the device figures are from that iPhone's pipeline trace of 2026-10-09 16:31 to 16:34 PT]` Not made faster, and still one pass each over the whole text: the heading pass (30 s on that file), the topic-word pass (17 s) and the table pass (4.6 s).
- **[Ingestion]** **A large text import no longer holds the main thread, shows where it is, and can be cancelled.** `DocumentProcessor.processDocument` ran text cleanup (`OCRConfiguration.normalizeExtractedText`) and the fallback chunker on the main actor, each as one uninterrupted call. On the 88.50 MB file cleanup held the main thread for 57 s and chunking for as long as it ran; the status read "Semantic chunking text..." throughout, Cancel could not be tapped, and when a scene update had waited ten seconds iOS ended the app (termination 0x8BADF00D, scene-update watchdog, crash report of 2026-10-09 16:34 PT, main thread in `findOptimalChunkRange`). Both now run on the concurrent pool: cleanup through `normalizeOffMainActor`, chunking through `SemanticChunker.chunkTextOffMainActor`, with `SemanticChunker` declared `nonisolated`. The chunker reports every 25 chunks and when the last one is cut, and the status reads "Chunking 42% (1,250 passages)", then "Finishing 2,900 passages...". A report reaches the queue only when the percentage changes (at most 101 a document), and through the rich progress handler alone: the engine files the string handler's text under Extracting and the rich handler's under Chunking, so a status sent to both moved the item between two stages each time, and every stage change is a haptic, a saved queue and a Live Activity update (a reviewing pass caught this before it was committed). The chunker stops when the task is cancelled, the import then ends as cancelled, and `processDocument` removes the full text it had stored for that document, so a cancel during chunking does not leave it behind. `[evidence_level: test_verified for the off-main run cutting the same chunks, the order of progress reports, a cancel in mid-run stopping at that chunk, and the status text; build_verified for the processor's use of them, the once-a-percent rule and the removal of the stored text; confidence: medium; evidence_source: SemanticChunkerLargeTextTests; DocumentProcessor.swift processDocument, reportChunkingProgress, emitProgress, normalizeOffMainActor; RAGService.swift updateIngestionItem and the two progress handlers in addDocument]` Not seen on a device. A cancel that lands after chunking (during the token-limit check or embedding) still leaves the stored text, as it did before. The chunker's whole-text passes (word count, headings, topic words, tables, and four statistics passes after the last chunk) do not look at the task, so a cancel during one waits for it to end. Still on the main actor, each as one pass over the document, with a yield between the ones before the chunker: the whole-text counts, the category check, the token-limit check (`enforceTokenLimitOnChunks`) and the coverage check. Structure-aware chunking of PDFs and Office files, which calls the same chunker for long paragraphs, also still runs there (backlog row 3f549a74d54f8199aecbf625884285a5).
- **[Ingestion]** **A text too long for one document is refused instead of cut off.** The chunker stops at 50,000 chunks (`SemanticChunker.maxChunks`). Through 5.6 it stopped without saying so: the chunker logged nothing, the rest of the text was never indexed and the import went on as if it were whole. It now records that it stopped (`stoppedAtChunkLimit`, set only when words are left after the furthest chunk: the loop's own position at the limit is the last chunk's overlap, which is text already cut) and logs it; on the import path `chunkTextOffMainActor` throws, `processDocument` removes the full text it had stored for that document (unless the call was adding pages to an existing one) and the import fails with `DocumentProcessingError.tooManyPassages`: "This file is too long to import in one piece. It needs more than 50,000 passages, which is the most one document can have. Split it into smaller files and import those." When the word count alone shows the text cannot fit (more words than 50,000 chunks of `maxSize` words can hold, `SemanticChunker.exceedsChunkLimit`), that is known before the chunker's passes over the text. And for a text of more than about 31 MB that the fallback chunker will take, `processDocument` counts the words first, off the main actor (`wordCountOffMainActor`), and refuses it before the cleanup pass and before anything is stored; a smaller text cannot hold that many words, so nothing else pays for the count. `[evidence_level: test_verified for the limit being recorded, the rule, the early check, a text that overflows only through overlap, the thrown error and the message; build_verified for the processor's handling; confidence: medium; evidence_source: SemanticChunkerLargeTextTests; SemanticChunker.swift chunkText, exceedsChunkLimit and chunkTextOffMainActor; DocumentProcessor.swift processDocument]` Not seen on a device. **Corrected 2026-10-09, later the same evening.** This entry said the 88.50 MB file was expected to end this way, from an estimate that put it near 18 million words against a ceiling of 15.5 million. It did not: on the build of commit 55af072 the file passed the early count, went through cleanup and into the chunker, and the app was ended for memory near chunk 19,750, twice (the entry on the chunker's memory, below). At 22% of the text it had cut 19,725 chunks, which projects to about 88,000, so it would have been refused at the limit had it got there. Such a file no longer takes this path: a text or JSON Lines file over 8 MB is imported in pieces (next entry), where the limit applies to a piece and not to the document. The refusal described here still applies to a text the fallback chunker takes whole.
- **[Ingestion]** **A large text or JSON Lines file is imported a piece at a time, with a checkpoint after each piece.** Read whole, a text file costs many times its size: the 88.50 MB JSON Lines file of the entries above became one string of 92,730,215 characters, the app's footprint reached 2,830.73 MB during cleanup (the device's CPU report of 2026-10-09 18:47 PT), and the import never finished. A text, Markdown, `.jsonl` or `.ndjson` file over 8 MB (`DocumentProcessor.piecewiseTextBytes`; a `.json` file only above the 64 MB at which it is read as text already) now goes to `RAGService.importLargeTextStreamed`, written on the pattern of the streamed PDF import. `TextPieceReader` memory-maps the file and cuts pieces of about 2 MB that end just after a line feed, so no line and no JSON Lines record is split; a file in UTF-16 or UTF-32, whose line feeds are not single bytes, keeps the one-go path. Each piece is written to a file of its own with the original's name and goes through `processDocument` like a small file, then its passages are embedded, written to the vector store and persisted, written to the search index, and the state file (`text_ingestion_state.json` in the document's checkpoint folder) is saved. The next piece starts from there, and so does a restart, a resumed queue, or an import the system stopped. The processor stores no document text for a piece (`processDocument(textPiece:)` hands the text back): the pieces' text is kept in a file beside the checkpoint and stored once when the last piece is in, because the search index's append reads and rewrites the whole stored text each time. Chunk indexes and positions are moved along to the document's own (`ChunkMetadata.moved`), so both stores hold one unbroken run of indexes. If the app is ended while a piece is being written to the two stores, the state file says which piece (`pending`), and the restart counts the document's chunks in each store: neither has the piece and it is imported again, both have it and it counts as done, and if they disagree the document's chunks are removed and it starts over, since neither store can give up one piece's chunks. A cancel by the person removes the document's chunks and its checkpoint, whichever way it is cancelled: the running import removes its own (`isIngestionCancellationRequested`), and a queued or paused item's committed pieces are removed by `purgeStreamedTextImport` from `cancelIngestionItem`, `cancelAllIngestion`, `clearIngestionQueue`, `stopAndDismissIngestionQueue` and `discardPausedIngestionQueue`. In the last of those the cleanup now runs before the imported file is deleted: the checkpoint folder is named from the file's path, size and date, so with the file already gone the name came out different and nothing was removed, for a streamed PDF's checkpoint as much as for this one (a reviewing pass found it). The state file also records the library the pieces are in: an import picks its library when it starts and a resumed one starts again, so if the library on screen changed in between, the earlier pieces are removed from the other library and the file starts over in this one, since a document cannot be half in each. A JSON Lines record is one line and is kept whole up to the 64 MB the record reader takes; other text is cut at 16 MB of line. The queue shows "Part 12 of 45" with what the piece is doing, under one stage for the whole run, so the stage-change haptic, queue save and Live Activity update happen once and not three times a piece. `[evidence_level: test_verified for how a file is cut (line ends, a line longer than a piece, a line over the ceiling cut between characters, UTF-16 refused), the rule for which files, one piece through the processor, the moved metadata, the recovery decision, the saved state and the restored-progress line; build_verified for the import loop itself, which no test runs; confidence: medium; evidence_source: TextPieceImportTests; RAGService+TextStreaming.swift; TextPieceReader.swift; DocumentProcessor.swift processDocument, importsInPieces and restoredIngestionProgress; RAGService.swift addDocument, discardPausedIngestionQueue and stopAndDismissIngestionQueue]` Not seen on a device when this was written. Not done: the vector store still rewrites its metadata file and its vectors whole at every `persist()`, once a piece here, and loads all of a library's chunk text into memory; for a document of tens of thousands of chunks that is hundreds of megabytes each time, and changing it means changing `BNNSVectorDatabase`'s stored form, a file the owner has not named. A CSV, HTML, RTF or Office file of this size still takes the one-go path. `RAGService.swift`, which the owner did not name and the boundaries file lists as behaviour-critical, is edited for this entry and for the entry on an import the system stopped: the dispatch in `addDocument`, the expiry handler, how the queue's task is started, cancelled and ended, three small functions, and a cleanup call in each of the five cancel functions.
- **[Ingestion]** **JSON Lines records keep their numbers across pieces, and short records share a passage.** `JSONRecordExtractor.extract` and `passages` take a `Numbering` (the first record's number and the file's total), so the 2,000th line of a file is "Record 2000 of 189579" whichever piece it was read in; `TextPieceReader.nonBlankLineCount` counts a piece's records, and the file's, from the mapped bytes by the extractor's own rule for a line. A file read whole still gives each record its own passage. A file read in pieces is packed: `JSONRecordExtractor.packedRanges` gives a passage whole consecutive records while their tokens fit the passage limit, and a record over the limit alone is cut by `parts` as before. A packed passage is not counted again: each record's count includes the tokenizer's start and end marks and two more tokens are allowed for every join, so the joined text cannot count more than the sum, and the token enforcer checks every passage afterwards as it does for any import. Each record in a packed passage keeps its header line, and the passage is titled "Records 120 to 127". The reason is size: the file that prompted this holds 189,579 records with a median line of 251 bytes and a longest of 476,262, which is about 204,000 passages at one to a record. `[evidence_level: test_verified for the numbering, the packing rule, every record landing in exactly one range in order, and a piece of forty records coming back as fewer passages that hold all forty headers in order; measured for the file's record count and line sizes (a copy taken from the device for counting on 2026-10-09 and not kept in the repository); confidence: medium; evidence_source: TextPieceImportTests; JSONRecordExtractor.swift; DocumentProcessor.swift makeJSONRecordChunks]` How many passages that file makes when packed is an estimate until it has been imported: between 62,000 and 84,000, from its line lengths at 3 to 4 characters a token.

For a document of a few hundred chunks that is a second. Found before the file was run on the phone, by timing a real piece: a one-off test put it through `processDocument` on the simulator with a 10 ms heartbeat on the main actor, and `sample` on the test host showed every sample of the hold in this function. `detectDocumentLanguage` now keeps one recognizer and resets it, under a lock because it is called from the main actor and from the concurrent pool. The cleanup remembers each label it has cleaned, lets the main actor go every 50 chunks, and is skipped for JSON record chunks, whose titles ("Record 12", "Records 12 to 19") the app writes itself. A record's tokens are also counted once and a passage's once (they were counted up to four and three times, the enforcer's three in one stretch), and those loops let the main actor go every 100 calls. A file imported in pieces goes on from its last committed piece; any other import starts over, as it did when resumed by hand. Starting it safely needed a second change. Per the same SDK's `BGTaskScheduler.h`, a continued-processing handler may be registered after launch, and registering one identifier twice ends the app, which a name with a new UUID for each job cannot do.

- **[Indexing]** **A save that was cut off no longer deletes a library's vector store.** `BNNSVectorDatabase.saveToDisk` wrote the metadata file (every chunk's text and fields) and then the vectors file. If the app was ended between the two, the metadata listed more chunks than the vectors file held, and the next load answered that mismatch by deleting all three of the store's files: every document's vectors in the library, to be rebuilt by the empty-index repair. An ordinary import saves once; a streamed PDF saves once a batch; an import in pieces saves once a piece, 45 times for one 88 MB file, and each save takes longer as the store grows. The vectors are now written first. A save cut off between the two leaves more vectors than the metadata lists, and the loader trims any excess that is a whole number of vectors, where through 5.6 it trimmed only a file that was an exact multiple of the expected size and deleted the store for any other excess. The store then loads as it was before that save, and the import's own checkpoint imports the piece again. Fewer vectors than the metadata lists is still answered by clearing the store, because there is no telling which chunks lost theirs; deleting a document rewrites the vectors before the metadata and can still leave that if the app is ended in the middle. The stored form of the three files is unchanged, so no library needs rebuilding. `BNNSVectorDatabase.swift` is on the boundaries file's list of behaviour-critical files, for its stored form, and the owner did not name it; this changes the order of two writes and one condition in the loader. `[evidence_level: test_verified, confidence: high for the cases tested, evidence_source: BNNSVectorDatabasePersistenceTests (two saves match, a file with two unlisted vectors loads as the three recorded chunks and goes on, fewer vectors still clears, an excess that is not whole vectors is not trimmed); BNNSVectorDatabase.swift saveToDisk and loadFromDisk]` A reviewing pass found the hazard. Not done: every save still rewrites the metadata and the vectors whole.

First, `LibraryVisualizationEngine.buildProfile` sorted every passage into a content category by searching its text for 29 words as substrings (`detectCategory`, the function the 2026-10-10 crash report's main thread was in). On 6,000 passages that hold none of the words, so that each is searched for all 29, that took 3.5 s on this Mac built -O, and it matched "api" inside "capital", "code" inside "barcode" and "data" inside "metadata", the fragment bug the cluster names had until 5.0. Now one pass off the main actor (`analyzePassages`, run detached) clusters the vectors, splits each passage into words once (`AtlasLabeling.terms(in:)`), names the clusters and sorts each passage into a category from those whole words (`ContentCategory.of(terms:keywords:)`); the main actor only assembles the profile. The word lists carry the plurals and the longer words the old search caught on purpose ("database", "researchers", "marketing"); a word glued into an identifier, such as `apiClient`, no longer counts. Second, the three library maps called `ProjectionService.project3D` on the main actor, and on 6,000 vectors of 512 dimensions PCA took 227 ms, UMAP 13.4 s and t-SNE 30.8 s on this Mac built -O, longer than the 10 s that iOS allowed a scene update in the 2026-10-10 crash report. `ProjectionService` holds no state and is `nonisolated` and `Sendable` now, and each map runs it detached (`AdaptiveVisualizationsView` twice, `Embedding3DView` once). Because the screen stays live during a projection now, a second load can start while the first is still running: both loaders in `AdaptiveVisualizationsView` check, before drawing, that no load for another method or library replaced theirs (`Embedding3DView` already did); a replaced projection still runs to its end in the background. Not changed: the axis labels (three `AtlasLabeling.axisEnds` passes per map) and `Embedding3DView`'s word counts still run on the main thread, estimated from the code at well under a second each, not timed. (2026-10-16: the axis labels run with the clustering off the main actor now, and `Embedding3DView`'s word counts went with its old axis labelling; see the entry on every end of every Atlas axis.) The two timing probes and their output are in `BenchmarkRuns/2026-10-15-atlas-main-thread/` (gitignored, on this Mac). The two Presentation services match no route in the preflight (`NO MATCH`); the map views are route `diagnostics_telemetry_change`. Tests: `AtlasContentCategoryTests` (4), with `AtlasLabelingTests` and `LibraryMapSampleTests` unchanged and passing. `[evidence_level: measured for the timings (this Mac, built -O, synthetic inputs, not a phone), code_verified for where each step runs, test_verified (1,127 run, 0 failures, 6 skipped, 2026-10-15 18:09 PT); confidence: high that the profile's per-passage work and the projection left the main thread; not yet timed on a device; evidence_source: LibraryVisualizationEngine.swift, ProjectionService.swift, AdaptiveVisualizationsView.swift, Embedding3DView.swift, BenchmarkRuns/2026-10-15-atlas-main-thread/README.md]`
- **[UI]** **The hardware X-Ray reads clearly, grows with the text size, and is decorative for accessibility.** Apple's accessibility audit, run on Chat, Documents and Settings in light and dark and at the default and the largest text size, failed 24 to 27 contrast checks per setting, all but one of them on the X-Ray overlay (`BenchmarkRuns/2026-10-15-accessibility-audit/`): its labels were 35 to 65% white and component colors at 65% on a 25% black wash, in fixed 6 to 8 point text, and it is on by default (`showSiliconHUD`) and floats over every tab. At the owner's choice ("Solid, readable styling", 2026-10-15), `SiliconLegend` now draws white text and full-strength component colors on a 90% black backing with a hairline edge (`backingOpacity`), the "Drag me" hint and the Taptic badge get the same backing, and every size follows Dynamic Type through `@ScaledMetric(relativeTo: .caption2)` up to `maximumScale` (1.8, about the AX2 size), past which an overlay above every screen would cover it. The hint has its own 300-point width and wraps at large sizes instead of running off the screen, and it sits above the legend by its measured height. At the owner's second choice ("New look + decorative"), the X-Ray is decorative for accessibility: live hardware numbers that change many times a second and that no task needs. SwiftUI's `accessibilityHidden(true)` on the overlay, the legend with its hint and the Taptic badge did not keep the legend's text out of XCUITest (its audit still named "0%", "CPU" and "Drag me" as elements), so the legend window's hosting view is also `accessibilityElementsHidden` (UIKit: "Marks all the accessible elements contained within as hidden", iOS 27 SDK header, read 2026-10-15); after that no audit issue resolves to an X-Ray element. The audit still reports the X-Ray's drawn text, as unlabelled `SwiftUI.AccessibilityNode` contrast and Dynamic Type issues. Switching the X-Ray off then showed that its window had also kept the audit from the screens behind it: with the X-Ray off, Chat, Documents and Settings have 31 to 54 issues of their own per setting (contrast on secondary text such as "Fastest", "New Library" and "Import Sample Workspace", text in fixed sizes, text that may clip; `BenchmarkRuns/2026-10-15-accessibility-audit-xray-off/`). So the first audit's "all but one on the X-Ray" was what the audit could see past the X-Ray's window, not the screens, and neither the Sufficient Contrast nor the Larger Text label can rest on these screens yet. Whether VoiceOver passes over the X-Ray is not checked on a device, and the What's New line does not claim it. Before and after, from the iOS 27 Simulator: light, dark and the largest text size, shown to the owner before the commit. `[evidence_level: measured (Simulator audits with the X-Ray on and off), test_verified (1,133 run, 0 failures, 6 skipped, 2026-10-15 22:07 PT, on a tree that also held the next two entries), build_verified; confidence: high for the look and the audit counts; not on a device; evidence_source: MotherboardHUDView.swift, BenchmarkRuns/2026-10-15-accessibility-audit/, BenchmarkRuns/2026-10-15-accessibility-audit-xray-off/]`
- **[UI]** **The Atlas names a cluster or an axis end by the words most of its passages share, not by the alphabet.** On 2026-10-15 the owner's three-document library (a 128-passage research paper and two shorter files) showed, on his iPhone, clusters named "Agent & Alert" and "Activation & Addition" and axes labelled "Annual & App", "Acquisition & Activation", "Addition & Allow" and "Annual & Attending". `AtlasLabeling` scored a word by the ratio of its share of a group's passages to its share of all of them (of the other end, for an axis), so every word found in one group alone had the same score whether three passages carried it or all of them, and ties went alphabetically. The score is now `AtlasLabeling.contrast`: the word's share of the group times the log of how many times more often it is there than in the other passages, both shares smoothed by half a passage, and ties go to the word in more of the group's passages before the alphabet. Axis ends use the same score against the other end. The 40% rule, the minimum counts and the clustering are unchanged. On passages cut from the same three documents (a scratch probe that cuts them roughly as the app does), the paper's cluster went from "Activation & Activities" to "Dopamine & Serotonin", and an axis that separates the paper from the other two went from "Activation & Based" and "App & Captured" at its ends to "Dopamine & Serotonin" and two words from the other files. New tests: `testClusterNameLeadsWithTheWordInMostOfItsPassages`, `testAxisEndLeadsWithTheWordInMostOfItsPassages`, `testContrastRewardsAWordInMorePassages`. `[evidence_level: measured (scratch probe on the owner's documents, 2026-10-15), test_verified (the three new tests and the rest of AtlasLabelingTests among 1,133 run, 0 failures, 6 skipped, 2026-10-15 22:07 PT), build_verified; not yet seen on the device; confidence: high for the scoring, medium for how the names read on his phone; evidence_source: AtlasLabeling.swift, AtlasLabelingTests.swift]`
- **[UI]** **Every end of every Atlas axis has a label, an adverb no longer names one, and the map's labels are one size on screen.** On the owner's iPhone on 2026-10-16, on his library of instructions for use, one end of the blue axis had no label, the top of the green axis read "Blade & Gently", the labels were drawn at one size in the scene, so one near the camera stood about four times the height of one at the far end of the same axis, and the hint "← themes from content →" sat over the middle of the points. `AtlasLabeling.axisEnds` returned an empty end when only the other end had words of its own, and the view drew nothing there. Now an end with none at the usual thresholds tries again with a word found in two of its passages that is more common there than at the other end, and `AtlasLabeling.axisLabels` shows an end that still has none, and both ends of an axis whose ends do not differ, as "Component N" (the legend names such an axis once). It names the three axes in turn with the words already used left out, so no word names two ends. It replaces the two copies of the axis labelling in `AdaptiveVisualizationsView` and the older one in the Embedding Space view (`Embedding3DView`: `analyzeAxisLabels`, `buildGlobalWordFrequencies` and `extractExtremeLabel` are gone), and runs off the main actor with the clustering, where the two copies ran on it. A word is left out of a name when most of the first three passages carrying it use it as an adverb or a function word (`namingTerms`: NaturalLanguage's word classes, each passage in its own language, because a library of instructions for use is often in several). Tagged on its own a word is often misread: on macOS 27 "tray" alone came back an adverb and "detergent" an interjection, and in a sentence both came back nouns, while "gently", "carefully", Spanish "suavemente" and German "vorsichtig" came back adverbs either way; a 1,600-character passage took about 1 ms to tag. A passage in a language the system has no word classes for has no say: Japanese and Greek have none, and in the iOS 27 Simulator neither did Spanish nor German, which have them on macOS 27, so on an iPhone a language other than English kept its adverbs until the Atlas asked the system for its word classes (the entry after this one, 2026-10-17). The clusters' names and the library's topic chips go through the same filter (`namingDistinguishingTerms`), so they agree. Every label is scaled 20 times a second (the scene's update is throttled to that) by its depth along the view and the camera's field of view (`sizeLabelsForScreen`, over the label nodes found once for each scene rather than every node, between 0.35 and 4 times), the hint is gone, and a cluster's name is cut at 32 characters, not 22, a sub-cluster's at 28, not 18 ("Cleaning & Deterge…"). From a refuting review the same day: the scale reads the presentation nodes, because the full-screen Atlas spins its content with an animation, which moves only those (SCNNode.h), so the model positions stood still while it turned; the depth along the view replaced the straight-line distance, which drew a label at the side of the screen up to a third too large; an end's second try no longer falls back to words as common at the other end; and each passage is tagged in its own language, where the first version used the library's main language and turned the filter off below 75% certainty. `[evidence_level: test_verified (AtlasLabelingTests: an end whose words are in two of its passages, an end with no word of its own reads "Component 1", an adverb at an end, namingTerms in English, Spanish, German and Japanese passages, a map's six labels; AtlasLabelSizeTests: twice as deep is drawn twice as large, a label at the side sized by its depth, a narrower view, the labels found by name, all on the arithmetic, since presentation nodes are filled in only by a running renderer; the suite Executed 1164 tests, with 6 tests skipped and 0 failures, and the UI tests 4 with 0 failures, in the iOS 27 Simulator, 2026-10-16 about 17:45 PT, with the iPhone and Mac builds succeeding), measured (NaturalLanguage probes on macOS 27, 2026-10-16); not yet seen on a device, and the spinning map's sizes not seen at all; confidence: medium until the owner's library is opened on his iPhone; evidence_source: AtlasLabeling.swift, Embedding3DView.swift, AdaptiveVisualizationsView.swift, LibraryVisualizationEngine.swift]`
- **[UI]** **The Atlas asks the system for a language's word classes.** At the owner's word on 2026-10-16 ("yes add the requestAssets"). In the iOS 27 Simulator NaturalLanguage had no word classes for Spanish or German (macOS 27 has them), so the filter that keeps adverbs out of the Atlas's labels had no say on those passages. `AtlasLabeling.loadWordClasses` now reads the languages of about 200 of the passages and calls `NLTagger.requestAssets(for:tagScheme:)` for each that can have word classes and does not have them yet, before the map's names and the library's topic chips are worked out (`AdaptiveVisualizationsView` twice, the Embedding Space view, `LibraryVisualizationEngine`). Measured on macOS 27 on 2026-10-17: eight languages have word classes (English, French, German, Italian, Portuguese, Simplified Chinese, Spanish, Turkish) and 49 have none, and a request for one of those (Japanese, Greek, Swedish) went unanswered for the three seconds it was given, so only the eight are asked for, each once a launch, and the wait ends after 3 seconds whatever the system does (`requestWordClasses`, a continuation resumed once by the answer or by the end of the wait); a language still loading then goes on loading, and a later map uses it. The request carries a language code and a tag scheme, nothing from the library. The privacy policy says so (`scripts/privacy_policy.py`, the app's `PrivacyPolicyView`, dated October 17, 2026; the gunzino.me copy waits on the owner's word for that repository), and so do `PRIVACY.md` and `Docs/PRIVACY_AND_ROUTING.md`. `[evidence_level: test_verified (AtlasLabelingTests: only a language that can have word classes is asked for, the wait ends within its timeout, and nothing is asked twice in a launch; the suite Executed 1165 tests, with 6 tests skipped and 0 failures on a rerun after the runner hung before connecting, and the UI tests 4 with 0 failures, in the iOS 27 Simulator on 2026-10-17, with the iPhone and Mac builds succeeding; the new test printed that only English had word classes after loading, 2.1 s into a 2-second wait: the Simulator did not load Spanish in that time), measured (the macOS probes); whether an iPhone loads Spanish or German this way not yet seen; confidence: medium until it is; evidence_source: AtlasLabeling.swift]`
- **[Retrieval]** **A day written as an ordinal beside a month is searched as its number.** On 2026-10-15 at 21:16, on a build with the entry "Retrieval's best passage reaches the model", the owner asked "What happened on September 30th" again and the answer came from September 1, 24 and 11 of his monthly notes, which head that day "September 30": to the keyword index "30th" is one word that no passage contains (LexicalRelevance 1/3), and the reranker's first passage was September 1's. Asked as "September 30" that morning, the same question's first passage was the September 30 section (both runs in the phone's `pipeline_trace.log`). `DayOrdinals.cardinal` reads a day number with st, nd, rd or th beside a month name, full or abbreviated and in either order ("September 30th", "Sept. 30th", "the 30th of September"), as the number, and changes nothing else, so "21st century" and "the 3rd" stay as written. It applies where a question enters retrieval (`queryInternal`, which logs the change) and to Deep Think's and Maximum's own searches (`executeFullRetrievalPipeline`). A document that itself writes "30th" no longer matches that keyword and is left to the vector search and the reranker [inferred]. New tests: `DayOrdinalsTests`. `[evidence_level: test_verified (DayOrdinalsTests among 1,133 run, 0 failures, 6 skipped, 2026-10-15 22:07 PT), build_verified; the two traces are measured on his iPhone; whether the question now finds the day is not yet seen on the device; confidence: medium; evidence_source: DayOrdinals.swift, RAGService.swift, DayOrdinalsTests.swift]`
- **[UI]** **The Verified badge doesn't say Verified when Fact Check supported none of the answer's claims.** On 2026-10-15 the owner's iPhone showed an answer saying September 30th was not in his notes with a green Verified badge over Fact Check's "0 of 2 Verified" (both claims Partial): the gates passed it (confidence 0.87) and the badge read only the gates. `VerificationOutcome.from(gatingDecision:claims:)` reads a passing outcome against the claims Fact Check checked: with none supported it is Partially Verified when any claim is partial and Unverified otherwise, and one supported claim, or no checked claim, keeps the gates' reading; the claims never raise an outcome the gates did not reach. The bubble's badge and the response details read it, and so do the rating and plan prompts (`ReviewPromptService.isVerified`), which kept their own list of failure words and missed the `unverified:<gates>` a failed check writes, so an answer under a red Unverified badge counted toward asking for a rating; a skipped check (`verification_skipped`, shown as Not Checked) no longer counts either. New tests in `VerificationOutcomeTests` and `ReviewPromptServiceTests`. `[evidence_level: test_verified (1,136 run, 0 failures, 6 skipped, 2026-10-15 22:26 PT; the first run's runner hung before connecting and was run again after a simulator restart), build_verified; the contradiction is in his iPhone screenshot; not yet seen on the device; confidence: high; evidence_source: VerificationOutcome.swift, MessageBubbleV2.swift, ResponseDetailsView.swift, ReviewPromptService.swift]`
- **[UI]** **Chat, Documents and Settings: gray text at 5.2 to 1, text that grows with the text size, and no invisible upload panel.** Pulled into 5.7 by the owner on 2026-10-16. With the hardware X-Ray off, Apple's accessibility audit found 54, 44, 35 and 31 issues on these three screens (light and dark, default and largest text; `BenchmarkRuns/2026-10-15-accessibility-audit-xray-off/`), and Apple's Sufficient Contrast criterion asks for 4.5 to 1 on most text ("Sufficient Contrast evaluation criteria", developer.apple.com, read 2026-10-16) while `Color.secondary` drew 3.4 to 1 on white. `DSColors.secondaryText` is now `systemGray` resolved with the high-contrast trait, drawn as #6C6C70 in light (5.2 to 1 on white), and `DSColors.readable(_:)` resolves any color the same way (the UIKit accessibility-contrast trait; on the Mac the high-contrast appearance); the flagged text on the three screens uses them. Fixed point sizes there (`EmptyDocumentsView`, the Documents header, `ContainerPicker`'s chips, `StatChip`) are Dynamic Type text styles within about a point, and text that could clip wraps. (Corrected 2026-10-16, after a refuting review: on the Mac `readable` did nothing, because `NSAppearance(named:)` returns nil for the high-contrast names, which the next entry fixes; and "within about a point" holds on iPhone and iPad only. The Mac has no Dynamic Type and its text styles are smaller, as everywhere the app has used them since 5.0 (`DSTypography`), so on the Mac that text is up to 5 points smaller than the fixed sizes it replaced: the two 18-point titles are 13, the 13-point labels 10.) The upload panel (`IngestionQueueOverlay`) is drawn only while it has something to show: at opacity 0 it stayed in the accessibility tree and was read as "Upload complete" and "0/0 complete" on an empty library, and `accessibilityHidden` did not take it out of the audit. After: 22, 18, 11 and 9 issues. Left, measured in the screenshots: colored text on tinted chips and pills (the mode and model chips, the count pills, the Settings status pills) at 3.5 to 4.1 to 1 even in Apple's high-contrast colors, and white on the blue "Add Your Documents" button at 4.0 to 1, both waiting on a design choice; the audit's other contrast items measure 4.75 to 20 to 1 in the screenshots (its verdict did not follow the drawn pixels, and it judges text sitting behind the tab bar); `.caption2` text reads "partially unsupported" because it never goes below 11 points, though it grows past 200%; and system-drawn text (the search field, the navigation title). `[evidence_level: measured (Simulator audits and screenshot contrast, `scratchpad/a11y/pixel_contrast.py`), test_verified (1,136 run, 0 failures, 6 skipped, 2026-10-16 07:23 PT), build_verified; not on a device; confidence: high for the measured numbers; evidence_source: Theme.swift, IngestionQueueOverlay.swift, EmptyDocumentsView.swift, ContainerPicker.swift, DocumentLibraryView.swift, DocumentQuotaBanner.swift, ChatScreen.swift, ModelStatusIndicator.swift, SettingsView.swift, AppTips.swift]`
- **[UI]** **Words on tinted chips are in the label color, the accent is a deeper blue, and the readable colors work on the Mac.** The owner's two choices of 2026-10-16 ("Black/white text, color stays", "Deeper blue #005CDB"): the mode chip, the model pill, the document and passage counts and the Settings status pills draw their words in the label color and keep the mode's or state's color in the icon and the tint, because colored words on a pale tint of themselves measured 3.5 to 4.1 to 1 even in Apple's high-contrast colors; and the AccentColor asset is #005CDB in light (was #007AFF), so white on a filled blue button and blue text on white both go from 4.0 to 5.9 to 1. In dark it stays #007AFF: the deeper blue would take blue text on black from 5.2 to 3.6 to 1, and against the dark cards (#1C1C1E) no one blue serves both white-on-blue and blue-on-card (white on the dark blue stays 4.0). A refuting review of the previous entry's commit found that on the Mac `readable(platform:)` did nothing, since `NSAppearance(named:)` returns nil for the high-contrast names (NSAppearance.h in the macOS 27 SDK), so `DSColors.secondaryText` there was plain `systemGray`: AppKit colors are now blended toward black in light or white in dark until they reach 4.5 to 1 against the window background. Also from the review: the iOS resolution uses `modifyingTraits` (`traitCollectionWithTraitsFromCollections` is deprecated since iOS 17); every tip's message takes the readable gray from the app's own tip style; and the model pill is fixed size like the quality pill beside it. A second refuting review found that this made the chat header's row run past a 402-point screen at every text size above the default and while an answer ran ("Using Private Cloud Compute" measures 154 points at the default size), and the system font's measured widths put a library of 100 passages or more past it too, so the row now takes as few lines as fit: the pills and the counts on one line, then the counts on a line of their own, then the pills on lines of their own, at the leading edge (`WrappingRowLayout`, a `Layout` that keeps the same views at every size; built first with `ViewThatFits`, a larger size built different views and Apple's audit read the row's text as not growing, 15 and 19 issues at the default size instead of 11 and 14); and the running line names the route the way the pill's first line names the preferences ("On-Device", "PCC", "Hybrid"), never wider than the line it replaces, so the row does not change shape when an answer starts. Two more defects at the largest text size, found on the screenshots and not reported by the audit: Settings' two status pills sat side by side with about 130 points each and broke "PCC" and "Available" in the middle of the word, the capsule cutting the last letter, so the identity row puts its icon above the text there, its two pills go onto two lines whenever they do not fit side by side (`WrappingRowLayout`), the pills become rounded rectangles that shape only their background, and their text keeps its full height so it wraps (the first stacked version cut it to "On-Device Fir..."); and the Documents header (title, counts, actions, libraries, quota) is taller than the screen there and sat outside any scroll view, so its stack was centered and spilled up under the status and navigation bars, with the large title's second line ("Docu-" / "ments") drawn over it. At accessibility sizes the header now scrolls with the documents in one scroll view, its Local and iCloud picker sits under the title at the leading edge, and the navigation title is inline. And the question field drew at a fixed 16 points, the audit's "Dynamic Type font sizes are unsupported" on Chat: it is Callout on iPhone and iPad now, 16 points at the default size and growing with the setting, and stays 16 points on the Mac, whose Callout is 12; at accessibility sizes its hint is "Ask...", because "Ask anything..." was cut to "Ask any..." there. The empty Documents screen's document-limit warning, orange words on a tint of orange, measured 4.43 to 1 on the Mac with AppKit's own colors and the same blend (`BenchmarkRuns/2026-10-16-accessibility-pass-2/mac_readable_on_tint.swift`), so its words are in the label color and the orange is in the icon, as on the other tinted chips; the quota banner's orange and yellow sit on the plain card, and against the window background the Mac's reach 4.73 and 5.11 in light and 5.74 and 9.08 in dark. `AccessibilityAuditTests` now runs with the X-Ray off, through a `-showSiliconHUD NO` launch argument that `SettingsStore` reads with `bool(forKey:)`. Apple's audit with the X-Ray off, in light and dark at the default and the largest text size, reports 11, 14, 7 and 7 issues on the three screens (54, 44, 35 and 31 on 2026-10-15), and 7 at the largest size below the accessibility sizes: every contrast item it names an element for measures 5.06 to 13.3 to 1 on the screenshot (an earlier run took one frame mid-scroll and read 1.13 for a pill that is 14.5 to 16.8 where drawn), the items it names no element for sit on screens pixel-identical to a run where they measured 4.55 or more, and the rest are `.caption2` text it calls partly supported although it grows past 200% and the system search field (`BenchmarkRuns/2026-10-16-accessibility-pass-2/`, local). `[evidence_level: measured (Simulator audits and screenshot contrast), test_verified (1,138 unit tests, 0 failures, 6 skipped as before, and the 3 UI tests, `AccessibilityAuditTests` with the X-Ray off among them, on the final tree), build_verified (iPhone, Simulator, Mac); the Mac colors are not measured on screen; not on a device; confidence: high for iOS, medium for the Mac; evidence_source: Theme.swift, AccentColor.colorset, ChatScreen.swift, WrappingRowLayout.swift, ModelStatusIndicator.swift (`routeName`), EmptyDocumentsView.swift, SettingsView.swift, AppTips.swift, SettingsStore.swift, DocumentLibraryView.swift, ChatComposerV2.swift, AccessibilityAuditTests.swift]`
- **[UI]** **The rest of the common tasks at every text size: an answer, adding documents, the first-run screen and the plans screen.** Apple's Accessibility Nutrition Labels describe how an app's common tasks work, not only its main screens, so the scratch audit (`AccessibilityLabelMeasurement`, run by `run_common_tasks.sh`, both in `BenchmarkRuns/2026-10-16-accessibility-pass-3/`) went on from Chat, Documents and Settings to the first-run screen on a fresh install, an answer with its Fact Check, its sources and a refusal (the screenshot mode's seeded scenes; the Simulator has no model), the import panel while the sample documents import, paused and finished, the filled library, a document's page and the plans sheet, in light and dark at the default size, at the largest size below the accessibility sizes, and at the largest. Every contrast item it named an element for was measured on its screenshot (`pixel_contrast.py`), and every largest-size screenshot was looked at, because most of what was wrong there the audit did not name: on a running import panel it gives no element at all.

  What changed:
  - **Text sizes.** The answer, its details, the Fact Check, the import panel and the document page drew most text at fixed point sizes. They follow Dynamic Type now through `scaledFont(size:weight:design:relativeTo:)` (`Theme.swift`, `@ScaledMetric`): the design size at the default, larger with the setting on iPhone and iPad, fixed on the Mac. Their grays are `DSColors.secondaryText`.
  - **Colors.** Colored words follow the owner's rule of 2026-10-16: on a tint they are in the label color with the color kept in the icon and the tint, elsewhere they take `DSColors.readable(_:)`, and green words are in the label color because Apple's high-contrast green measured 4.07 to 1 on the cards' gray. Measured before, on the screenshots: the sources' "Excellent" 2.01 to 1, their rank numbers 1.79 and percentages 1.99, the document page's "Persisted" 1.99 and "MARKDOWN" 4.49, the plans screen's "Avoid interruptions by upgrading..." 3.48 and "App Store privacy label: Data Not Collected" 3.34, and on the import panel "Processing Log" 3.82, "BATCH TOTALS" 3.99 and the paused panel's explanation 4.48. The same rule went through the import panel's per-file details, Deep Think's reasoning steps, the steps shown while an answer is written (which also no longer fade the earlier steps to 78%), the answer type and claim verdict badges in Response Details, and a document's tags.
  - **The import panel at the largest sizes** broke words in the middle ("Pause / d", "Com / plete", "42" as "4 / 2"), cut its buttons to "Conti..." and "Disc...", and the decision panel and totals filled the screen while only the files below them scrolled, out of reach. Everything under the header scrolls as one now, up to 560 points and less when the screen has less, and pausing scrolls Continue and Discard into view. The stage line, the totals, Continue and Discard, and the console's rows stack at accessibility sizes (`AnyLayout`, so the views stay the same when the size changes), file names take up to three lines there, and the panel's texts wrap. Each file's row sits on the elevated surface, nearly opaque, where its gray detail read 4.24 to 4.43 to 1 over the glass panel on the plain one and reads 5.14. A file's details grow with the text size up to the largest size below the accessibility sizes, with timing columns that grow with them; the audit could not open them, because finished files leave the queue 4 seconds after they end, so they were checked in the code only.
  - **The import panel's header.** Its buttons are 44 points to tap on iPhone and iPad (`DSSpacing.tapTarget`, 28 on the Mac), which at the default size left the title 53 points and cut it to "Pr...": the title and the import's own controls share a `WrappingRowLayout`, side by side when they fit, as on the Mac, and otherwise the controls go under the title. Paused, the header no longer repeats the decision panel's Continue and Discard as two unlabeled circles, and, as before, it has no close button then: closing would delete the paused files' checkpoints without Discard's question. Minimized, the panel keeps its full width and shows Pause; the processing-mode chip shows only when the panel is open, and it is read as "Processing mode" with its value. A file's details button is 44 points and named, the totals are read as "1 of 3 files done", "27 passages", "13 vectors" and "2.2K words" instead of a symbol's name and a bare number, the decorative icons are hidden from VoiceOver, and the separators are drawn dots: a "•" hidden from VoiceOver while it shows is text Apple's element check reports as out of VoiceOver's reach, which the repository's own audit test did on a filled library.

- **[Ingestion]** **A table cell that holds only a number or a code is no longer emptied.** Every cell of a table went alone through `OCRConfiguration.normalizeExtractedText` with the default profile, whose last step (`stripDocumentNoise`) removes a line that is only a page number (`^\d{1,4}$`, ranges, doubled numbers), an all-capitals code with digits, or a page-reference cluster. That step is written for the lines of a page ("Only removes entire lines"); a cell is one line, so "34", "7", "2024", "10-20", "SKU10293" and "INV2024001" came back empty while "12345", "4.5" and "M12" survived. A new test showed it end to end before the fix: a CSV row `Widget,34,2024,SKU10293` was stored as `ROW 1: Widget |  |  | `. The cells were in no passage, vector or search-index row, and nothing was logged. Now `TextNormalizationProfile` has a third case, `tableCell`: every repair `extractedDocument` makes, without that last step. It is used at the ten places that clean a cell, a list item or the two sides of a `key: value` line (`promotedLayoutTableElements`, `tableQualityScore`, `tableRowQualityScore`, `regionRescuePayload` tables, `splitPipeTableCells`, `parseKeyValueLine` three times, `normalizedListItems`, `formattedPipeTableText`, which is the CSV and spreadsheet path). Page lines are cleaned as before. Two side effects: the two table quality scores now count a number-only cell as filled, so a numeric table scores higher, and a table imported before this needs importing again to get its cells back. Found by a read-only audit of the import pipeline on 2026-10-10, which measured it with a copy of the function, and confirmed here in code and by the test. Tests: `IngestionAccuracyTests` (three on cells) and `IngestionFormatCoverageTests.testCSV_KeepsCellsThatAreOnlyANumberOrACode`. `[evidence_level: test_verified (777 tests, 0 failures, 3 skipped, 2026-10-10 10:14 PT; the CSV test failed before `formattedPipeTableText` was changed and passes after), not seen on a device; confidence: high for CSV and pipe tables, medium that no other cell path still uses the page profile (37 call sites of the function in DocumentProcessor.swift were read and sorted into cells and page lines by hand); evidence_source: BenchmarkRuns/2026-10-10-ingestion-and-ui-audit/A-ingestion-cost-and-loss-map.md (local), OCRConfiguration.swift stripDocumentNoise, /private/tmp/oi-57-test15.log and oi-57-test16.log]`
- **[Ingestion]** **A line in Greek, Chinese, Arabic or another script is no longer deleted from a page as garbage.** `OCRConfiguration.isGarbageText`, rule 5, calls a line garbage when it is longer than 10 characters and under 35% alphanumeric. Its count of letters (`totalLetters`) holds ASCII letters and Cyrillic letters only, because the script-mixing and vowel rules need exactly those, so a line in any other script counted as 0% alphanumeric. PDF pages are filtered with `revertIfTooAggressive: false`, so a page that lost every line stayed empty. Rule 5 now counts every scalar that Unicode marks alphabetic or numeric, and takes the larger of the two counts. The other rules are unchanged. Tests (`IngestionAccuracyTests`): a Greek, a Chinese and an Arabic line are not garbage in a document of that script; a four-line Greek page keeps four lines through `filterGarbageText(_:revertIfTooAggressive: false)`; a line of symbols is still garbage. Not tested: a single line of another script inside a Latin-script page with `isLatinDocument: true`, which other rules may still remove. Not changed: `StructuredDocumentParser` tags a scanned paragraph low quality from a 170-word English list and the paragraph is then skipped; that is listed in the audit row. `[evidence_level: test_verified (same run), not seen on a device or on a real PDF; confidence: high for the rule, low for how a whole Greek PDF now fares end to end (the low-quality tag and the OCR language list are separate steps); evidence_source: BenchmarkRuns/2026-10-10-ingestion-and-ui-audit/A-ingestion-cost-and-loss-map.md (local), OCRConfiguration.swift isGarbageText]`
- **[Ingestion]** **A recording's language is no longer read from two letters inside a word of its file name.** `extractTextFromAudio` chose the transcription language with `filename.contains("_es")`, `"_fr"`, `"_de"`, `"_zh"`, `"_ja"`, so "notes_friday.m4a" was transcribed as French, "plans_december.m4a" as German, "my_essay.mp3" as Spanish and "taxes_january.m4a" as Japanese. `DocumentProcessor.speechLanguage(forFilename:)` now takes a two-letter code only when it is the last word of the name before the extension ("lecture_fr.m4a", "talk-de.mp3"), or the language's English name anywhere in it, and returns en-US otherwise. The default for an unmarked name is still en-US whatever the device's language; that is not changed here. Tests: two in `IngestionAccuracyTests`. `[evidence_level: test_verified (same run), not run on a recording; confidence: high; evidence_source: BenchmarkRuns/2026-10-10-ingestion-and-ui-audit/A-ingestion-cost-and-loss-map.md (local), DocumentProcessor.swift]`
- **[Chunking]** **A passage over the embedding limit is no longer retyped when it is cut.** `splitOversizedChunkByTokens` split such a passage at every ".", "!", "?" and line break and joined the pieces back with ". ". The stored passage, which search indexes, a citation shows and the model reads, then held "$75. 50" for "$75.50" and "Lease. pdf" for "Lease.pdf", every "?" and "!" had become ".", and every line break ". ". It applied to every format, whenever a chunk came out over 430 embedding tokens, and the only trace was the "SPLIT OVERSIZED CHUNK" log line. Found on 2026-10-09 by a reviewing pass over the JSON import and confirmed by reading the function (roadmap row 3f449a74d54f81db9f1ce1c943e94a0b, filed that day). `OversizedChunkSplitter` now builds each part from the passage's own lines, cuts a line that is too long at its sentence ends with `AnswerSentenceSplitter`, a sentence that is still too long between words, and a single run with no space that is too long by itself (Chinese or Japanese prose, a base64 block) by characters; the one change to the text is that the whitespace between two sentences of a line that had to be cut becomes one space. A document imported before this keeps its stored text until it is imported again. `[evidence_level: test_verified, confidence: high; evidence_source: OversizedChunkSplitterTests (5 cases); DocumentProcessor.swift splitOversizedChunkByTokens; the full suite on an iOS 27.0 simulator on 2026-10-09 from a copy of the tree made outside iCloud: 670 tests, 0 failures, 3 skipped (the two EmbeddingProviderAgreementTests and LayoutReadingOrderTests' interleaved-stream case, which skip themselves)]` How often a chunk exceeds the limit in real documents has not been counted.
- **[Shortcuts]** **The eleven older actions return values and fail by throwing; two of them take a mode.** `RAGAppIntents.swift`, named by the owner on 2026-10-09. The ask actions ("Ask My Documents", "Ask a Library", "Ask About a Document", "Summarize a Document", "Compare Two Documents") go through one `AskActionRunner`: they return an `OIAnswerEntity` with the answer, its passages and its source list, use the engine's default generation settings (through 5.6 each cut its answer at 300 or 400 tokens and set its own temperature; these are still not the chat's model and cloud choices), and throw `OIIntentError` where they used to return a successful result holding an apology. "Ask My Documents" and "Ask a Library" take a Standard, Deep Think or Maximum menu, starting on Standard, which is what every action ran in through 5.6: each built an engine with no settings store, and the mode falls back to Standard without one (`QueryRuntimeCoordinator.swift`, the `qualityModeOverride ?? settingsStore?.ragQualityMode ?? .standard` line). The three actions that name a document have no menu and run in Standard, where the document limit is enforced and checked. "List Documents" returns the documents and takes an optional library, "List Conversations" returns the conversations, and "Check Import Status" and "Get Embedding Model" return their sentence or name. "Add a Document" and "Start a Conversation" open the app and return nothing (rows 3f449a74d54f8153bac7f4996d704249, 3f449a74d54f8191a690c82195d5fbc9). On the free plan a Maximum answer is checked against the same daily allowance as in the chat before the question and counted only after an answer comes back (`ShortcutsPlanGate`, using `MaximumModeQuotaStore` and the plan stored in UserDefaults); the app's own remaining-runs display catches up when it next recalculates. "Ask My Documents" keeps its Passages number without a range, so a larger number saved on 5.6 is still accepted, and the runner holds it between 1 and 12.
