- **[Shortcuts]** **"Ask About a Document" is limited to that document.** It wrote the file name into the question and called `query`, which has no document filter, so the answer could be written from any document in the library (row 3f449a74d54f81c0963bc125ccabbb33). `RAGQueryScope.documentIds` is a task-local set the action puts around its one `query` call. The limit is applied in four places. (1) `HybridSearchService.search`, which every search in Standard, Deep Think and Maximum goes through, scores the named documents' passages directly when a scope is set (cosine similarity and the share of the question's words, by the search's own two weights) and returns nothing else; the library-wide vector index and FTS5 are not consulted, because they ignore the candidate list and a small document in a large library would be crowded out. (2) In Standard, after retrieval, the result and the two candidate lists the later stages draw on are narrowed, a scoped question skips the query cache in both directions, and a scoped question with no passage throws `RAGQueryScopeEmptyError` where the general fallback would have answered from the model alone. (3) The candidate lists of Deep Think's and Maximum's precision lookup and full retrieval, and of `searchDocumentsRaw`, are narrowed the same way. (4) `AskActionRunner` runs these three actions in Standard and, as a last check, refuses to return an answer whose passages are not all from the named documents, or that has none. "Summarize a Document" and "Compare Two Documents" use the same scope; Compare refuses two documents from different libraries, and a document stored with no library id is taken to be in the first library. A first version of this, written the same day, narrowed only the Standard result after a library-wide search; a reviewing pass showed it leaked through three later searches and did nothing in Deep Think or Maximum, and it was replaced before anything was committed. `[evidence_level: test_verified for the scope's filter and inheritance (RAGQueryScopeTests, 3 cases), code_verified for the four places, confidence: medium; evidence_source: HybridSearchService.swift searchInsideScope; RAGService.swift queryInternal, performFullRetrievalPipeline, executeAgenticPrecisionLookupIfAvailable, searchDocumentsRaw; RAGAppIntents.swift AskActionRunner; the full suite on an iOS 27.0 simulator on 2026-10-09 from a copy of the tree made outside iCloud: 670 tests, 0 failures, 3 skipped (the two EmbeddingProviderAgreementTests and LayoutReadingOrderTests' interleaved-stream case, which skip themselves)]` No scoped question has been asked anywhere: no test runs one through `queryInternal`, because that needs a library and a model. What stands behind the claim until a device run is the action's last check. *Corrected 2026-10-13:* the cosine half of (1) did nothing with the store the app uses until the fix in the next entry. That store hands passages back without their vectors, the similarity came out as 0 for every passage, and the ranking was by the question's words alone.
- **[Retrieval]** **A question limited to a document is ranked by meaning as well as by its words.** `HybridSearchService.searchInsideScope` (added 2026-10-09 for "Ask About a Document", "Summarize a Document" and "Compare Two Documents") scores each passage of the named documents by cosine similarity with the question and by the share of the question's words it holds. It took the similarity from the vector on the passage, and `BNNSVectorDatabase` hands passages back without their vectors, which stay in its own file. So every similarity was 0 and the ranking was by words alone: a question that shared no word with the passage that answers it had nothing to rank by. Found by the review of the storage change (row 3f849a74d54f81609d79e3a41e229eb1). The scoped search now asks the store how close each passage is, and its log line says how many passages were compared by vector. As first committed (`c73b3b9`) it asked for the vectors by id, 2,048 at a time, because `BNNSVectorDatabase.swift` is a hard-boundary file and the owner's word then covered the storage change only. On 2026-10-13 he answered the report that said the faster form waited on his word for that file with a two-word go-ahead for everything in it. He did not name the file himself; the agent read the answer as his word for it, told him so, and kept the change to methods that read (the file's format is untouched). The store now answers `similarities(to:forChunkIDs:)` from the vectors where they are, the mapped file or the vectors not saved yet, with none copied out. A store without a way of its own answers it from `getEmbeddings(forChunkIDs:)`, 2,048 at a time, and the store kept in memory now answers that from the vectors on its passages. A scope of more than 6,000 passages (`scopedExactLimit`; the owner's library is one document of 144,247) no longer has every passage's text searched for the question's words: every vector is scored, and words are compared for the 2,000 closest passages and for the keyword index's best 400 hits that fall inside the scope. That holds whether the passages are handed in or read by id, so one question gets one set of candidates. When no vector can be scored for any passage of the scope, every passage is read and ranked by its words; that includes a question whose own embedding failed, which was being taken for a match with the empty vector each passage comes back with. The closest passages are picked on the concurrent pool, equal similarities in store order, so one question picks the same passages every time. A passage that is far from the question and that the index does not rank among its best hits can be missed on the bounded path; up to 6,000 passages every passage is still scored both ways. The keyword hits come from the index's own chunk search, which matches every word first and any word only when nothing matches all of them, across the whole library: when a passage outside the scope holds every word, the scope gets no keyword candidates for that question. That cannot happen where the scope is the whole library, as in the owner's. No version with the scoped search has shipped. `[evidence_level: test_verified (HybridSearchServiceTests, nine tests: a store on files with one passage not yet saved, a 5,000-passage document, the store's scores against vectors copied out, a 7,000-passage document with and without the keyword index and handed in or read by id, the same size on the store kept in memory, a scope with no vectors anywhere, a question with no vector, how the closest passages are picked, and a small scope in memory), code_verified that the similarity was 0 before; not run on a device, and what it changes in the answers of the three Shortcuts actions is not measured, confidence: high for the ranking, medium for what the bounded path misses on real documents, evidence_source: HybridSearchService.swift searchInsideScope; BNNSVectorDatabase.swift similarities; VectorDatabase.swift]`

The gate counts nothing it skipped before (`NumericValueExtractor.isCountedByGate`): digits with a letter against either side ("5th", "10pm", "Q3", "4K", "3B", "iPhone17") were never counted and still are not, and years still pass. Apple's `DataDetector` was not used: its matches arrive as an asynchronous sequence and the gate is synchronous. Stricter on purpose: "100" no longer passes against "100,000", and a number written only inside a name no longer supports the bare number. - **[Indexing]** **iCloud sync leaves a library's vector store alone while an import into it is unfinished.** An import puts its passages in the store as it goes and saves its document record last. `WorkspaceSyncService.synchronizeVectorStore` works from the document records: it decodes every passage of the store from both the device and the iCloud copy, keeps the passages whose document it knows, and writes the store back where that differs. A pass runs two seconds after every save of the store, and an import in pieces saves once a piece. On the 88.50 MB import of 2026-10-10 the trace on the iPhone holds 135 such reads of one store in its last 500 KB (two a piece, of a store growing to 144,247 passages and 381 MB on disk, 1.9 to 2.8 s each at the end), every one ending in "refusing to delete", which is what the pass says for a library with no document yet. In a library that already has a document the same pass would have written the store back without the import's passages (`mergeVectorChunks` keeps a passage only when its document has a record); the import's own store object writes them again at its next save, so the window that loses them is the one after the last save and before the document record, about 35 s in that trace, and a pass did run inside it. That case was read from the code and not run on a device. Now a pass reads the import queue before it reads the documents and again before the vector step (`librariesWithUnfinishedImports`: an item this device holds that is not complete, cancelled or failed) and skips the vector store of any library named in either reading; the documents, the queue and the other files sync as before, and the store syncs on the first pass after the import ends. - **[Shortcuts]** **"Scan a Document" opens Apple's document scanner, and the app's own camera is back to Debug builds only.** Its first 5.7 version (entry above) opened `CameraVisionOverlayView`, the app's camera, which the owner keeps to Debug builds (decided 2026-09-13; `ChatScreen.visionCaptureAction`): the action, the Home Screen quick action and `openintelligence://chat/scan` made it reachable in a Release build. Found by a read-only triage of the roadmap on 2026-10-14. The route now presents `VNDocumentCameraViewController` (VisionKit, the scanner Notes and Files use, which runs its own camera session); The pages are read and compressed one at a time (`PDFPage(image:options:)`, JPEG quality 0.75) with a yield between pages, so a long scan never holds every page image at once and the screen keeps drawing, and `ScannedDocument.store` writes them as one PDF to the imported-documents folder, named from the scanner's title and the time; the import review takes it like a file opened from another app. A device without the scanner gets a message. `[evidence_level: test_verified (ScannedDocumentTests: pages become one PDF in the import folder, a scan with no pages is refused, the name), build_verified; confidence: medium; the simulator has no camera, so the scanner itself has not been run; evidence_source: SystemDocumentScanner.swift; ChatScreen.swift takeNavigationRequest and storeScan]`
- **[Infrastructure]** **An answer's background task is submitted under a name of its own.** `BackgroundTaskService.beginUserInitiatedQuery` submitted its `BGContinuedProcessingTask` under the fixed name `com.openintelligence.rag-query`, the shape the import's task had when it was seen accepted and never started on an iPhone on 2026-10-09 (entry above). It now submits under `<bundle id>.rag-query.<UUID>` and registers the handler under that name as it submits, as the import does, and `Info.plist` permits `$(PRODUCT_BUNDLE_IDENTIFIER).rag-query.*` (backlog rows 3f549a74d54f81d1b0c2c7159e127e07 and 3c149a74d54f8171adfcce5dcb345777, found together by the triage of 2026-10-14). `[evidence_level: build_verified; confidence: medium; closes when a Deep Think answer left in the background logs "Starting continued query task" in the trace; evidence_source: BackgroundTaskService.swift continuedQueryIdentifierStem; Info.plist]`
- **[Indexing]** **Spotlight entries are the app's items, last until their document is deleted, open what they name, and can be rebuilt.** Through 5.6 a document's entry expired after 30 days and nothing indexed it again; a passage's entry (`chunk-<id>`) opened nothing and stayed for 30 days after its document was deleted; `reindexAll` had no caller and the background re-index task only set a flag nothing read (row 3f449a74d54f81beadd4f8e3dcaf38f5). Now `OIDocumentEntity` and `OILibraryEntity` adopt Apple's `IndexedEntity` and each entry carries its item (`associateAppEntity`), so its Open action opens it; documents and passages do not expire; a passage's entry is `passage-<document>-<passage>` and opens its document (`AppLink.destination(forSpotlightIdentifier:)`); deleting a document removes its passages through their domain, a recursive delete in Apple's documentation (`RAGService.removeDocument` passes the library, a one-line change, and `EngineSDKCompatibility.swift`'s stub takes the same parameter); passage entries are built off the main actor; and `reindexFromStoredRecords` rebuilds the library and document entries from the two record files, for the background task and, on 27, for Spotlight itself (`IndexedEntityQuery`). The app has no document rename; a library's rename indexes its entry again under the same identifier. On the first launch of 5.7 every library and document entry is written again whole, so it lasts whether or not an update moves an expiry, which Apple's documentation does not say: the document's text preview, its first 500 characters, is read again from the full-text store (`SQLiteFullTextService.getContentPreview`; none for a document of more than 2,000 passages, whose whole text that read would load). The background task refreshes names as updates (`isUpdate`), which now leave an attribute a record lacks unset, where setting it to nil deleted it. Passage entries made by 5.6 still expire 30 days after their import. Passage entries no longer set `relatedUniqueIdentifier`, which Apple documents for activities only (second review, 2026-10-14). `[evidence_level: test_verified for the entries' descriptions and the passage address (SpotlightItemsTests, AppLinkTests), build_verified for iPhone and Mac; confidence: medium; nothing was searched in Spotlight on a device; evidence_source: SpotlightIndexService.swift; AppNavigationRequest.swift; BackgroundTaskService.swift handleSpotlightReindex; developer.apple.com, CSSearchableIndex deleteSearchableItems(withDomainIdentifiers:), read 2026-10-14]`
- **[Indexing]** **A document's Spotlight entries go when it goes, wherever it was deleted, and turning Spotlight Indexing off removes them.** From 5.7 entries do not expire (entry above), and a second review on 2026-10-14 found three ways an entry could then outlive its document: a document deleted on another device arrives through `RAGService.applyLoadedDocuments`, which removed nothing from this device's index; an import that failed or was cancelled after its entry was written left the entry; and a delete that came while a document's passages were still being written was followed by the write. Now `SpotlightIndexService.documentsLoaded` compares each load with the documents this device knows, a list kept in the device's own folder (`spotlight_documents.json`, document and library identifiers only), and removes the entries and passage domains of those gone, leaving alone an import in progress; a document this device has not listed, imported on another device or while the switch was off, is listed from its record, without passages. The import's catch removes the entry it wrote, and a passage write a delete overtook removes its entries once written. Through 5.6 turning "Spotlight Indexing" off only stopped new entries; `indexingSwitched(on:)` now deletes every entry the app made (`deleteAllSearchableItems`), its saved activities (`NSUserActivity.deleteAllSavedUserActivities`) and its Open Library donations (`IntentDonationManager.deleteDonations`), and turning it on lists every library and document again. A failed read of the document records now changes nothing (`loadDocumentsSnapshotFromDisk` returns nil): through 5.6 it emptied the library on screen and removed what was kept about every library, and with the list it would have removed every entry. `[evidence_level: test_verified for the list's arithmetic and the preview text (SpotlightItemsTests), build_verified; confidence: medium; nothing was searched in Spotlight on a device and no document was deleted on a second device, which is what closes this; evidence_source: SpotlightIndexService.swift documentsLoaded, indexingSwitched, indexDocumentChunks; RAGService.swift applyLoadedDocuments, loadDocumentsSnapshotFromDisk, addDocument; ContentView.swift; EngineSDKCompatibility.swift stub]`
- **[Infrastructure]** **A changed setting is saved with its new value.** `SettingsStore` saved every setting from one sink on the settings' publishers, and a `@Published` property publishes from its `willSet`, so the sink read the value the property had before: the setting just changed kept its old value on disk until another setting changed, and a relaunch brought the old value back. A standalone Swift probe of the same pattern printed `in memory: false saved by the sink: Optional(true)` (2026-10-14). The save now runs on the main queue once the value is set (`receive(on: DispatchQueue.main)`); the apply step was already debounced and read the new value. It mattered most for "Spotlight Indexing", whose readers outside the settings screen read the saved value. `[evidence_level: measured (the probe) for the mechanism, test_verified (SettingsPersistenceTests), confidence: high; evidence_source: SettingsStore.swift setupPipelines]`
- **[Ingestion]** **A PDF over 10 MB keeps its page rows for every batch.** The keyword rows were fixed earlier in 5.7 (entry above); the page rows had the same kind of loss by another route. `DocumentProcessor` stores each fifteen-page batch with `store(append: true)` and then `storePages(append: true)`, and `store` clears the document's rows with `delete(for:)`, which also deletes its `document_pages` rows, so only the last batch's pages could be found by page (backlog row 3f549a74d54f8178ad55d3d273cc832a, confirmed by a read-only triage on 2026-10-14). `DocumentProcessor.storeText` now reads the earlier rows (`retrieveAllPages`) before the text store and stores them again with the batch's own; a page the batch brings replaces its earlier row, so a batch stored again by a resumed import leaves one row per page. The fix is in the caller: `SQLiteFullTextService.swift` is on the boundaries list and the owner has not named it, so it is unchanged. The re-store grows with the square of the page count. A PDF imported before this keeps its last batch's pages until it is imported again. `[evidence_level: test_verified (PageRowsAcrossBatchesTests), confidence: high; evidence_source: DocumentProcessor.swift storeText, pageRows]`
- **[Ingestion]** **A PDF's page rows carry their own page numbers, and a batch of one page keeps its row.** Both PDF extractors put a page break only before a page that has text, and `processDocument` numbered the pieces between breaks by their place, so after a blank page every later page's row, and its `--- Page N ---` marker in the stored text, carried the number of the page before it; corrective retrieval cites a page hit by its row's number, so such an answer named the wrong page. `DocumentProcessor.numberedPages` numbers each piece from the extractor's own list of pages with text (`PageInfo.pageTextRanges`) and falls back to the place only when the counts differ, which neither extractor produces. A batch of a PDF imported fifteen pages at a time is numbered even when it holds one page, so the last page of, say, a 31-page PDF has its row; the page-row entry above said every batch kept its rows, and a one-page batch did not (second review, 2026-10-14). A PDF imported before this keeps its rows until it is imported again. `[evidence_level: test_verified (PageRowsAcrossBatchesTests.testPagesAreNumberedByThePagesThatHaveText), code_verified that both extractors insert a break only before a page with text (DocumentProcessor.swift, the two page assembly loops); confidence: high; not seen on a device; evidence_source: DocumentProcessor.swift numberedPages]`
- **[Orchestration]** **Without Apple Intelligence, an answer made of excerpts says why.** When no answer could be written, the excerpts ended by saying the model "may be temporarily rate-limited", and with no excerpts the answer said it could not reach the model, whatever the cause; on a device that cannot run the model, or with Apple Intelligence off, neither is the reason (row 3e449a74d54f8170a3cbfb570a41d0a5). `AppleIntelligenceCopy.excerptClosing` gives the closing line from the same unavailability reason the empty chat's notice uses, and the no-excerpt case uses `chatMessage`; the line on a device that can run the model no longer guesses at a rate limit. `RAGService` is edited at that one fallback. `[evidence_level: test_verified (AppleIntelligenceCopyTests), confidence: medium; evidence_source: AppleIntelligenceAvailability.swift; RAGService.swift, the extractive fallback; not seen on a device without Apple Intelligence, which is what closes the row]`
- **[UI]** **Settings no longer lists tool functions for Deep Think and Maximum.** The list of what those modes do showed "4 Tool Functions" with four names. `FoundationModelToolRegistry.createTools` registers six, and neither number was true of these modes: every model call they make passes `disableTools: true` (the fourteen `generateWithProperConsent` calls in `AgenticOrchestrator`, the chain sessions through `disableToolsForSession`), so no tool reaches the model in them (backlog row 3ea49a74d54f8167a9b3d92c94539567, whose premise was the count). The line is removed, with a comment that says when to add one back. `[evidence_level: code_verified, confidence: high; evidence_source: SettingsView.swift; AgenticOrchestrator.swift; a grep for disableTools on 2026-10-14]`
- **[Ingestion]** **The Mac's page classifier looks at the page, and import summaries are written without tools.** `PageComplexityAnalyzer.renderPageForAnalysis` returned nil on the Mac, so the Vision pass that finds tables, charts and figures a text layer does not show never ran there (backlog row 3cb49a74d54f81fbb47cec6d8ed526d8); it now draws into a Core Graphics bitmap. `DocumentSummaryService` handed the six library tools to every summary it wrote during an import; it sets `disableTools` now (part of backlog row 3b449a74d54f818486feee1dada5554b). `[evidence_level: build_verified for the Mac; confidence: medium; the same PDF has not been compared on both platforms; evidence_source: PageComplexityAnalyzer.swift; DocumentSummaryService.swift]`
- **[UI]** **Fact Check quotes the sentence of a passage that best matches its claim.** The supporting quote under a claim was the first 240 characters of its passage, wherever the claim's words were (backlog row 3ec49a74d54f81059d22d46955b11bc4). `StructuredAnswer.quote(from:supporting:)` picks the line or sentence that shares the most words with the claims citing the passage (sentences cut by `EvidenceLineSplitter`, which keeps "p.m." whole), adds what follows up to 240 characters, and cuts a longer sentence at a word; with no claim or no word in common it is the start, as before. A quote belongs to the passage, not to one claim: two claims citing one passage share the sentence that matches both best, and a passage retrieved with its parent section is quoted from that section (`parentContent`), the text the model was given, so the sentence can lie outside the passage itself (second review, 2026-10-14). `[evidence_level: test_verified (FactCheckQuoteTests), confidence: high; evidence_source: StructuredAnswer.swift quote(from:supporting:); not seen on a device]`
- **[Indexing]** **iCloud no longer gets every library's chat history, transcript and memory again on every sync pass, and a vector store goes up to iCloud whole.** Through 5.6 `WorkspaceSyncService.coordinatedCopyItem` deleted the destination and copied the source in again on every pass, changed or not, so each library's `chat_history_`, `transcript_` and `conversation_memory_` file went up to iCloud again from every device on every pass, and two devices doing that at once is how iCloud keeps both versions as "<name> 2.json". On 2026-10-14 the owner's iCloud container held 564 files, 500 of them such copies, all in the six per-library file families (transcripts 119, conversation memory 92, chat history 87, vector metadata 75, vectors 62, norms 62; only file names and sizes were read). An unchanged file is now left alone and a changed one is replaced in place (`replaceItemIfDifferent`, `replaceItemAt`), and the result keeps the source's modification date as the old delete-and-copy did (a probe of both on 2026-10-14), so every newest-wins choice decides as before. A library's vector store was written into iCloud by opening it there, clearing it (which deletes its three files) and writing new ones with no coordination. It is now written in a replacement folder on the same volume, and its three files replace the iCloud ones under one coordinated claim (`persistVectorChunksIntoICloud`, `coordinatedReplaceItems`), at both places sync writes one. Each pass logs how many conflict copies the folder holds; the app reads none of them and removes none. The sweep guard and the modification-date logic are unchanged. Row https://app.notion.com/p/3ca49a74d54f8103b69be921f0335171; closes when the iPhone and the Mac use one library through a session and the count doesn't grow. `[evidence_level: test_verified, confidence: medium; evidence_source: WorkspaceSyncFileWriteTests; two devices writing at once not run]`
- **[Indexing]** **An import whose embedding model returns empty vectors stops and says so, and a library built from them, or missing a document's passages, is repaired.** The batch path every import uses (`EmbeddingService.generateEmbeddings`) checked nothing, so a model that returned zeros built a library that looked healthy and could be searched only by its words: one made in the iOS 27 Simulator on 2026-10-08 held 127 passages, every vector zero, and 9 of 11 answers in it failed Gate E. 5.7 makes the Core ML copy, which wrote those zeros there, the default for new iPhone and iPad libraries. `EmbeddingVectorCheck.requireUsable` now runs after the embedding call of each import path (an import, a rebuild, a PDF over 10 MB, a large text file): when more than half of a batch is unusable (zero, empty or not finite) the import stops with "The embedding model returned empty results for N of M passages of <file>, so they weren't added to search. Try importing it again."; fewer are logged and stored as before. Opening a library (`evaluateSemanticIndexHealth`) now samples 64 of its vectors and re-embeds it when nine in ten are empty, and counts each document's passages: a listed document that had passages and has none in the store is rebuilt by itself (`reembedDocuments(onlyDocumentIds:)`). The owner's library backup of 2026-09-10 held such a document, 455 passages with none in the store or the keyword index, so search couldn't find it at all. Each repair runs once a launch, and one that doesn't take leaves the rebuild banner up. Gate E reports nothing to ground against for empty vectors, as it does for none, instead of failing every answer. Rows https://app.notion.com/p/3f349a74d54f8134833acf73aac40048 and https://app.notion.com/p/3d749a74d54f812cb828d19ff84661e9; they close when an import on the iPhone sent to the background at once leaves no zero in `_norms.bin`, and a document missing its passages is found again after a library switch. `[evidence_level: test_verified, confidence: medium; evidence_source: LibraryVectorHealthTests; not run against a failing model on a device]`
- **[Retrieval]** **MMR compares the passages' vectors, so it diversifies.** `RAGEngine.applyMMR` measured how alike two passages are with the vectors on the passages it was given, and the vector store hands passages back without them (`BNNSVectorDatabase` keeps vectors in their own file). So every pair measured 0 alike and MMR kept passages in score order whatever λ said, in Standard, Deep Think and Maximum, in 5.6 and before, while Settings listed MMR Diversification. The two callers now fetch the candidates' vectors by id (`RAGService.storedVectors`, `getEmbeddings(forChunkIDs:)`) and pass them in. This changes which passages reach every answer: a passage that is nearly the same as one already picked gives way to a different one when their scores are close. Every figure in `BenchmarkRuns/LEDGER.md` was measured with MMR doing nothing, and a paired run on the Mac is the measure of the change. Each answer mode's own λ is still read only by logging. Row https://app.notion.com/p/3ea49a74d54f817ab4bcf5bb6ad07509. `[evidence_level: test_verified, confidence: high; evidence_source: MMRStoredVectorsTests; no benchmark run yet]`
- **[Retrieval]** **Lexical recall keeps the passages it matches.** `HybridSearchService.lexicalRecallCandidates`, the path a search takes when no library has keyword rows, skipped every passage whose vector was not the question's length, and passages come from the store without vectors, so it kept none. It now scores the passages it matches with `similarities(to:forChunkIDs:)` and keeps one the store has no comparable vector for with a similarity of 0. Row https://app.notion.com/p/3f849a74d54f81eba369ff0faa1ba190. `[evidence_level: build_verified, confidence: medium; evidence_source: HybridSearchService.swift; no test reaches this path]`
- **[Orchestration]** **A two-character code such as "E1" counts as a keyword when evidence lines are picked.** The line selection that builds Standard's evidence dropped every two-character word from the question and from each line, so in "What does E1 mean on the air fryer?" the word that names the answer never counted. `RAGService.isEvidenceKeyword` keeps a two-character word that mixes letters and digits; two-letter words and two-digit numbers are still dropped. This is the likely cause of the sample library's wrong E1 answer (row https://app.notion.com/p/3ec49a74d54f813baee7fd58e6484a32), not yet seen on a device. `[evidence_level: test_verified, confidence: medium; evidence_source: MMRStoredVectorsTests.testAShortCodeCountsAsAKeyword]`
- **[Indexing]** **Core ML runs on the CPU in the iOS Simulator, so a library made there has real vectors.** On the units the app asks for on a device, the Core ML copy of the embedding model handed back all-zero vectors in the Simulator: a library of the ten sample documents held 127 passages, every vector zero, and with the model limited to the CPU the same library held 2,375 passages with every norm 1.000 (both measured 2026-10-08). `DeviceCapabilityService.preferredComputeUnits` and `embeddingComputeUnitsDuringIngestion` return `.cpuOnly` there (`simulatorComputeUnits`). Without it the empty-vector check above would stop every import in the Simulator, as it did for two of `ShortcutsActionTests` in the first run with the check. A device is unchanged. `[evidence_level: test_verified, confidence: high; evidence_source: the full suite on the iOS 27 Simulator]`
- **[Orchestration]** **A claim whose passage says the opposite is no longer Supported.** `VerificationGateService.evaluateClaim` counted "Pets are allowed with a refundable deposit of four hundred dollars" as Supported by a lease that says pets are not allowed, because three of its nine words are in it (the fourth known gap of `ClaimCheckEvaluationTests`, found 2026-10-09). It now applies the rule "Check a Claim" uses (`ClaimCheck.negationDiffers`) to the passage's sentence that shares the most words with the claim, and reads such a claim as partly supported. The sample is an asserted one of the evaluation now. The flipped "not", the ordinal and the half-covered sentence stay known gaps. Row https://app.notion.com/p/3f449a74d54f812d9685f440b8fead30. `[evidence_level: test_verified, confidence: high; evidence_source: ClaimCheckEvaluationTests]`
- **[UI]** **An answer made only of excerpts reads Not Checked.** When no answer could be written, the excerpts answer carried the gating decision `reliability_fallback`, which `VerificationOutcome` reads as Partially Verified, though no model wrote it and no check ran. It now carries `excerpts_only`, which reads Not Checked and, like the other unverified states, keeps the App Store review prompt away (`ReviewPromptService`). A best-effort answer the model wrote and the gates checked keeps `reliability_fallback`. Deep Think and Maximum still run no verification gates of their own; that half of row https://app.notion.com/p/3e749a74d54f81b1ae8bc414159799ed stays open. `[evidence_level: test_verified, confidence: high; evidence_source: VerificationOutcomeTests]`
- **[Orchestration]** **On iOS and macOS 26, Settings says Private Cloud Compute needs 27 instead of that the build lacks it.** `FoundationModelCapabilityProvider.snapshot` returned "This build was compiled without Private Cloud Compute (requires Xcode 27)" both for a build without it and for one with it on a system older than 27. The second case now says "Private Cloud Compute needs iOS 27 or iPadOS 27" or "needs macOS 27". Row https://app.notion.com/p/3ea49a74d54f8126a005f0a27a318498; only a device on 26 shows it. `[evidence_level: build_verified, confidence: high; evidence_source: FoundationModelCapabilityProvider.swift]`
- **[Ingestion]** **Import progress names the step, not hardware nobody measured.** The PDF progress line printed "[Metal GPU]" whenever the GPU rendering setting was on, and the two Vision steps printed "[ANE]" as fixed text (`DocumentProcessor`). The lines now name the step only. Row https://app.notion.com/p/3cb49a74d54f818e9665e04b9f2b1a9b. `[evidence_level: build_verified, confidence: high; evidence_source: DocumentProcessor.swift]`
- **[UI]** **Seven screens use NavigationStack, so the Mac no longer draws them as a two-column split.** `NavigationView` stayed in the citation sheet (`GroundedAnswerView`), Response Details, Semantic Search, the Database Dashboard, the Chunk Inspector, Visualizations and the Processing Summary; previews keep theirs. Row https://app.notion.com/p/3ca49a74d54f81eb867cf24a119af0c1; closes when the six reachable ones are opened on the Mac. `[evidence_level: build_verified, confidence: medium; evidence_source: the seven files; not opened on a Mac]`
- **[Ingestion]** **A folder can be imported.** A folder dropped on the library, or chosen in the Mac's open panel, brings in the files inside it that the app imports, through its subfolders (`ImportedFileStaging.copyIntoWorkspaceExpandingFolders`, `importableFiles`). Hidden files and packages are skipped, and a folder brings in no more than the room left on the plan, and never more than 500 files. Through 5.6 a dropped folder was filtered out and refused, and the open panel couldn't choose one. The open panel's list of types moved to `ImportedFileStaging.importableTypes`, which the folder walk uses. The sheet (`WhatsNewStore`) now has fifteen items, among them dictation, Siri asking before Private Cloud Compute, the new actions, Spotlight and Visual Intelligence, the Mac menu bar and Services, Lock Screen progress, the widget and Handoff; the App Store notes for both platforms were rewritten to the same scope (3,332 characters for the Mac, 3,363 for iPhone and iPad), and the README's table and status say the same. `[evidence_level: code_verified, confidence: high; evidence_source: WhatsNewStore.swift; fastlane/metadata*; README.md]`
- **[UI]** **A Home Screen and Lock Screen widget asks, adds and scans.** `QuickActionsWidget`, in the extension that already holds the Live Activities and the controls, opens the app's own links (`openintelligence://chat`, `documents/add`, `chat/scan`) like the controls do: the small size and both Lock Screen sizes ask, the medium size has Ask, Add and Scan. - **[Ingestion]** **The Words figure counts words.** `DocumentProcessor` counted words by splitting at spaces only, so two words either side of a line break counted as one, and both large-file paths summed their passages' counts, which counted every overlap twice. Words are now split at any whitespace (`DocumentProcessor.wordCount(of:)`), a large text file counts each piece's own text, and a PDF over 10 MB counts each batch's text as it was read. Corrected 2026-10-15: an ordinary import still summed its passages for the import queue's Words and characters figures (`RAGService.addDocument`), so every import of a file under the large-file sizes still counted shared words twice; it now reads the document's own counts (`ProcessingMetadata.totalWords` and `totalCharacters`), and a streamed XML file's passages are counted at any whitespace too. The truncation half was checked on one file: a text file of 600 different words, ten to a line, counts 600 and keeps every word in its passages. Row https://app.notion.com/p/3d749a74d54f81aa94a6cb1831c2af20. `[evidence_level: test_verified (WordCountTests), confidence: medium; the queue's figure is read from the same metadata and was not seen in the app]`
- **[Chunking]** **A word that opens a sentence is no longer an entity.** The capitalized-word supplement in `SemanticChunker` runs on every chunk and kept "However", "Therefore", "First" and their like as entities; its stop list held 18 words and now holds the words that open sentences and are never names alone. Corrected 2026-10-15: "Should", which the row names, was still missing; the modal verbs, the pronouns and common sentence adverbs were added, and a test reads a chunk that opens with "However," and "Should" five times and gets neither as an entity, the names in it found, and the same entities each time. - **[Orchestration]** **Six model tools that nothing registered are gone.** `SearchDocumentsTool`, `ListDocumentsTool`, `GetDocumentSummaryTool`, `GetCorpusStatsTool`, `FindRelatedDocumentsTool` and `CompareDocumentsTool` were declared in `FoundationModelToolRegistry.swift` and returned by `createTools` nowhere, so no model ever called them (row https://app.notion.com/p/3b449a74d54f818486feee1dada5554b). The `RAGService` methods they called stay for the diagnostics screens. A missing tool handler now logs that the session gets no tools. `[evidence_level: build_verified, confidence: high; evidence_source: a grep of each name before removal found its declaration only]`
- **[UI]** **The MLX route nothing produced is gone.** `ChatExecutionLocation.mlxLocal` and its branches in `UnifiedMetricsBar` and `StatusPillV2` named a route that has had no runtime since local models were removed, and on 2026-10-06 the owner kept the app on Apple's models (row https://app.notion.com/p/3ab49a74d54f8138ad4dc38b3b895398). `ModelBackend.mlx` stays, because that type is decoded from saved data. `[evidence_level: build_verified, confidence: high; evidence_source: a grep for mlxLocal across the app]`
- **[UI]** **VoiceOver reads a library chip as one button, and the controls Apple's accessibility audit found too small are not.** The UI-testing target runs Apple's audit now (`AccessibilityAuditTests`, `performAccessibilityAudit`): the first-run screen when it shows, then Chat, Documents and Settings, for missing descriptions, small hit areas, wrong traits and text the audit cannot reach. Contrast, Dynamic Type and clipped text are left out until the owner has looked at what fixing them changes, and the audit for an action with no name exists on macOS only (`XCUIAccessibilityAuditTypes.h`, Xcode 27). Its first runs found five things, each fixed: the chat field's microphone, about 22 points square, is 44 points square, Apple's default control size on iPhone and iPad (Human Interface Guidelines, Accessibility, read 2026-10-15: default 44x44 pt, minimum 28x28 pt), and the paperclip beside it is the same, so both match the send button; "I'll add my own documents" on the first-run screen, about 20 points tall, has a 44-point tap area that reaches into the empty space around it, so the screen keeps its layout; a library chip (`ContainerPill`, an `HStack` with `.onTapGesture`) was two small elements with no button trait, its folder icon read "Move" (the symbol's own label), and it is one element now with the library's name, its document count and the button and selected traits; and the Document Quota's title row and its bar, about 20 and 4 points tall, are one element read as "Document Quota, 0 of 5 documents". The test closes an alert that is up before it audits, because an alert hides the screen under it from VoiceOver; through the first 5.7 builds the Documents screen showed one at every launch in the Simulator, which has no on-device model (the entry on "Error Processing Document" below). Not covered: using the app with VoiceOver on a device, which is what closes the roadmap row. `[evidence_level: test_verified (AccessibilityAuditTests on an iPhone 18 Pro Simulator, iOS 27.0), confidence: medium]`

- **[Ingestion]** **An import goes to the library it was queued for.** Every queue item records its library (`IngestionItem.containerId`), but `RAGService.addDocument` imported into the library that was active when the queue reached the item, so a file queued before the person switched libraries, "Add to Library" naming a library other than the open one (its result named the library it asked for), and a camera capture or picked file waiting behind a long import all landed in the open library. The ingestion loop now passes the item's library to `addDocument(containerId:)`, which uses it when it still exists and the active library otherwise; the large-file paths take the library from it, and the import summary names that library's embedding model. `[evidence_level: test_verified (ImportTargetLibraryTests: a real import of a note queued for a second library, which lands there while the open library stays open), confidence: high]`

- **[Retrieval]** **The passage retrieval ranks first reaches the model whole, however the question is worded.** On 2026-10-15 "What happened on September 30" in a library holding a monthly log (the owner's iPhone, 5.7 build of 67977e4) had the September 30 section ranked first by the reranker, and the answer said the documents did not cover the 30th. The extractive path then sorted the passages by the question's words, of which only "happened" counted ("September" is in over 40% of the passages and a number is not a keyword), and filled the compact context, which took sentences from two of the 37 passages, with sentences that repeat those words: the September 24 and 25 sections. The September 30 section's lines name people and events, so none of them was chosen. Now `RAGService.leadingWithTopRanked` keeps the reranked first passage at the front of the extractive ordering, and `extractRelevantSentences(leadingCandidateWhole:)` puts it in whole as [S1], at most three fifths of the budget and cut at a line or sentence end, before the chosen sentences from the other passages fill the rest; a line it already gives is not chosen again from a neighbouring passage. This is an invariant of the answer path, not a rule about one phrasing. `[evidence_level: test_verified (LeadingEvidenceTests, written passages shaped like that log), confidence: medium; not yet asked again on the device, and the retrieval benchmark has not been rerun]`

- **[Ingestion]** **An import saves its document before the model work that improves it, and in the background that work waits for the app.** After the passages were embedded and indexed, `RAGService.addDocument` called Apple's model for the RAPTOR-lite overview and the content tags, then saved the document, then called it again for suggested questions. On 2026-10-15 an import into a library on the owner's iPhone (the 67977e4 build) ran in the background through iOS's continued-processing task: embedding 50 passages took 19 s, the overview 9 s and the tags 11 s; the save followed the tags, and iOS ended the task 53 s after it started, during the suggested questions, so the Lock Screen said "Task failed" while the document was in, and the last step finished when the app came forward. Ended ten seconds earlier, during the overview or the tags, the import would have stopped before the save. Apple's documentation says the system ends first the tasks that show little or no progress (BGContinuedProcessingTask, read 2026-10-15), and the progress stood at 98 and 99 percent through those calls. The vector store is saved and the document recorded right after the keyword index now, and the three model calls run after it in `enrichImportedDocument`. While the app is in the background they are not run: the document goes on a list (`deferEnrichment`, kept in the app's settings) that `runDeferredEnrichmentsIfNeeded` works through, one document at a time, when the app comes to the front and when the import queue empties with the app in front. `[evidence_level: test_verified (DeferredEnrichmentTests for the list) and build_verified for the order, confidence: medium; not yet seen on a device]`

- **[Retrieval]** **Cached searches no question can reach are deleted, so a deleted library's text leaves the app's database.** `semantic_query_cache` keeps each search: the question, its embedding and the passages found. Its key names every library's documents with their passage counts and the app's version (`queryCacheKey`, 5.7), so after any import, deletion, re-import or update no older row can be found again, and nothing deleted a row: the questions and the passages, a deleted library's text among them, stayed until the app was removed, and the table grew with every change. After the documents are saved and at launch, `RAGService.deleteUnreachableCachedSearches` compares a digest of the documents and the app's version (`queryCacheDocumentsToken`) with the one recorded at the last deletion and, when they differ, deletes every row (`SQLiteFullTextService.deleteAllCachedQueries`, added at the owner's word on 2026-10-15; the table is unchanged). A change of a library's embedding model alone leaves that library's rows unreachable until the documents next change. The privacy policy says what 5.7 and earlier versions do. `[evidence_level: test_verified (QueryCacheClearingTests), confidence: high]`

Settings' Private Cloud Compute card, the Model Configuration route line, the model card, How It Works, a library's Private Cloud Compute card and the Settings capability row named only the first, and the published policy did too until this commit; How It Works also said the app always asks and that On-Device fails on a large question (it retries on the device with fewer, shorter passages, `usedOverflowRetry`). The "When to allow it" choice shown as "Allow for Deep Think and Maximum" allows every request without asking (`cloudConsentDecision` reads no mode), so it is shown as "Allow without asking"; the saved value is unchanged (`PCCSettings.displayName`). The consent prompt said "Your data is never stored" and "Same security as your device" in the app's voice: its rows now use Apple's words (security.apple.com/blog/private-cloud-compute, read 2026-10-15) under "As Apple describes Private Cloud Compute". The model picker's Privacy row said "End-to-end encrypted" for a model that runs on the device; a library's card called the permission "iCloud consent" and gave a fixed 4,096-token threshold; the model card's "Zero data retention" and "4,096 token context window" are gone (the window is the one the device reports, 8,192 on the owner's Mac); the App Store screenshot demo said data "never leaves Apple's secure enclaves". The card also said Standard goes to "3 sessions (12K)" when a question is complex (it chains sessions only when a developer turns on `forceReasoningChain`) and that Self-RAG 2.0 passes insights between sessions (it is Maximum's check of the answer against its sources). Settings' Private Cloud Compute header said "after you approve each request" and showed Available in the Simulator above a line saying it is unavailable there; it says "with your permission" and reads the live snapshot (`pccCapability`). It reads the plan and the document packs the app's store last saved (`EntitlementStore.savedDocumentAllowance`, built from `savedEntitlementSnapshot`, both added at the owner's word; the SDK target's stand-in in `EngineSDKCompatibility.swift` answers with the free plan, as it does everything; no price or limit changed). Document packs, discontinued, count only for people who bought one, as they do in the app. Found on 2026-10-15 when `ImportTargetLibraryTests` failed on the free limit: its teardown deleted the library and left the document, five runs left five, and the test's own Pro setting never reached a bare engine. The teardown removes the library's documents first now. `[evidence_level: test_verified (SavedEntitlementSnapshotTests, ImportTargetLibraryTests), confidence: high; not run as an action on a device]`
- **[Retrieval]** **An answer no longer cites a passage whose document is in no library, and Local Only libraries remove such passages at launch.** A passage whose document was deleted, or whose import stopped before the document was recorded, stayed in its library's vector store: `rebuildLocalSearchIndexesFromCanonicalState` wipes such passages but skips a library with no documents, and searches kept them, named "Unknown" in `queryInternal` and by `getDocumentName` in `searchDocumentsRaw`, which Deep Think, Maximum and the model's search tool use. On 2026-10-15 a trace from the owner's iPhone showed library BDB0BCA5 holding 27 passages and no document. Both searches leave such a passage out now (`passagesFromKnownDocuments`; a passage the pipeline made itself, a camera or barcode capture, keeps its own name and stays), and the launch check that removes an unfinished import's keyword text removes these passages from Local Only libraries (`removePassages`) under the same guards: not while an import is queued or running, and not when the document list read from disk is empty or unreadable. iCloud libraries are left alone: their documents can arrive after their passages, and a removal would reach the person's other devices. The index rebuild still skips a library with no documents, on purpose: it can run on an empty list after a failed read at launch, and wiping there would take real passages. `[evidence_level: test_verified (PassagesFromKnownDocumentsTests), confidence: high; not yet seen on the owner's iPhone]`

### Changed
- **[UI]** **The chat is plainer by default, and Technical Details brings its console back.** The owner, 2026-10-17, with a screenshot of an answer on its way: "add the ability to toggle technical/console instead of seeing the big blue/purple/yellow block ... I love it personally, but I'm sure it's too complicated feeling for the average user"; he chose that a new install starts simple and an existing one keeps its chat. `SettingsStore.showTechnicalDetails` (key `showTechnicalDetails`) is off on a new install and on for one that ran before, which has its model temperature saved (`persistAll` writes every setting at launch, and has in every version); it is read before anything is written that launch. The first version read the X-Ray's setting instead, and a refuting review found that `-showSiliconHUD NO` (which the accessibility UI tests pass) and the screenshot steps' write of that setting made a new install look like one that ran before; no launch argument or script sets the temperature. The chat screen puts it in the environment (`showsTechnicalDetails`, `ChatPresentation.swift`). Off: no stats bar under the header (`UnifiedMetricsBar`); while an answer is on its way, the bubble shows a spinner and what it is doing in words (`ChatProgress.phrase`: "Reading your question…", "Searching your documents…", "Checking what it found…" for the gate that runs before the answer is written, "Reading what it found…", "Looking through your documents…" for a Deep Think or Maximum tool, "Writing the answer…", "Checking against your documents…", "Thinking it through…"), in one bubble whose words change in place (the step console is made again at each step) instead of the last five steps by their technical names (`LivePipelinePreview`); under an answer no Deep Think or Maximum chip, tool count or reasoning steps; Fact Check's line says "Found in your documents · page 23 of Handpiece IFU.pdf" instead of "SUPPORTED • 96% Conf. • 1 Source", and the open card has no confidence bar; the header has no passage count. The route badge (on-device or Private Cloud Compute, spelled out in the plain chat where the console says "PCC", and for VoiceOver in both) and the verification badge stay either way. A Technical Details switch is in the chat's ⋯ menu, which is now open with an empty chat for it, and in Settings, Appearance; Settings' search finds it as "technical", "console" or "stats". Moved from `Future Backlog` (row "Chat shows every control at once to someone seeing the app for the first time"). `[evidence_level: test_verified (SettingsPersistenceTests: off on a new install, on for one that ran before (its temperature saved), a saved choice kept; ChatProgressTests: the words for each step, the gate's and a tool's among them, and no technical name in any of them; on 2026-10-17 at about 11:24, after the review's fixes: DEVICE BUILD SUCCEEDED, TEST BUILD SUCCEEDED, Executed 1168 tests, with 6 tests skipped and 0 failures (the second run on the same build, after the simulator failed to launch the test host), Mac BUILD SUCCEEDED, UI tests Executed 4 tests, with 0 failures); not yet seen on a device; confidence: medium until it is; evidence_source: SettingsStore.swift, ChatPresentation.swift, ChatScreen.swift, MessageListV2.swift, MessageBubbleV2.swift, GroundedAnswerView.swift, SettingsView.swift]`
- **[UI]** **The hardware X-Ray starts off on a new install.** The owner's word, 2026-10-16: on an iPhone running 5.6 the X-Ray floated over the suggestions, and in 5.7 its box and "Drag me" hint opened on top of Chat's mode pills, the first things a new person sees and none of what they came for. `SettingsStore` reads an unset `showSiliconHUD` as off (it read it as on). Anyone who has opened the app has the setting saved, because `persistAll` writes every setting at launch, so they keep what they had. It is the Telemetry HUD switch on Settings' Appearance page. Settings' search finds it as "X-Ray" and "Telemetry HUD" too. `[evidence_level: test_verified (SettingsPersistenceTests.testTheXRayStartsOffOnANewInstallAndASavedSettingIsKept: a new install reads it as off and a saved setting is kept), code_verified (that an existing user has it saved: `persistAll` writes every setting when the store's publishers first emit, SettingsStore.swift), confidence: high]`
- **[Indexing]** **Opening a library no longer analyses every passage in it, and the analysis is not made again while the library is unchanged.** `LibraryIntelligenceCenter.analyzeLibrary` (`AdaptiveEmbeddingOptimizer.swift`) joins all the text it is given into one string, runs a language recogniser over it, tokenises every word into an array and a set, counts characters twice, runs a sentence tokeniser over all of it, and from those measures recommends chunking and embedding settings. Three screens show what it finds (the Database card, the Pipeline Audit and Library Settings), and on a library that tunes itself the chunking it recommends is applied; nothing else reads it. `RAGService.generateIntelligenceSnapshot` gave it every passage of the library, and ran when the app loaded its documents at launch (`applyLoadedDocuments`), after each import of a file read whole, after each delete, and when a screen asked for a fresh one (`force: true` in the Pipeline Audit, the Database card and Library Settings). Nothing bounded it. Measured on the Mac on 2026-10-13 (M3 Pro, simulator test process, unoptimised build, the 144,247-passage benchmark store, 143.8 MB of passage text, `LibraryAnalysisScaleBenchmarkTests`, new): 11.4 s for 6,000 passages, 43.4 s for 24,000, and 199.1 s for all 144,247, with the process 1,054 MB over what it held when the analysis started. On the owner's library that is what ran, off the main thread, each time the app opened with that library active. It was found on 2026-10-13 by timing it, after the owner answered the list of what the storage change had left open with a go-ahead for all of it. Seven changes. (1) A library past 4,000 passages (`analysisPassageLimit`) is analysed from at most that many: up to a quarter are the first passages of its documents, in store order (a document's preview is its first passage), and the rest an even sample (`LibraryIntelligenceCenter.samplePositions`). They are picked from the store's records and read by id, so the rest of the library's text is never read. The report keeps the library's real passage counts (`LibrarySize`), says how many passages it was measured from (`CorpusSignals.analysedChunkCount`, `isEstimate`), and scales the word totals up to the library's size. (2) A report made from a sample never asks for another embedding model (`RAGService.resolveAutoAdjustments`). Its measures are not the library's (vocabulary reads richer in a sample, 0.0248 against 0.0073 on the benchmark store, and whether code or math is found depends on which passages were drawn), and another model means embedding every document again. The chunking settings follow a sampled report as they follow any report: they are used the next time something is cut into passages, they are not part of a library's embedding fingerprint when the app sets them (`EmbeddingFingerprint.chunkerRecipe`), and each import sets them again from the file it reads (the pre-scan in `addDocument`). As of this entry nothing applies an embedding change on its own for any report, sampled or not: the one place that would (`scheduleSelfTuningRebuild`) is behind `IngestionContext.allowsSelfTuningScheduling`, which is false for every kind of import, so the rule guards a path that is switched off, and the two places that would queue the rebuild (the import path, and `handleSelfTuning`, which nothing calls) each refuse a sampled report by themselves, because they queue it for any reason, the chunking included. The version of this change that went to its second review stopped a sample from changing anything, on the ground that an import seeing a reason would queue a rebuild of the library. The review showed that no import can, and that a large library would then have kept whatever the last import's pre-scan had set, so the chunking half was put back. (3) The report is kept on disk, in the app's local cache folder beside the keyword index, under a name that says what it was made from: the library, a hash of its documents (each one's id, file hash, passage count, date and name) and the store's passage count (`LibraryIntelligenceCache`). While the library is what the name says, a launch reads the kept report and no passages. The vocabulary the first question builds (`CorpusVocabulary.build`, 2.7 s on that store) is kept the same way. (4) Both files hold text from the documents (a 160-character preview of each, up to 300 snippets of 200 characters), so they are removed with what they came from and not only replaced: for one library when its documents are no longer the ones a file was made from (`removeStale`, on every refresh), when it is deleted (`remove`), and for every library each time the documents are loaded (`sweep`, in `applyLoadedDocuments`), which is where a document or a library removed on another device arrives and where a library whose last document is gone is seen. A vocabulary is kept only when it was built from every passage in the library's store and each of those belongs to a document the library lists (`RAGService.vocabularyMayBeKept`). A store can hold passages of a document that is not listed yet (one being imported in pieces, whose passages are stored as each piece finishes) or no longer (one removed on another device and not cleared here yet), and a file holding their words would be named by nothing that removes files. The files are read and written off the main actor, so the stale check runs once more after each write, and a kept report is checked against the library once more after it is read. They are written in place and not through a temporary file: an atomic write keeps its temporary file beside the destination under a name the removal does not know (`<name>.sb-...` on macOS 27, seen by listing the folder during a write), and a write cut short only leaves a file that does not decode, which reads as nothing kept. Nothing is removed before the documents have been loaded, when every library's list is still empty. A report whose library changed while it was being made is not kept, and a refresh for a library that no longer exists analyses nothing (it used to analyse the active library under the deleted one's id). A report held in memory is now replaced when the library's documents change; it used to stand until a forced refresh, so a library changed by sync kept showing the report of what it had been. (5) An import in pieces (a large text, JSON Lines or PDF file) now ends with the same refresh as any other import. Through 5.6 it ended without one, so the analysis and the vocabulary held for the library stayed those of the library before the document until the app was opened again; with files kept on disk they would have stayed there as long. (6) `RAGService` is isolated to the main actor by the module's default (`-default-isolation=MainActor`), and three pieces of this work ran there as plain calls. The vocabulary build is the large one: a part-of-speech tagger over up to 4,000 passages, 2.6 to 2.7 s on the benchmark store, in the middle of the first question after each launch. It now runs on the concurrent pool (`libraryVocabulary`, one function for both question paths; Deep Think and Maximum built their own copy and it was never kept). That function also decides what is held in memory for later questions: only a vocabulary of the whole library, and only when the library's documents are still the ones it was made from at the end of the build, checked and stored in one step on the main actor so that a refresh cannot empty the cache in between. Since 2026-10-09, when a question could first be limited to named documents, a Deep Think or Maximum question limited that way built a vocabulary from those documents alone and left it as the library's for every question after it until the next refresh; no version shipped with that. Picking the analysis's sample walks every record of the library and is off the main actor too (`libraryAnalysisInput`), as are reading and writing the kept files. (7) A document the sample did not reach is shown with its name and its real passage count and no preview; an empty list of passages used to mean "Awaiting chunk extraction.", which is still what a document with no passages says. The same analysis is also written into the import path, before a document is embedded, behind the switch named in (2), so it does not run in any build so far. It read every existing passage with the new document's; it was changed in step and shares the limit between the two by their sizes, so that switching it on would not bring back a read of every passage. After: on the same store, opened fresh so that nothing was decoded, picking the sample from the records, reading its 3,999 passages by id and analysing them took 6.1 to 6.5 s in four runs, the last on the tree as committed, and added 0 to 4 MB (an earlier version of the sampler, which read 4,001 passages, took 10.3 s and added 7 MB in one run), and recommended the same plan as the full analysis (Core ML, 384, confidence 0.28). With the report kept, a launch does none of it. The three screens that show the analysis (the audit's Corpus Signals card, Library Settings, the Database card) say "Measured from" when it is a sample, and the Database card's note about what analysing a library costs is corrected. `[evidence_level: test_verified (LibraryAnalysisSampleTests, 14 tests: the sample and its bound, the counts, the scaled word totals, what a sample may change, a document the sample missed, the signature, both kept files, when a vocabulary may be kept, and removal by stale check, by sweep and by library) and measured on the Mac as stated; the full suite, 892 tests with 0 failures and 5 skipped, run 2026-10-13 at 11:52 PT after a device build and before a Mac build; code_verified for everything inside `RAGService` (where the removal is called from, the refresh after an import in pieces, which actor each piece runs on, what the vocabulary function holds in memory): no test builds that service; not run on a device, and the time on the owner's iPhone before or after is not known, confidence: high for what the code reads and removes, medium that a sample's measures stand in for the full analysis's on libraries other than the benchmark store, evidence_source: AdaptiveEmbeddingOptimizer.swift, RAGService.swift refreshIntelligence, removeStaleKeptAnalysis, generateIntelligenceSnapshot, libraryAnalysisInput, libraryVocabulary, resolveAutoAdjustments, applyLoadedDocuments, addDocument; ContainerService.swift; ContentView.swift; QueryEnhancementService.swift; BenchmarkRuns/2026-10-13-launch-analysis (local)]`
- **[Indexing]** **The storage audit no longer reports every passage as a dimension mismatch.** `RAGService.runVectorAudit` counted the passages whose own vector was not the length the library expects. The vector store hands passages back without their vectors, so every passage of every library was counted, and the Pipeline Audit screen's "Mismatched dims" showed the library's passage count. A store keeps vectors of one length, its own: the audit now compares that with the library's setting, and still compares a passage's own vector where a store hands one back (`vectorAuditFigures`). The app opens a store at the length its library is set to, so for a store that opened the row reads 0, which is now true. - **[Indexing]** **The vector store opens a library by indexing its passage file, and decodes a passage when it is asked for.** Built 2026-10-12 at the owner's word ("ok now do the storage change"). His words name the change and not a file: the change is the plan of the 2026-10-10 decision record, which is a change to `BNNSVectorDatabase.swift` with the three files and their format left as they are, and the go-ahead was read as covering that file for that plan. Through 5.6 a load read `_meta.json` whole and decoded every passage (`JSONDecoder().decode([DocumentChunk].self)`), and the store held every passage's text from then on: for the owner's 144,247-passage library that was 159 MB of JSON and 3.0 to 3.2 s on an iPhone 16 Pro Max when it was first measured on 2026-10-10, and 2,189 to 2,526 ms in the six loads of that library his phone's log held on 2026-10-12, at every launch and every switch to it. A load now memory-maps the file and walks its bytes once (`PassageFileIndex.scan`, new file), keeping one 72-byte record for each passage: its id, its document, its number, page and level, and the byte range of its JSON object. The walk knows strings, nesting and whole numbers and nothing else of JSON. An object that is not written the way the encoder writes it (a key with an escape in it, one of the keys the walk reads appearing twice, an id that is not a plain UUID string, a number with a fraction) is read by `JSONDecoder` instead, and a file the walk cannot follow (brackets that do not match, a value nested more than 64 deep, a member whose value is a bare word other than a number, true, false or null) is decoded whole as before. A large file is walked in several stretches at once: the stretch starts are guesses (the bytes `},{"`, which the encoder writes between two passages and which also turn up where a passage's text ends in `},{`), each stretch has to end exactly on the next one's start, and one miss sends the file back to a single walk. A passage is decoded from its own bytes when something asks for it, and the decoded passage must carry the id its record names. A search decodes only the passages that came out on top. The first caller that asks for every passage gets them decoded on all but two processors, after which the store holds them, as it did from load before, until the system reports memory pressure: then it lets them go (`releaseDecodedPassages`) and decodes them again for the next caller. Saving still writes each file whole to a temporary file and moves it into place, vectors first, so what a cut-off save leaves is what it left before. What changed is where the bytes come from: a passage already in the file is copied byte for byte and only new passages are encoded, and the vectors are streamed from the mapping (through 5.6 a save encoded every passage again and gathered every vector in memory, 221 MB for that library). A delete reads which passages stay from the records and writes neighbouring vectors as one run. The store protocol gains `passageRecords()`, `readableChunks()`, `chunks(forDocument:limit:)`, `chunkCount(forDocument:)` and `chunks(withIDs:)`, with defaults for the other stores, and ten callers that asked for every passage and used a few now ask for what they use: the import queue's recovery check, the Atlas sample, the suggested-question pool, the count, the rebuild and the question sample of an import in pieces, the question sample of a streamed PDF, re-embedding one document, and in hybrid search the scoped search and the keyword-hit lookup when no passages are handed in. A caller that picks passages from the records reads them by id, so a passage added or removed between the picking and the reading cannot change which passages come back. The two selections with logic of their own, the suggested-question pool and the keyword-hit lookup, are held to what they selected before by tests (`VectorStorePassageIndexTests`). The other moved callers are single reads of one document's passages or of the records, checked by reading and without a test of their own. Two faults that only a store with no files had are fixed on the way: a delete emptied its vectors, and an update did not change the vector. Measured on the Mac (M3 Pro, simulator test process, unoptimised build, 144,247 synthetic passages, 216.5 MB of metadata and 211.3 MB of vectors, `VectorStoreScaleBenchmarkTests`), the 5.6 code and then this code as committed: load to first count 1.97 s and 0.68 to 0.73 s (four runs; a fifth, taken just after the Mac woke from sleep with its search reading 0.24 s where 0.14 s is usual, read 1.31 s); memory held after the load 276 MB and 10 to 12 MB; every passage decoded, when first asked for, 0.43 and 0.45 s in the two quiet runs; a save at full size 1.75 s and 0.20 to 0.51 s; the 49 saves of building that library 60.8 s and 15.5 and 16.3 s in two builds; deleting a 3,500-passage document 2.31 s and 0.27 to 0.52 s; a search 0.133 s and 0.14 s. The walk alone, optimised and outside the test process, took 0.018 s on eleven threads and 0.117 s on one. The files the 5.6 code wrote load in the new code, and the files the new code writes decode whole the way 5.6 reads them. The walk as committed was also run on the Mac over copies of the nine libraries' metadata files from the owner's iPhone, written by builds from 2026-09-14 to 2026-10-10 (145,500 passages, 144,247 of them in one 159,339,521-byte file; copied on 2026-10-13 and deleted afterwards): it found exactly the passages the decoder found, with the same id, document, number, page and level for every one, every passage's byte range decoded to that passage, and the walk in stretches matched the single walk. That file took 0.015 s on eleven threads optimised and 0.17 s unoptimised (0.10 s and 0.98 s on one thread). **What this does not change.** Eleven callers still read every passage's text. Ten do it through `readableChunks()`: the question path (`answerQuestion` hands the array to the summary, enumeration, parent-expansion, cross-reference, extractive-scan and multi-hop steps; the two agentic entry points and the scan itself fetch it too), the library analysis that runs at launch and after an import (`refreshIntelligence`, which joins all the text to measure vocabulary), the pre-import analysis, the audit screen, the search-index rebuild and hybrid search's lexical recall. The sync merge does it through `allChunks()`. So memory is what it was once the analysis has run or a question has been asked: with every passage decoded the store measured 333 to 345 MB, and a plain decode of the same file in the same process 328 MB. Moving those steps off the array is retrieval work (they would have to ask the keyword index instead of scanning memory) and is not started. `[evidence_level: test_verified (22 tests in VectorStorePassageIndexTests and 10 in BNNSVectorDatabasePersistenceTests; the full suite, 864 tests with 0 failures and 4 skipped, run 2026-10-13 at 06:29 PT on the tree as committed, with a device build and a Mac build) and measured on the Mac as stated (2026-10-12 and 2026-10-13); not run on a device: the load time and the memory on the owner's iPhone are not known, confidence: high for the format and for the two selections held by tests, medium for the moved callers that have no test and for the device figures, evidence_source: BNNSVectorDatabase.swift, PassageFileIndex.swift, VectorDatabase.swift, RAGService.swift, RAGService+TextStreaming.swift, RAGService+Streaming.swift, HybridSearchService.swift; the benchmark's printed lines]`
- **[Indexing]** **The storage change was reviewed before it was pushed, and the review changed it.** The first version of the entry above was committed locally (`1fdf69e`) and handed to a second agent told to refute it. Nothing was pushed or put on a device until its findings were dealt with. (1) *A delete ended part-way could pair passages with the wrong vectors.* A load reads a vectors file longer than the metadata lists as a save that was cut off, and keeps the first vectors. A delete takes vectors out of the middle, so a delete ended between its two files while passages were still unsaved could leave such a file with the vectors in another order, and the next launch would have loaded every passage with a neighbour's vector and no sign of it. This came in with the 2026-10-09 change that taught the loader to accept the longer file, and was carried into the rewrite. `deleteChunks` and `updateChunk` now write out anything unsaved first, so a cut-off delete always leaves fewer vectors than passages, which loads the store empty. (2) *A load wrote and removed files.* It cut surplus vectors off the file and, on a mismatch it could not explain, removed all three files. Sync opens its own store on a library's files and could do either while the app's store was saving. A load now only reads: surplus vectors are ignored in memory and the next save writes the file at its right length, and a store that loads empty leaves the files where they are. (3) *A metadata write that failed after a delete was never retried,* so the next launch found fewer vectors than passages. The store now stays marked unsaved and `persist()` writes it. (4) *The first version wrote every save to one fixed temporary name beside the library and did not synchronise it to disk.* A second store's save could write into the same temporary file, and iCloud Drive could see it. Each file is now written under a unique name in the system's replacement directory for the store's volume, synchronised, and renamed over the old one. (5) *One passage that failed to decode shortened `allChunks()`,* and sync pairs that array with the vectors by position. Through 5.6 such a passage failed the decode of the whole file and the library opened empty. Now it is counted and left out: of searches, of reads of a document or by id, and of `readableChunks()`, which the ten readers of text call. `allChunks()` throws, and sync is its one caller in the app. Deleting the document the passage belongs to removes it. (6) *Passages picked by position could be other passages by the time they were read.* The Atlas sample and the suggested-question pool now read by id, and `chunks(atIndices:)`, added in the first version, is gone. (7) *The walk was laxer than this file said.* A key the walk reads could be spelled with an escape, or appear twice, and be read differently from the way the decoder reads it, and a skipped value was not checked at all. The walk now hands those objects to the decoder and refuses a file whose skipped values are not JSON as far as it looks. The first form of that check made the walk a third slower in an unoptimised build and was rewritten until it cost about a twentieth (1.05 s, 1.41 s and 1.11 s on one thread over the 216 MB file). Apple's `JSONDecoder` accepts four things that are not JSON numbers (`01`, `1.`, `1e`, `-`) under a key nothing decodes (measured on macOS 27.0 and in the iOS 27.0 Simulator; `JSONSerialization` refuses all four). The walk refuses them, and a refused file is decoded whole. A hook that exists only in debug builds (`interruptMetadataWrites`) ends a save before its metadata write, so the cut-off cases are tested: a save and a delete ended there, the same two when the app goes on running, and a delete whose first save fails. `[evidence_level: test_verified (BNNSVectorDatabasePersistenceTests, 10 tests, 6 of them new; VectorStorePassageIndexTests, 22 tests, 4 of them new), confidence: high for the cases the tests reproduce, medium for two stores open on one library's files at once, which no test reproduces, evidence_source: BNNSVectorDatabase.swift deleteChunks, updateChunk, loadBinary, replaceFile, everyChunkIfWhole, readableChunks; PassageFileIndex.swift; NSFileManager.h in the iOS 27 SDK, which says only the volume of the URL passed for the replacement directory is used]`

While it is set: the embedding loop waits 120 ms between batches of 16, the reasoning chain and the Maximum loop wait one second between sessions, and the continued-processing policy does not ask for the background GPU. Nothing is started or cut because the flag changed, which is what Apple's header asks for, and the number of sessions is not reduced. It needs its own field, which the stored message does not have. (2) *High-fidelity question translation* (row 3f449a74d54f813cb8c4fe67e7122b46): a `TranslationSession` made without a strategy already reports `.highFidelity` on this Mac in a binary built with the 27 SDK, and Apple's page says `.lowLatency` is the default only without Apple Intelligence or with an SDK before 26.4. 5.6 was built with that SDK, so the behaviour the row asks for is the current one; asking for `.lowLatency` on this Mac failed with "Unable to Translate" because its language pack is not installed.
