### Fixed
- **[UI]** **Inline citations open their passage.** `GroundedAnswerView` linked only a bare `[3]` (`\[(\d+)\]`), and the answer prompt asks for `[S1]`, so no citation in a real answer could be tapped (roadmap row 3ec49a74d54f81d6980ad6deb0dc7744, seen on 5.5 build 483). `CitationLinker` now links `[S2]`, `[S2, S5]`, `[S1: name]` and `[3]`, and puts the chunk's id in the link instead of an index. It has to, because an answer can number its sources two ways: text rendered from claims counts positions in `StructuredAnswer.evidence` (`citationLabels`), and text kept as the model wrote it counts retrieved chunks. When the two disagree, the passage that shares more words with the cited sentence wins. A citation link that resolves to nothing is swallowed instead of handed to the system, which logged "Failed to open URL". `[evidence_level: test_verified for the link forms and for the tie-break as written, confidence: high for the link forms, medium that the tie-break picks the right passage in a real answer; evidence_source: StructuredAnswer.swift citationLabels and citedEvidence read 2026-10-07; CitationLinkerTests, 8 tests passed on the iOS 27.0 simulator 2026-10-07]` Not yet tapped on a device.
- **[Orchestration]** **Answer cleanup no longer rewrites numbers and times.** Three passes in `LLMService` rebuilt answers from pieces split at every period: `deduplicateProseBlock` matched `[^.!?\n]+[.!?]?` and joined every match with a space, repeats or not, so every prose answer had "$300.00" rewritten as "$300. 00"; `collapseFuzzySimilarSentences` split on `.!?`, dropped every piece under 15 characters and rejoined with ". ", which is how "10:00 p.m." became "10:00 p."; `collapseRepetitiveTemplates` did the same on a merge (row 3ec49a74d54f81c99348dbe44263dbd9). `AnswerSentenceSplitter` ends a sentence only where whitespace follows its closing punctuation and keeps each sentence's own text, so the pieces join back with one space to the same answer, and all three passes now return the text untouched unless they actually remove something. `[evidence_level: test_verified for the splitter, code_verified for the three functions that call it, confidence: high; evidence_source: the three functions read 2026-10-07; AnswerSentenceSplitterTests, 9 tests passed on the iOS 27.0 simulator 2026-10-07]` The suite's first run failed two of those tests, and both were right about the splitter: a single letter before a period was always read as an initial, so "It holds 4.5 L. See Dr. Smith" stayed one sentence, and the round-trip check assumed a line break could never sit inside a sentence, which it does after "p.m.". A single letter after a number is now a unit that can end a sentence, and the check now states what holds: every sentence is a run of the original text, unchanged, with only whitespace between one and the next. No test calls the three cleanup functions themselves; they are private to `LLMService` and run only on a model's output. The row closes on a device answer quoting $300.00, 6:50 p.m. and Section 16.
- **[Orchestration]** **Sentences that differ in a number or a code are never treated as repeats.** The near-duplicate pass compares word sets and ignores words of two characters or fewer, so "E1 indicates a temperature sensor open circuit" and "E2 indicates a temperature sensor short circuit" score 4 of 6 shared words, over its 0.65 threshold, and one was dropped. Both that pass and the template merge now skip sentences whose digit-bearing words differ (`AnswerSentenceSplitter.identifierTokens`). This is a likely contributor to the wrong E1 answer in row 3ec49a74d54f813baee7fd58e6484a32 and is not shown to be its cause: the three wrong sample answers in that row have not been re-asked. `[evidence_level: code_verified for the mechanism, unverified for the row; confidence: medium]`
- **[Orchestration]** **The Evidence-First prompt no longer ends with an outline the model prints.** Its last line read "End with: What sources show → What's missing → Confidence note.", and Apple's on-device model wrote that line into answers, with source ids the reader cannot map (row 3ec49a74d54f81d2a20afe103ad55419). The line now asks for one plain sentence about what the excerpts do not cover, with no source ids, heading, summary or confidence note. `[evidence_level: code_verified, confidence: high that the outline is gone from the prompt, unverified for what the model writes instead; evidence_source: RAGService.swift, the only occurrence in the tree]`
- **[UI]** **Fact Check rows and streaming answers show no markdown markers.** `ClaimCard` and the response details drew a claim with `Text(claim.claim)`, which shows `**350°F**` as typed; both now parse inline markdown (row 3ec49a74d54f816c8acfcd0dcbb9c486). The streaming bubble stays plain `Text`, as its own note explains, and filters header marks, `**` and backticks in one pass (`StreamingMarkdown`), holding back a marker that has only half arrived (row 3ec49a74d54f81958ae8f84b2db284e1). `[evidence_level: test_verified for the streaming filter, code_verified for the two claim views, confidence: high; evidence_source: GroundedAnswerView.swift, ResponseDetailsView.swift, MessageListV2.swift; StreamingMarkdownTests, 3 tests passed on the iOS 27.0 simulator 2026-10-07]`
- **[UI]** **The Verified badge shows only for a check that ran and passed.** The bubble and the response details each mapped a short list of failure words and called everything else Verified, so `unverified:<gates>`, `verification_skipped` and `source_only_abstained` read Verified, and the details sheet read "Verified Response: Answer passed all quality checks" when there was no status at all, which is every Deep Think and Maximum answer whose source-only check did not run (row 3e749a74d54f81b1ae8bc414159799ed; `verificationService.verify` is called at two places, both on Standard's path, re-checked 2026-10-07). Both surfaces now read one classifier, `VerificationOutcome`: `verified:` or `source_only_refined` is Verified, a failed gate or an abstaining source-only check is Unverified, and anything else is a new state, Not Checked. The Fact Check header counts supported claims ("2 of 3 Verified", "3 Not Checked") instead of printing the claim count beside "Verified", and a claim with no verdict reads NOT CHECKED, not VERIFYING. This is the label half of the row. Its other half, running answer-type and entailment checks in every mode, is not in 5.6. `[evidence_level: test_verified for the classifier, code_verified for the two surfaces that read it, confidence: high; evidence_source: MessageBubbleV2.swift, ResponseDetailsView.swift, RAGService.swift gatingSummary; VerificationOutcomeTests, 4 tests passed on the iOS 27.0 simulator 2026-10-07]`
- **[Orchestration]** **Gate E no longer fails an answer whose every claim the claim check supported.** Gate E passes when the answer's best chunk similarity is at least 80% of the question's. On 2026-10-02 it failed nine of nine right, cited answers to two sample questions, seven of which Fact Check supported in full (row 3ed49a74d54f8186b358d123b6f22d8b). Which of its three conditions failed was not logged; reading the anchor-term code against both questions leaves the ratio as the likely one. From 5.6, when the ratio is the only condition that failed and Gate B supported every claim, the gate passes with the detail "Grounded by claims". The absolute floor and the topical check still fail it alone. With the badge change above this matters more than it did: those answers would otherwise have gone from a wrong Verified to a wrong Unverified. `[evidence_level: test_verified for the rule, inferred for the cause; confidence: medium; evidence_source: VerificationGateService.runGateE; SemanticGroundingClaimTests, 3 tests passed on the iOS 27.0 simulator 2026-10-07]` Closes on the two sample questions showing no Gate E line, on a device or the Simulator.
- **[Orchestration]** **"Just Once" consent for Private Cloud Compute covers one question in Deep Think and Maximum.** The transient grant was cleared at the start of a Standard question only; Deep Think and Maximum return from `queryInternal` before that line, so after Just Once the next agentic question was sent without the sheet (row 3ea49a74d54f81fd814ae82ce9449e41). The grant is now cleared before `executeAgenticQuery` as well. One question still makes several model calls under one grant. `[evidence_level: code_verified, confidence: high; evidence_source: RAGService.swift queryInternal and ensureCloudConsentIfNeeded read 2026-10-07]` No test reaches this path. Closes on iOS or macOS 27: Just Once, then a second Deep Think question that routes to Private Cloud Compute shows the sheet again.
- **[UI]** **A device that cannot run Apple Intelligence is told so.** Chat answered every unavailable-model error with "Apple Intelligence isn't available. Enable it in Settings.", and Settings and the model chip said "Preparing AI Models..." for every reason. `DeviceCapabilities` now carries Apple's reason as a value (`AppleIntelligenceUnavailability`: device not eligible, not enabled, model not ready), and `AppleIntelligenceCopy` gives chat, Settings, the chip and a new notice on an empty chat one wording each. The notice says what still works: documents are read, indexed and searched, and Standard questions come back as excerpts (row 3e449a74d54f8170a3cbfb570a41d0a5 observed that on the Simulator on 2026-09-23). `[evidence_level: test_verified for the wording each reason gets, code_verified for where each surface reads it, unverified on hardware without Apple Intelligence; confidence: high for the wording; evidence_source: ChatScreen.swift userFacingErrorMessage, SettingsView.swift statusText, RAGService.checkDeviceCapabilities; AppleIntelligenceCopyTests, 3 tests passed on the iOS 27.0 simulator 2026-10-07]`

- **[UI]** **The hardware legend first appears after the welcome screen.** At the owner's word the legend and its "Drag me" hint are kept and only where they first show up changes: `FloatingLegendWindowManager` hides its window while the welcome screen is up, driven from `ContentView` (row 3df49a74d54f81e3a350ff1d1d34af2b). iPhone and iPad only; the Mac draws the legend inside its window and is unchanged. `[evidence_level: code_verified, confidence: high; evidence_source: MotherboardHUDView.swift, ContentView.swift]` Closes on a first launch on a device or the Simulator.
- **[Infrastructure]** **A healthy library is no longer told its search index needs rebuilding.** The owner imported a document, asked one question, and the Documents tab said "This library needs its search index rebuilt"; after Rebuild it said so again (row 3f249a74d54f81069664e9431e2e687e). His iPhone's `pipeline_trace.log` gave the fingerprints, and recomputing them from the bundled tokenizer names the causes. All are in code unchanged since 5.5. **(1) A new library's fingerprint was recorded from the wrong embedding service.** `stampEmbeddingFingerprintIfAbsent` read the app's default service, which is Core ML, while `resolveEmbeddingContext` compares against the library's own, which on iOS 27 is Core AI. The two identities can never be equal, so every new Core AI library was flagged on its first question. On the 5.6 development build the log's recorded value `69f06c6c23035978` is exactly the Core ML fingerprint and its live value `3236a5aa2cfabb78` exactly the Core AI one. The stamp and the shareable index snapshot now use the library's own service (`fingerprintService(for:)`). **(2) The chunker term held the app's own tuning.** On the 5.5 build the same log's values are Core ML with `balanced/300` recorded and Core AI with `densePrecision/320` live: the self-tuner had changed the strategy between them and logged "No rebuild required". `EmbeddingFingerprint.chunkerRecipe` now counts only a chunk size the person chose. **(3) A stored fingerprint was gone after `ContainerService.reloadFromDisk`**, which the Documents tab calls before every workspace reload (recorded 16:08:32, reloaded 16:10:02, flagged 16:11:32; and a rebuild that completed at 16:06:14 was flagged again at 16:06:52). Each save of the library list ran in its own detached task with nothing ordering them, so an older list could land after a newer one; saves now go through one serial queue, and the fingerprint is also kept in a device-local record (`UserDefaults`, with the provider and dimension it was recorded under) and restored when a list read from disk lacks it. A fingerprint stored through 5.5 that equals what the old stamp or the old chunker term would have produced is re-recorded without a flag. `WorkspaceSyncService.swift` was not edited; its `mergeContainer` still does not carry the fingerprint, and the local record is what covers that. The first version of this fix, built to the owner's iPhone the same day, had causes 2 and 3 only, and the phone flagged a new library again 38 seconds after import; cause 1 was found from that log. `[evidence_level: measured for causes 1 and 2 (all four fingerprint values in the owner's log reproduced by hashing the provider, the pooling, the model revision, the chunker term and the bundled tokenizer, 2026-10-07), measured for the loss in cause 3 and inferred that unordered saves are what lost it, test_verified for the chunker term, the two identities and the record (RebuildFalseAlarmTests, in the full run recorded in Docs/ai/STATE.md), unverified on a device for the corrected fix; confidence: high for causes 1 and 2, medium for cause 3; evidence_source: RAGService.swift stampEmbeddingFingerprintIfAbsent and resolveEmbeddingContext, ContainerService.swift, EmbeddingFingerprint.swift]` Closes on a device: import into a new library and into an existing one, ask a question in each, open Documents, and no rebuild banner shows; rebuild a library that predates the fingerprint, reopen Documents, ask again, and the banner does not return.

### Changed
- **[Orchestration]** **A Deep Think chain session reads its chunks whole when they fit the live window.** Through 5.5 a chain session's evidence was sized in characters (3,500, cut to 2,200 once there were earlier findings) and went through `extractRelevantSentences` first, which keeps a sentence only when it, its heading or the line before it matches a keyword of the question. Those constants assume 1.4 characters a token, and prose measures 4.2 to 5.5, so a session carried roughly 500 tokens of a 4,096 or 8,192 window (row 3f249a74d54f815cbbd3c2e1c492dfd7). From 5.6, when the SDK can count tokens (iOS and macOS 26.4 on), `SessionEvidencePlan` sizes each window of `executeReasoningChain` in tokens against `SystemLanguageModel.contextSize`. The budget leaves room for the answer (700), the question, the earlier findings and the prompt's own wording, and stops at 3,200 tokens of evidence whatever the window: for a 100-character question that is 2,700 for the first session on a 4,096 window and 2,020 for later ones, and 3,200 on an 8,192 window. A 4,096 window keeps four chunks a window; an 8,192 window reads up to seven, and never so many that a pool the old path split into two sessions becomes one, because one session's notes would skip the synthesis pass. A window is read whole only when it holds at least as many chunks as the character-sized window would, so the chain never plans more windows than before; how many sessions then run still depends on the chain's own early exits. These keep the character-sized path: an OS that cannot count, a window that does not fit, any mode other than Deep Think and Maximum's chain, and sessions that may run on Private Cloud Compute (the picker set to PCC), so this change sends no more text off the device. A whole-chunk session that overflows is rebuilt from sentence extraction over the same chunks before it is cut. **Maximum's own loop is unchanged:** it skips a window that has no matching sentence, and whole chunks would have made it generate for every window. Counting costs one SDK call a chunk, for at most the first 56 chunks, before the first session. Not in this change and still open on the row: the structured-answer limit and the order of passages, which would change every Standard answer on the larger window and have no measurement behind them. Two paths that predate this change can still hide an overflow from the retry (an error after ten or more streamed characters is returned as a partial answer, and a configured fallback service answers instead of rethrowing), and larger prompts make them easier to reach. `[evidence_level: test_verified for the budget, cap and window arithmetic (SessionEvidencePlanTests, 17 tests, in a full run of 568 tests, 0 failures, 3 skipped on the iOS 27.0 simulator 2026-10-07), code_verified for the loop, unverified on any device; confidence: medium; evidence_source: AgenticOrchestrator.swift executeReasoningChain and buildChainPrompt; a refuting review of the first draft the same day, whose findings changed it; the reading ceiling and the reserve ratio rest on one run on one Mac in the owner's local BenchmarkRuns/2026-10-07-context-window-probe]` Closes on a device: a Deep Think question whose log shows "whole chunks, N of B evidence tokens, window W" for its sessions and no "failed after streaming partial output", and an answer that cites the right passage for a question sharing no keyword with it.
- **[General]** **The roadmap's `Target Release` has `v5.6`.** Added 2026-10-07 by restating every existing option by name and colour, as `v5.5` was. The count of rows per release was read before and after and matched for all 20 existing options (296 rows). The eleven rows the Fixed entries name moved from `Future Backlog` to `v5.6` and from `To Do` to `In Progress`; none is `Completed`, because none has been seen on a device. The empty-library entry has no row. The three cached copies of the option list say `v5.6` (`.claude/skills/notion-roadmap/SKILL.md`, `.agents/workflows/sync-notion.md`, `.agents/rules/01-docs-and-notion-sync.md`). `[evidence_level: measured, confidence: high; evidence_source: the data source's schema as the ALTER COLUMN call returned it, and the per-release counts queried before and after, 2026-10-07]`

### Fixed
- **[Infrastructure]** **A Pro subscription left permanent Lifetime access behind.** Through 5.4, `EntitlementStore` stored every verified paid purchase as sticky paid-history protection, and that protection resolves to Lifetime, so a month of Pro, or an annual trial that never converted, kept Lifetime access after the subscription ended. From 5.5 only a Lifetime purchase earns it, and a subscription gives Pro while it is active. One rule, `EntitlementStore.protectionEarned(by:originalPurchaseDate:isRevoked:)`, decides it at launch, on each purchase or restore, and when a new device rebuilds it from StoreKit history. People who already have it keep it: the owner decided on 2026-09-29 that the app's mistake is not theirs to pay for, so protection stored on a device is never lowered, and a subscription that began before 2026-09-30 00:00 UTC still earns it on a new device (`Docs/ai/DECISIONS.md`, 2026-09-29). `EntitlementProtectionTests` pins the rule. Nothing changes for anyone who has the protection now, so there is no user-facing note. `Docs/BILLING_AND_LIMITS.md` section 4 has the lines.
- **[Retrieval]** **A "how much" question could be answered with an unrelated measurement from another document, marked Verified.** On 2026-09-25, on the macOS Debug build of 5.4 in Deep Think, "How much notice do I have to give before I move out?" was answered "1 lb." from an air fryer manual's cooking chart, with a Verified badge; the lease in the same library says 60 days. Two rules in `SpecificationExtractor` stacked to score "1 lb" 0.85 against the 0.82 precision lock (`EvidenceScoringPolicyService.precisionLockThreshold`): "much" or "many" appended liters, quarts, gallons, capacity and volume to any question, which made it a measurement question worth +0.12 to every measurement in the evidence, and the liquid-unit test found the substring " l" inside "1 lb", worth +0.22. A span at or above the lock becomes the answer (`RAGService.highPrecisionLookupOverrideAnswer`): Deep Think and Maximum return it before the model runs, Standard swaps it in after generation, and the source-only check declines any source-locked answer. "Much" and "many" now add the volume words only when the question also names one of the extractor's existing anchors (gas, gasoline, fuel, tank, capacity, volume, coolant, oil); "capacity" and "hold" add them as before. The unit test compares the measurement's unit exactly, which also recognises "4.5L" written without a space. The extractor's debug lines now print every descriptive keyword in the order the question produced them; they printed five from a per-process shuffle, which in the incident's trace read as the question's words having been replaced. `PrecisionLockAnswerTypeTests` pins the incident, the same question against a liquid capacity, a fuel question against weights, and a capacity question that must still lock "5.8 qt". An 18-question probe over both versions: the change removes three wrong locks (the notice question's "1 lb" and "5.8 qt", and "5.8 qt" for "How much power does it use?"), gains one right one ("4.5L"), and loses one right one: "How much water does the reservoir take?" now goes to the model instead of locking "1.2 L", because "water" is not an anchor. **Not fixed:** when volumes in one passage tie, the extractor keeps the first ("Same-category candidates ... picking best"), so on a car manual's capacities list both fuel-tank questions lock the engine oil's 4.5 L and "How much coolant does it need?" its 4.8 US qt, before and after this change. That belongs to the roadmap row on regex extraction in the answer seat. `[evidence_level: measured, confidence: exact_for_these_runs, evidence_source: the extractor, detector, scoring policy, logging and chunk model built from source with Swift 6.4 on Linux in the engine target's compiler settings, Docs/AuditArtifacts/RAGArchitectureAudit_2026-09-26/swift/run.sh and its four output files; before the change 3 of the 4 tests fail ("1 lb" 0.85, "5.8 qt" 0.88, "3,200 lb" 0.88) and after it 4 of 4 pass. Since then: built with Xcode 27 for iPhone and Mac on 2026-09-26; re-asked on the unsigned Mac Debug build the same day, where the notice question no longer locks ("Precision lookup: No high-confidence source span locked") but Deep Think then answered "at least 30 days" where the lease says 60, marked Verified, so the incident row stays open; and on 2026-09-28 the four tests passed in Xcode on an iOS 27 simulator in the full suite on the merged tree, 504 tests, 4 skipped, 0 failures]`
- **[General]** **Three documents said the extractive shortcut was switched off and every question goes through the model.** `Docs/HOW_IT_WORKS.md` step 5.10, the study guide's "Is extractive QA used?" and the step 5.10 row of `Docs/Engineering/RAG_TECHNICAL.md` said so. Step 5.10's own code path is commented out, but the same extractor answers through five live call sites of `RAGService.highPrecisionLookupOverrideAnswer`, which is how the "1 lb" answer above skipped the model. Each is corrected in place with the withdrawn wording quoted. `Docs/Research/RESEARCH_PAPERS_REFERENCE_SHEET.md`, which says only that step 5.10 "is commented out and does not run", is accurate and unchanged. `[evidence_level: code_verified, confidence: exact, evidence_source: RAGService.swift:8705, :8734, :9199, :14383, :15192 and the commented-out block at :13350]`
- **[General]** **Every routed task reloaded about 130 KB of documents that `AGENTS.md` rule 15 had retired, and `CHANGELOG.md` had grown past what any agent can read.** `repoos_router.py` `UNIVERSAL_DOCS`, step 2 of the Codex routing skill, `AGENTS.md` rules 3, 4 and 17, `.agents/rules/00-repoos-routing.md` and `Docs/RepoOS/01_TASK_ROUTER.md` still sent every task to the Atlas, the Transition Plan, the canonical doc, `AGENTS.md` and three RepoOS files in full (131,868 bytes). Every task now reads `Docs/ai/STATE.md`, the superseding protocol and `Docs/ai/ARCHITECTURE.md` (22,981 bytes) plus what its route names, by section; `test_every_task_loads_only_the_small_universal_set` pins the list. Versions 2.0 to 5.2 moved verbatim from `CHANGELOG.md` (651 KB before, 99 KB after) to `Docs/Archive/CHANGELOG_2.0_to_5.2.md`. `HANDOFF.md` (17 KB, 14 statements stale) is a 3 KB reading order; its four facts that lived nowhere else moved to `Docs/ai/RUNBOOK.md` and `Docs/ai/PROJECT.md`. `.agents/AGENTS.md` and `.geminirules`, both drifted copies, are pointers, with their old text in `Docs/Archive/`. Six routes read a named section instead of a whole 50-60 KB document, and `HANDOFF.md`, `GEMINI.md` and `.geminirules` are routed for the first time. `scripts/verify_doc_claims.py` now also checks `Type.member` names, the route table's document paths and the instruction files, and skips gitignored machine-local paths, which had made it fail on every clean clone. The pre-commit hook tested `-x` on that script, which git stores as 100644, so the claims gate never ran on a fresh clone; it tests `-f` now. `mktemp -t prefix` is BSD-only, so `instructions-loaded.sh`, `notion-receipt.sh` and `instructions_report.sh` did nothing on Linux and `test_enforce_docs_hook.sh` could not run; all four use an explicit template. Repository tooling only: no app source, build input or store copy changed. Roadmap: https://app.notion.com/p/3e549a74d54f815087d7db62848adb40 (Future Backlog).
- **[General]** **The reference docs pointed agents at the wrong code in two of three measured lookups, and restated a shipped version that went stale every release.** `Docs/ai/ARCHITECTURE.md` sent the on-device-versus-PCC decision to `Services/AIPlatform` (it is `ModelExecutionPlanner.makePlan` in `Services/RAG/Orchestration`, with `FoundationModelRoutePolicy` deciding only when no plan is attached), sent fusion to `Retrieval/` (it is `RAGEngine.reciprocalRankFusion`), said `HybridSearchService` reranks (it does not) and said a missing reranker falls through to fusion order (it runs a heuristic fallback); all corrected, with a product-vocabulary section (Deep Think, Maximum, Glossary, HUD, consent sheet and others to their files). Across the Atlas, the canonical doc and the retrieval, ingestion, privacy, billing, evals, roadmap and index docs, 50 edits: hardcoded shipped versions replaced by a pointer to `Docs/SHIPPED_VERSION.json`, `Docs/AUDIT/` citations marked as not in the repository, dead paths fixed, and Unknown/Verify notes beside the Evidence Threads sync claim and prefer-cloud routing. `verify_doc_claims.py`'s version floor is now 0 because no core doc restates the version. 100 further proposals and 60 suspected code defects, none independently verified, are kept in `Docs/AuditArtifacts/DocOverhaul_2026-09-24/`. `python3 scripts/verify_doc_claims.py` passes (717 claims).
- **[General]** **Nothing showed which of the repository's 264 documents still matched the code.** Added 2026-09-28, also while no version was open. `.claude/skills/repo-map` surveys every tracked file at a commit, read-only, and rebuilds the owner's private Repo Map page. It gives each document a status with its evidence: references to files, types, functions or line anchors that no longer resolve, cited Swift that changed after the document's last edit, a header version older than the live one, or history. It also lists the Swift files no other file names, draws the flow from `Docs/ai/ARCHITECTURE.md`, and lists the loose files git ignores. At `df2c5b5`: 2 documents mostly out of date, 17 with dead references, 17 behind the code, 58 whose references all resolve, 68 unchecked prose and 102 history; 28 Swift files unused. `test_build_map.py` re-derives its verdicts by a second route and passes (6 tests). Repository tooling only: no app source, build input or store copy changed.
- **[General]** **Nineteen documents named code or files that no longer exist; seven still do, and each is a plan, playbook or ledger that names what was proposed.** Added 2026-09-28. Corrected against the code, each with an evidence tag: `Docs/INGESTION_PIPELINE.md` describes the token limit as `DocumentProcessor.enforceTokenLimitOnChunks` enforces it (the embedding model's own `swift-tokenizers` tokenizer, 430 tokens) instead of naming `BertTokenizer`, which left the code on 2026-07-01; both EdgeToEdge documents name `HybridSearchService.searchWithFTS5`, where the vector and FTS arms run, instead of `hybridSearch`, which no commit declares; `Docs/Engineering/FULL_SYSTEM_TRACE.md` names `OCRConfiguration.configureRequest`; the research reference sheet maps token gating to `DocumentProcessor` and dates the removal of three vendored `swift-transformers` files; the Transition Plan and the v5 embedding ledger carry dated notes that the planned routing files were never created and that the Core AI scaffolding was deleted in `cb82471`. The repo-map survey stopped reporting three kinds of correct reference as dead: a `+` inside a file name, an xcodebuild test identifier, and a name whose own sentence says it is gone. `test_build_map.py` passes (9 tests). The seven top-level audit snapshots stay where they are, per the 2026-08-17 decision recorded in `Docs/README.md`. Documentation and tooling only.
- **[General]** **Nothing told a new agent session which code makes up a feature, or what that code ties into.** Added 2026-09-28, also while no version was open. `Docs/ai/codemap/` holds 46 feature slices whose primary files cover every app Swift file once; each names the entry points to read first, its hard-boundary traps, and typed links between symbols (`calls`, `renders`, `depends_on`, `tested_by`), each citing a file, a line and its text. A link is VERIFIED only where the Swift syntax on that line states it (it constructs the type by name, or calls the member through its type), and INFERRED where only a name matched. A `UserPromptSubmit` hook prints the slice a prompt names with every cited line re-found in the live code, `.claude/codemap/codemap.py check` re-finds them all, and `AGENTS.md` rule 19 carries the workflow for every agent. At `0e9728e`: check 0 errors, 606 VERIFIED and 519 INFERRED links. An independent reader refuted 2 of 20 sampled VERIFIED links (wrong type) and 7 of 20 INFERRED before the builder was fixed, and 0 of 20 and 2 of 20 after. In an A/B of three questions in fresh sessions, the session with the slice found the live model picker (`ModelStatusIndicator.swift:18`), which all three sessions without it missed for sheets only `#Preview` creates; `Docs/ai/ARCHITECTURE.md` now names the second of those sheets. Repository tooling only: no app source, build input or store copy changed.

### Changed
- **[UI]** **The plans screen compares each plan with the others from the storefront's live prices, and Lifetime Cohort is Lifetime.** Pulled into 5.5 at the owner's word on 2026-09-29, who took 5.5 out of App Review to carry it, alongside the prices that take effect on 2026-09-30 (Lifetime $49.99) and 2026-10-01 (Pro Annual $24.99, Pro Monthly $4.99). `PlanUpgradeSheet.swift`: under Annual's price, "Save N% vs Monthly" (`PlanPriceComparison.annualSavingsPercent`, through `LaunchSale.percentOff`, rounded down: 58 in the US, 57 in Mexico) and "$X a month, billed yearly" (`perMonthText`, rounded up in the currency's smallest unit: $2.09, ¥334); under Lifetime's, "Less than a year of Monthly" when its price is below twelve Monthly payments (`lifetimeCostsLessThanAYearOfMonthly`), above the existing months-of-Annual line. Each needs both StoreKit products loaded in one currency (`livePrices`) and says nothing otherwise, so the US fallback prices never feed a comparison. The hardcoded "Save 58% vs monthly" bullet and the "Best Value" label are gone. Every card now shows its label ("Month to month", "Pay once"); only the featured card drew one before, so "Flexible" and "One-Time" were written and never visible. Everything computed sits below the billed price and smaller, per Apple's rule that the billed amount be "the most prominent pricing element" and a breakdown or saving sit "in a subordinate position and size" (developer.apple.com/app-store/subscriptions, fetched 2026-09-29). Fallback prices are $4.99, $24.99 and $49.99. "Lifetime Cohort" reads "Lifetime" on the plans screen, in Settings' Lifetime banner and in the terms. `LaunchSale.regularLifetimePrices` holds the $49.99 price point and `scripts/schedule_sale.py`'s `REGULAR_PRICE` is 49.99, so the next sale measures its discount from the real regular price. `scripts/verify_sale_prices.py` reported every recorded currency matching App Store Connect across the 28 territories it maps, but a read of all 175 the same day found USD at 49.99 in 87 territories and 59.99 in 22, and EUR at 59.99 in 24 and 49.99 in one, so keying the sale by currency is not safe for this price point; no build is exposed, because the only sale window closed on 2026-09-30 (Future Backlog row filed). Win-back offers on the plans screen were considered and declined by the owner: they need `StoreKitBillingService.swift`, which the billing route forbids without his naming it, and StoreKit already shows its own win-back sheet in the iPhone and iPad app (`Message` is unavailable on macOS). New `PlanPriceComparisonTests` pin each figure; `LaunchSaleTests` pins the new USD regular price. Those renders show the configuration's old prices ($5.99, $29.99, Lifetime $39.99 or $59.99), so the new prices ($2.09 a month; 24 months at $49.99 and $24.99) are covered by unit tests only.
