Each says the notice question now goes to the model, and that the model's answer to it on the Mac was still wrong ("at least 30 days" where the lease says 60) and still marked Verified. The store notes' closing line is rewritten: "that one writing step" had pointed at nothing since 5.3. Three independent checkers (facts, voice, format) read the drafts before they were applied; their findings narrowed the title from "the wrong number" to "numbers from the wrong document", because a fuel-tank question can still lock the engine oil's 4.5 L from the same manual. `[evidence_level: code_verified, confidence: exact, evidence_source: CHANGELOG 5.5 retrieval entry; Docs/AuditArtifacts/RAGArchitectureAudit_2026-09-26/swift/probe_after.txt; memory extractor-fix-device-check (Mac re-ask 2026-09-26)]` Corrected 2026-09-29, store copy only: the closing line of the notes now reads "unless you okay Apple's Private Cloud Compute", because with Private Cloud Compute chosen in the model picker Deep Think and Maximum send their reasoning passes there too, not only the final writing step; and the description offers iCloud Drive sync to paid plans only, which is what `WorkspaceSyncService.swift:411-413` enforces.
- **[General]** **`Docs/RELEASE_NOTES.md` stopped at 5.3, so gunnarguy.me's copy of it never showed 5.4.** Added `## v5.5 - unreleased` and `## v5.4 - September 24, 2026` in the file's engineering register, function and performance only, wrapped at 100 columns like the sections below them, with each claim's verification level stated (the 5.4 streaming of short answers is framework-probe-verified, and the haptic, clock restart and expiry note are code-verified, not yet observed). `Docs/Release/APP_STORE_METADATA_HISTORY.md` has a `### 5.5` entry with both texts.
- **[General]** **The repository kept 27 superseded files beside the live ones, a privacy summary and a roadmap the code contradicted, and 14 scripts nothing runs.** Added 2026-09-29, at the owner's word to carry out the cleanup plan's recommendations as long as nothing fundamental breaks. Moved with `git mv` into `Docs/Archive/`, each with a row in its README and an old-to-new path table so citations inside archived text still resolve: the v4.1 `Docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`, whose name matched the live `Docs/ai/ARCHITECTURE.md`; the root v2.0 `HOW_IT_WORKS.md`; the April storage trace; a pre-WWDC 2026 copy of Apple's document APIs; the spent 4.8 release-notes draft; the finished Phase 1A plan and three one-line playbook aliases; the RepoOS prompt compiler the preflight replaced; and fourteen files plus the PR manifest of the July zero-regression audit from `.agent/`, whose risk register stays because live files cite RISK-20. `PRIVACY.md` and `Docs/ROADMAP.md` are now pointers: the first to the published policy at gunzino.me and `Docs/PRIVACY_AND_ROUTING.md`, with three statements each checked against the code; the second to the Notion roadmap, with a dated status. Their old bodies are archived verbatim. Deleted, each still in git history: seven one-off audit and analysis scripts, after the v4.1 audit tables inside three of them were extracted verbatim to `Docs/Archive/v4.1-audit/`; a release watcher for the removed Xcode-beta; six Google Ads scripts that duplicate the maintained copies in the Fascinaiting repository; an onboarding screenshot nothing references; and `Docs/Release/CONVERSION_AND_REVIEWS_2026-09.md`, copied first to the owner's App Store Connect workspace. Six undated notes moved from this file's `## Notes` to the end of `Docs/Archive/CHANGELOG_2.0_to_5.2.md`. `Docs/ai/RUNBOOK.md` gained the device-build procedure verified on 2026-09-23, 09-24 and 09-28. `Docs/ai/DECISIONS.md` records why the four June audits, the Transition Plan, the engine SDK and the `.agent/` risk register stay, and that DEC-35 was reversed in code without being amended. The Codex routing skill and the notion-roadmap skill stopped describing `Docs/ROADMAP.md` as a version source and a mirror. Documentation and tooling only; no file Xcode compiles changed.
- **[General]** **The study guide, its audio and the reference documents taught facts the code contradicts: that Private Cloud Compute is simulated or dormant, that fusion weighs vectors 0.7 and keywords 0.3, that the GPU profile never gates vector search, and that citations carry byte offsets.** Added 2026-09-29. Each correction cites the line that settles it, and none deletes a claim. Private Cloud Compute shipped in 5.2 on iOS and macOS 27; it writes the final answer after consent, and with Private Cloud Compute chosen in the model picker Deep Think and Maximum's reasoning passes can go too, behind the same consent check. Passages that called it a local simulation now say they were true from `fca838e` to `c6052df`, while a stand-in existed. 32,768 tokens is Apple's documented PCC context; the planner budgets from the value the SDK reports, and context assembly still sizes from the app's 32,768 constant. The on-device window is what the SDK reports, with 4,096 as a fallback. Fusion's first pass starts at 0.50 and 0.50, the question's intent moves it to 0.45 and 0.55 for keyword questions or 0.60 and 0.40 for conceptual ones, clamped to 0.35 to 0.65; in Standard a weak-retrieval cascade and a low-similarity keyword retry lean keyword, down to 0.3 vector and 0.7 keyword; 0.7 vector and 0.3 keyword is an initializer default only a test uses. Metal vector search needs 1,000 vectors, a Metal device and the Performance or Maximum GPU profile, so Balanced, the default, stays on the CPU. A citation names a chunk with its page and a quote of up to 240 characters. The tool registry registers six tools and defines twelve. The extractive shortcut's own step is off, but the same extractor answers value questions through five live call sites. For lookup answers a source-only check adds two on-device sessions, one drafting claims and one rating them. The consent sheet shows how much would be sent and why, behind Show Details, and Always Allow stops it asking. Embedding concurrency reaches 128 on a Mac. Corrected in `Docs/STUDY_GUIDE.md`, all five audio passes and eight word-bank files (the full audio text rebuilt from the passes, now about 80 minutes), the EdgeToEdge modules and `EDGE_TO_EDGE_FULL.md` (patched line by line, so each module still matches its span), `Docs/HOW_IT_WORKS.md`, `PRIVACY.md`, `Docs/Engineering/` (the PCC reference, hard limits, Apple models, the 2025 technical report, the full system trace, RAG technical, the retrieval upgrade plan, the v5 embedding ledger and both V50 audits), the research index and reference sheet, `Docs/LIMITATIONS.md`, `Docs/EVALS.md`, `Docs/BILLING_AND_LIMITS.md`, `Docs/DOCUMENTATION_CONSISTENCY_AUDIT.md`, `.agent/RISK_REGISTER.md`, the agent playbooks and audit-folder banners, the benchmark ledger header (extracting the run archive at the repository root overwrites the ledger; the header gives the exclude flags), `Benchmarks/README.md`, the bug-report template and `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` (swift-tokenizers, the vendored swift-transformers wrapper, the bundled tokenizer and the MiniLM embedding bundle; the Rust crates swift-tokenizers links statically still need entries, because their license texts are not on this Mac). The fixture script and the tiny research suite's README name the current benchmark runner with the library backup in front of it, because the runner writes into the app's real library. Three reviewers then tried to refute the corrections against the code, and every defect they confirmed was fixed before commit. Five findings that need app changes are filed as Future Backlog rows: in Deep Think and Maximum a Just Once consent for Private Cloud Compute carries over to the next question (High), Settings lists four tool functions where six run, iOS 26 Settings blames the build rather than the OS for missing Private Cloud Compute, each quality mode's MMR value is never read, and sentence-opening words become entities. Routes: `documentation_governance_change` for the documents and `repoos_workspace_automation` for the scripts, whose Command Center, Task Router and change-impact matrix name none of the moved or deleted paths. Documentation and tooling only.

## 5.4 - September 24, 2026

<!-- Shipped on both platforms with build 478 (Xcode Cloud #478 from c276b9a): iOS at 14:32 PT and
     macOS at 17:00 PT on 2026-09-24, each released through the App Store Connect API at the owner's
     request after approval, because the Release button would not work for him. No next version is
     open; the owner picked 5.5 as the next number (2026-09-24). Open
     a 5.5 heading, with its unreleased marker, above this one before the next source push, or
     ci_post_clone.sh stamps the released 5.4 and App Store Connect rejects the build. -->

### Fixed
- **[Orchestration]** **Standard held a finished lookup answer behind a blinking cursor and a locked composer while a second source check ran.** After the last streamed word, a Standard answer to an extractive-first question (`AnswerIntent.lookup` or `.tableLookup`; "what file types", "how many", "which embedding model" were all classified that way) ran `SourceOnlyAnswerService.verifyAndRender`: a structured draft call and a structured review call, in sequence, while `ChatScreen.isProcessing` kept the cursor blinking and the text field disabled. Standard now skips the check when the nine verification gates passed it (`RAGService.standardSkipsSourceOnlyCheck`, logged as `[SourceOnly] Skipped — Standard answer passed the verification gates`); a Standard answer the gates flagged, and Deep Think and Maximum answers, still get it unless the person finishes the answer first. Last streamed word to finished answer, macOS 27.0 Debug build, M3 Pro, on-device model, `Docs/HOW_IT_WORKS.md` as the only document: "What file types does OpenIntelligence handle?" 163.8 s and 19.4 s before, 0.33 s after; "How many verification checks does OpenIntelligence run?" 58.3 s before, 0.21 s after; "Which embedding model does OpenIntelligence use for retrieval?" 37.6 s before, 0.15 s after; an "explain" question that never ran the check, 0.12 s before and 0.10 s after. In all four lookup runs before the change the answer shown was still the model's own, and after it the streamed text matched the finished answer character for character. `SourceOnlyStandardGateTests` pins the rule. Accuracy A/B on 25 QASPER cases with greedy sampling (`BenchmarkRuns/LEDGER.md`, 2026-09-23): no case went from pass to miss, and the check replaced no answer in either run; the after run scored 11/25 against 6/25, but that gain is not the change's doing (seven before cases hit a Foundation Models `SensitiveContentAnalysisML error 15` episode and the three clean flips differed in retrieved context before generation). `[evidence_level: measured, confidence: exact_for_these_runs, evidence_source: harness and --rag-validation-stream-probe runs 2026-09-23, recorded in Docs/ai/STATE.md and BenchmarkRuns/LEDGER.md; not yet measured on a device]`
- **[Orchestration]** **The source-only check's first call could write until the model's context overflowed.** The draft call passed no `maximumResponseTokens`, and Apple documents that the model may then produce "the longest answer its context size supports" and throw if it does not stop (developer.apple.com, `GenerationOptions.maximumResponseTokens`, read 2026-09-23). The 163.8 s run above spent 163.5 s in that call and ended in "The session's transcript exceeded the model's context size". The draft now has a 1,024-token limit (`draftTokenLimit`); a structured response that reaches a limit throws a parse error at once (probed 2026-09-23: 0.9 s at a 30-token limit), which `verifyAndRender` already treats as "keep the generated answer". The limit was first set to `draftOutputReserve`, the 700 tokens the prompt budget reserves, and review asked whether that cut legitimate drafts. It did: on three Deep Think lookups the same day, completed drafts used 571 and 675 of 700 tokens and the third failed to parse, most likely at the limit. 1,024 is about 1.5 times the largest completed draft and still well short of the overflow. Each completed draft logs its use (`[SourceOnly] Draft used N of 1024 output tokens`), and every exit of the check logs its duration and outcome (`[SourceOnly] Finished in …`, `[SourceOnly] Applied: …`). In those three runs the check took 22.8 to 32.6 s and changed none of the answers. `[evidence_level: measured+doc_verified, confidence: high_for_these_runs]`
- **[UI]** **When an answer's text is complete, the cursor goes, the composer unlocks, and Stop keeps the answer with its sources.** `ChatScreen.isCheckingFinishedAnswer` is true from the last streamed word until the response returns. The bubble renders the finished text as markdown without the cursor and, after 0.6 s, says "Checking sources…" in Standard or "Refining…" in Deep Think and Maximum, where a later stage can still replace the text. The text field accepts the next question; sending it, or tapping Stop, calls `RAGService.finishAnswerNow()`, which cancels the source-only check and any stage run through `runFinishableStage` (a cancelled `respond(generating:)` returned `CancellationError` 0.00 s after `cancel()` when probed 2026-09-23), so the pipeline returns the answer it has, with its sources; the old Stop saved the text without them. One question waits at a time and goes once that answer lands; a second send used to queue a second question that cancelled the first on arrival (found in review before commit). The answer's gating record then carries `finished_early_by_person` (`ResponseMetadataGatingTests`). Measured with `--rag-validation-finish-after 3` on a Deep Think lookup: the request came 3.1 s after the text stopped, the source-only draft was cancelled at once, and the answer returned 0.01 s later with its full streamed text and sources. `[evidence_level: measured for the pipeline; the checking label and the unlocked composer are not yet observed in the running app, because the sample library held the check open for 0.1 s]`
- **[UI]** **Deep Think and Maximum stream their final answer.** `ChatScreen` passed a stream handler only in Standard, because every model call of an agentic run streams through `LLMStreamingContext.handler`. `AgenticOrchestrator.execute` now parks the chat's handler in `LLMStreamingContext.finalAnswerHandler` and clears `handler` for the whole run; four calls that write a final answer stream through `LLMStreamingContext.answerHandler`: Deep Think's reasoning-chain synthesis, Maximum's core and exhaustive syntheses, and direct synthesis for moderate retrieval. The no-retrieval direct answer does not stream, because a failed self-critique replaces it with a stage `finishAnswerNow()` cannot end (found in review). Deep Think's recursive research and verification loop and Maximum's refinement, which can replace the streamed text, run through `RAGService.runFinishableStage` with both handlers cleared, so nothing they generate streams and `finishAnswerNow()` ends them with the chain's answer standing. Not streamed, as before: speculative and decomposed answers, precision lookups, and Go Deeper. Measured with the stream probe on macOS: a Deep Think "explain" question showed text from 50.0 s of a 67.7 s answer (before, nothing until 67.7 s) with exactly one streamed call; a Maximum answer from 34.6 s of 44.5 s; and a Deep Think lookup streamed a 3,779-character synthesis that research replaced 17.7 s later with the correct "Nine verification checks run." Two side effects: a Standard query the planner escalates to the agentic path (non-Apple models only) no longer streams every intermediate call into the bubble, and the Deep Think fallback to Standard for precision questions now streams, so, like Standard, it keeps a streamed answer that lacks citations instead of retrying for them (`preserveStreamingResponse`). `[evidence_level: code_verified+measured]`
- **[UI]** **A Standard answer small enough for structured generation now streams instead of replaying.** Under the 3,600-token structured budget, `FoundationModelStructuredGenerator` called `respond(generating: DirectRAGAnswer.self)` and emitted the finished answer in one piece, so the typing indicator showed for the whole generation. It now calls `streamResponse(to:generating:)` and emits the `answer` field as it grows; `answer` is the schema's first property, so it is generated first. Probed 2026-09-23: the partial answer grew in 14 steps across 27 snapshots, each extending the last, and the value rebuilt from the final snapshot's `rawContent` matched the streamed text. `[evidence_level: probe_verified; the app path itself not yet exercised, since the measured questions exceeded the structured budget]`
- **[UI]** **Nothing said when an answer finished off screen, and the elapsed clock froze on return.** A finished answer now plays `DSHaptics.success()`; one that finishes, or fails, while Chat is not on screen increments `ChatAnswerNotice`, which badges the Chat tab until Chat appears. Observed 2026-09-23 on the iOS 27 simulator: a badge of 1 on the Chat tab while Documents showed, gone on return. On macOS the answer is counted (`[ChatAnswerNotice] Answer finished off screen; unseen=1` in the app's output) but the toolbar tabs do not draw the badge, so the Mac gets no visible tab signal yet. `.onDisappear` stopped the processing clock and nothing restarted it for an answer still running, so the clock now restarts on appear. The iOS background task's progress moved only at the first streamed word, so Deep Think and Maximum sat at 62% for their whole run; each pipeline step now moves it a sixth of the way toward 80%, never backwards, and a finished text reports a checking stage at 92%. When iOS ends the background time, `keepAnswerStoppedByBackgroundExpiry` keeps whatever was written, in the query's own library, marked as a partial answer, or, when the text was complete, as saved without its sources; before, the expiration handler cleared the session id before the query's own error path ran, so the answer vanished without a message. `[evidence_level: observed on the iOS simulator and macOS for the count, on iOS for the badge; code_verified for the rest; the haptic, the clock and expiry not yet observed]`
- **[UI]** **The in-app Product Guide, the README, `HOW_IT_WORKS.md` and `STUDY_GUIDE.md` said the consent sheet shows exactly what would be sent.** `CloudConsentPromptView` shows the provider, the model, character and chunk counts, the payload size and the reason; `CloudTransmissionRecord.promptPreview` holds the prompt's first 160 characters and nothing reads it. All five sentences (`SampleDocumentManager.swift:59`, `README.md:31`, `Docs/HOW_IT_WORKS.md:44` and `:802`, `Docs/STUDY_GUIDE.md:1650`) now say it shows how much would be sent and why, matching the corrected 5.4 store description. The sample guide changed by that one sentence only: the post-edit hook's swift-format pass re-indented the file's multi-line sample documents, which can change the text users are shown, so it was reverted.
- **[UI]** **What's New and the in-app update notes promised a rating request never comes twice in four months; a thumbs-up can bring one sooner.** The 120-day gate covers only the automatic request (`ReviewPromptService`); the thumbs-up alert's "I love it" calls `requestReview` itself, limited to once per version and 14 days apart. The store copy (both `release_notes.txt` files and `WHATS_NEW.md`) now says "on its own" and the in-app text (`WhatsNewStore.swift`, `Docs/USER_CHANGELOG.md`, `OpenIntelligence/Resources/VersionHistory.md`) "on the app's own initiative". `VersionHistoryTests` 3 of 3 and `WhatsNewCoverageTests` 4 of 4 pass on the iOS 27 simulator. The rest of `WhatsNewStore.swift`'s diff is swift-format whitespace and one trailing comma in an array literal, from the post-edit hook.
- **[General]** **`scripts/asc_listing_extras.rb` does the App Store Connect work `asc_prepare_release.rb` does not, and never submits.** It appends `fastlane/review_notes/<version>.txt` to the App Review notes (the 5.3 notes told a reviewer to try Maximum and said nothing of the new cap), copies the live sale line onto the version while the sale runs so a mid-sale release keeps it, sets the Pro and Lifetime descriptions to `QuotaPolicy`'s limits within Apple's 55 characters, and uploads `fastlane/iap_images/<productId>.png` to any subscription without an image, because a win-back offer is promoted on the App Store only with an approved one. `scripts/render_iap_images.swift` draws those images. Not yet run: the permission classifier refuses App Store Connect writes from an agent, and `api.appstoreconnect.apple.com` dropped the TLS handshake from the agent shell on 2026-09-23 while Apple reported no incident.
- **[General]** **fascinaiting.me and gunzino.me still said the app shows "exactly what would be sent" before Private Cloud Compute.** Both now say it shows how much would be sent and why (Fascinaiting `bdff4ff2`, Gunzino `cc2912b`), matching the corrected 5.4 description; Gunzino's `extract_pages.py --check` reads 22 of 22 pages matching.
- **[UI]** **The plans screen at the end of setup crashed the app, 1.5 seconds after "Start Asking", for every free user who finished setup.** `ContentView` presented `PlanUpgradeSheet(entryPoint: .onboarding)` from a `.sheet` attached after `.environmentObject(entitlementStore)`, so the sheet sat outside that injection, and `PlanUpgradeSheet` reads its `@EnvironmentObject EntitlementStore` as it draws. Reproduced 2026-09-22 on a Debug simulator build of build 469's Swift: `Fatal error: No ObservableObject of type EntitlementStore found`, `EXC_BREAKPOINT`, with `PlanUpgradeSheet.heroSection` and `.body` on the faulting stack. The sheet now receives the store itself. Found in a pre-submission audit, so build 469 was never submitted and no release carried it. The same code runs on macOS and was not run there. The What's New sheet beside it is unaffected: `WhatsNewView` reads only `dismiss`. The rest of `ContentView.swift`'s diff is swift-format whitespace from the post-edit hook; with whitespace removed, the file differs from before only by this fix.
- **[UI]** **The 5.4 App Store description said nothing you import leaves the device, and that the app shows exactly what would be sent.** With Private Cloud Compute allowed, `CloudEvidenceMinimizer` sends passage text with document names and page numbers, and `CloudConsentPromptView` shows the provider, model, character counts, passage count, payload size and reason, never the text. The opening sentence now ends "unless you allow it", the consent sentence reads "The app shows you how much would be sent and why, and asks you first", and the retention sentence is attributed to Apple. Committed in `3f6773e`; App Store Connect keeps the old text until `scripts/asc_prepare_release.rb 5.4 469 --apply` runs. The live 5.3 listing, `README.md:31`, `Docs/HOW_IT_WORKS.md:44`, `Docs/STUDY_GUIDE.md:1650` and the in-app sample guide (`SampleDocumentManager.swift:59`) still say "exactly". `[evidence_level: code_verified, confidence: high, evidence_source: ModelExecutionPlanner.swift CloudEvidenceMinimizer; CloudConsentPromptView.swift:164-183]`
- **[General]** **Every session started by calling a correct handoff foreign.** The session brief read the recorded commit with a pattern that stops at whitespace, and `Docs/ai/STATE.md` writes the sha as inline code, so the token it took was the sha *with its backticks*. That is not a revision git can resolve, and the brief printed "names a commit not in this repository" on 2026-09-21 while `STATE.md` named HEAD exactly. A warning that fires on a correct handoff teaches the next agent to ignore the one that fires on a stale one. The backticks are now stripped before resolving; a plain sha resolves as before. **The same bug remains in the global copy at `~/.claude/hooks/session-start.sh` line 146,** which runs for every repository on this Mac and is outside this one. `[evidence_level: measured, confidence: exact, evidence_source: extracted token before and after the fix, resolved with git rev-parse against HEAD 3cce8b3]`
- **[General]** **Two test gates were passing or failing for reasons unrelated to what they test, and one was quietly changing file permissions.** `scripts/test_verify_doc_claims.sh` stood at 5 passed and 1 failed. Its wrong-version case edited a literal string out of a tracked doc, and commit `a375fc8` removed that string on 2026-09-02, so the case failed with the fixture missing and the verifier was blamed. A second case, the pattern-floor guard, had passed for weeks while testing nothing: its replacement pattern kept an empty alternative that matched almost every line, so the verifier failed for other reasons and the case only checked the exit code. `scripts/test_stop_handoff.sh` stood at 9 passed and 2 failed, because it measured history against a moving HEAD, and that range grew to contain the very docs it asserted were missing. **Both verifiers were correct; the fixtures had rotted.** Each case now builds its own fixture in a scratch copy or a throwaway git repository and never touches the checkout. Each must also see the tool's own failure line rather than any non-zero exit, which makes the assertions stricter. Results are 6 of 6 and 11 of 11, identical on a dirty checkout and a clean clone. **The side effect:** the old test restored edited docs with `mv`, which left `Docs/INGESTION_PIPELINE.md`, `Docs/RETRIEVAL_PIPELINE.md`, `Docs/ai/ARCHITECTURE.md` and `scripts/verify_doc_claims.py` at mode 0600. Git records only the executable bit, so nothing showed it. They are back at 0644. `[evidence_level: measured, confidence: exact, evidence_source: both scripts run 2026-09-22, 6 passed 0 failed and 11 passed 0 failed; stat -f %Lp before and after]`
- **[General]** **`ci_post_clone.sh` told the operator to do the one thing it forbids.** When `[Unreleased]` has entries, the old message said to bump `MARKETING_VERSION[sdk=macosx*]`, and the same script fails the build if that per-SDK override reappears. The message now names the file and heading it read, the version it extracted, why a shipped version is rejected by App Store Connect as on 2026-07-28, and the fix: open `## X.Y <!-- unreleased -->` under `## [Unreleased]` before the next push. Message text only; the extraction logic is unchanged. `[evidence_level: measured, confidence: exact, evidence_source: bash -n exit 0; old and new scripts run on three sample changelogs, identical exits]`
- **[Ingestion]** **`testSilentAudio` could never skip: the helper failed the test before its own timeout branch ran.** The test's comment explains that a timeout is deliberately an `XCTSkip`, because "it hung" was an inference from one unmeasured cold start. But `processingOutcome` waited with `XCTestCase.fulfillment(of:)`, and that form records a test failure the instant the wait expires, so the `.timedOut` case returned to code that could no longer skip. Every run since 2026-09-18 either carried the test by hand in `-skip-testing:` or failed on it, which is what made its exclusion "prose-only". The helper now waits on a standalone `XCTestExpectation` through a delegate-less `XCTWaiter`, which reports the timeout as a result. Verified both ways on the iOS 27 simulator: normally the WAV throws in 0.8 s and the test passes; with the timeout forced to 1 ms in a throwaway copy, it skips with its own message and the suite succeeds. The underlying question, hang versus missing speech model, is unchanged and still needs a device. `[evidence_level: measured, confidence: exact, evidence_source: IngestionFormatCoverageTests run 12 passed 0 failed 2026-09-22; forced-timeout run "1 test skipped, 0 failures"; XCTest.swiftinterface fulfillment(of:) overloads]`
- **[General]** **The sale now ends itself.** The Lifetime price reverts on its own, but "Lifetime is on sale until September 29" is plain copy on the live listing and on three websites, and nothing expired it. `scripts/asc_end_sale.rb` finds every live or in-preparation version on both platforms whose promotional text still mentions a sale and replaces it with the non-sale text recorded for 5.3; it refuses to run before 2026-09-30, and a second run changes nothing. Dry-run against the live store on 2026-09-22 matched exactly the four records that carry the sale line. A one-time scheduled task, `openintelligence-end-lifetime-sale`, runs it on 2026-09-30 at 09:00 Pacific, removes the sale line from fascinaiting.me, gunnarguy.me and gunzino.me, verifies each page live, and records the result here. `[evidence_level: measured, confidence: exact, evidence_source: asc_end_sale.rb --dry-run output; scheduled task file]`
- **[General]** **The "33% off" promotional text is corrected on both live 5.3 records** (HTTP 200 each, 2026-09-22); the two websites that still name a figure now say it is the US discount. **Both Pro subscription descriptions cannot be corrected in place:** Apple returns 409 `UNMODIFIABLE` for a localization in `ACTIVE` state, and separately caps the field at 55 characters. Two facts lower the urgency: the app never renders a StoreKit product description (no call site), and the app has zero promoted purchases, so no customer sees the string today. It stays wrong until a subscription change goes through review. `[evidence_level: measured, confidence: exact, evidence_source: asc_fix_listing_copy.rb output; promotedPurchases count 0; grep for .description/.displayDescription under Features/Billing and Services/Billing]`
- **[General]** **fascinaiting.me served the agent instructions, the Google Ads configuration and four internal audits to anyone who asked.** GitHub Pages publishes that site from the root of `main`, so every tracked file was live, `robots.txt` allowed indexing, and `google_ads_config.json` carried the customer id, every campaign and ad id, and all ad copy. No credential was exposed; the analytics key was gitignored and never committed. A Jekyll `_config.yml` exclude list now keeps those files in the repository and out of the site. Verified after the Pages rebuild: each returns 404 and the home page still returns 200. That change lives in the Fascinaiting repository, commit `572a5d51`. `[evidence_level: measured, confidence: exact, evidence_source: curl status codes before and after, 2026-09-22]`
- **[General]** **A win-back offer for each Pro subscription, as a script.** Three Pro Monthly subscribers cancelled at 24, 26 and 31 days and all five cancel-sheet views ended in a cancel with nothing offered. `scripts/asc_winback_offers.rb` creates Pro Monthly at $2.99 for the first three months and Pro Annual at $19.99 for the first year, for lapsed subscribers who paid at least a month, in every territory Apple equalizes the USA price point to, one offer per customer per year. Apple presents win-back offers itself on launch and on the App Store; this app does not suppress StoreKit messages, so no code is needed. Dry run by default; `--apply` creates them. The owner ran it on 2026-09-22 after two rejected attempts taught the script what Apple actually accepts (priority `NORMAL`, a start date at least a day past Apple's clock): offers `6814902295` (Monthly) and `6814902418` (Annual), active from 2026-09-24 for one year. `[evidence_level: code_verified, confidence: exact, evidence_source: winBackOffers count 0 on both subscriptions read 2026-09-22; POST schema from post-v1-winbackoffers.json]`
- **[UI]** **The listing's first screenshots showed an empty chat, a document quota and an Atlas reading "0 Docs".** Those three are what a stranger sees in search results, and in the App Store data only 2.1% of impressions become page views while 18.6% of page views become downloads: the page converts, the search result does not get opened. None of them showed an answer, and all carried a debug "A20 GPU" badge. The 5.4 set is four captioned scenes staged with the app's real grounded-answer view: an answer whose three claims each cite a page, a refusal with its Information Gaps panel ("Your documents don't say whether pets are allowed"), the sources sheet, and the Private Cloud Compute consent sheet. The only figure shown is the measured 27 tokens a second. The earlier demo scenes displayed "65 tok/s" and "384-dim embeddings", neither measured, and are left unused. New DEBUG-only launch argument `--screenshot-store answer|refusal|sources|consent`; screenshot mode now also hides the sale banner, which would have outlived the sale on the listing. `scripts/compose_store_screenshots.py` captions them in SF Pro on the icon's blue, and `scripts/asc_upload_screenshots.rb` uploads a full set before deleting the old one. 16 images across four sets, all `COMPLETE` on the 5.4 record. `[evidence_level: measured, confidence: exact, evidence_source: appScreenshotSets read back 2026-09-22]`
- **[General]** **Keywords: "rag" is replaced by "manual".** Nobody outside AI engineering searches "rag"; people with equipment manuals do. "intelligence" stays, because the app name is the single word OpenIntelligence and it is not established that Apple splits it. `[evidence_level: code_verified, confidence: high]`
- **[UI]** **The Maximum-mode daily cap is enforced for the first time.** `EntitlementStore.consumeMaximumModeUseIfNeeded()` and `MaximumModeQuotaStore.consumeIfAllowed` existed since 6dc093c (2026-05-12) with no caller, `RAGServiceError.maximumModeQuotaReached` was declared and never thrown, and the "3 left today" label never decremented. So the paywall's headline promise, "Pro and Lifetime lift the daily cap on Maximum mode", gated nothing, and every free user had unlimited Maximum for four months. `ChatScreen.sendMessage` now consumes a run before dispatch when the effective mode is Maximum; `.blocked` shows the dialog that already existed with Standard, Deep Think and See Plans; paid tiers return `.allowedUnlimited`. `MaximumModeQuotaStoreTests` pins three runs, a blocked fourth that does not consume, and the midnight reset. `[evidence_level: test_verified, confidence: exact, evidence_source: repo-wide grep for the two consume methods before this change; MaximumModeQuotaStoreTests]`
- **[UI]** **One rating request, on the first verified answer, and agentic answers count.** Two schedulers asked Apple on the same third answer: `ReviewPromptService` at 2 s and the pre-5.2 `AppReviewPromptTracker` in `ChatScreen` at 4 s, spending two of Apple's three yearly prompts at once. The tracker also needed two distinct usage days, and 37% of device-weeks in the App Store data show one session. The threshold is now 1, the tracker is reduced to the version stamp the thumbs-up alert reads, and `isVerified` takes the retrieved source count, because Deep Think and Maximum answers leave `gatingDecision` nil unless source-only refinement ran, so no agentic answer had ever counted. `source_only_abstained` joins the flag list. `ReviewPromptServiceTests` pins all of it. `[evidence_level: test_verified, confidence: exact, evidence_source: ChatScreen.swift before this change lines 2931 and 3063 to 3086; ReviewPromptServiceTests]`
- **[UI]** **A dismissible plans screen at the end of the setup checklist, once.** Over half of paid conversions happen on install day and 72% in Productivity (RevenueCat, March 2026); the first offer here came at a limit or the fourth verified answer. `ContentView` presents `PlanUpgradeSheet(entryPoint: .onboarding)` 1.5 s after `markOnboardingCompleted`, never after a skip, free tier only, gated by `PlanAskService.noteOnboardingCompleted`, which records the ask before presenting so it cannot repeat. The moment-of-value ask stays at the fourth answer so the two never stack. `[evidence_level: test_verified, confidence: exact, evidence_source: PlanAskServiceTests.testOnboardingAsk_onceFreeOnly_neverUnderTest]`
- **[UI]** **The paywall's "social proof" banner says two checkable things instead of none.** It read "Upgrade anytime, cancel in App Store settings." It now shows the App Store privacy label, Data Not Collected, and the app's own rating from Apple's public lookup, cached a day, only once the count reaches 20; five ratings tells a buyer the app is new. `AppStoreRatingService` sends the app id and nothing else, so the privacy label stays true. `[evidence_level: test_verified, confidence: exact, evidence_source: AppStoreRatingSnapshotTests]`
- **[General]** **Subtitle and keywords are in both metadata trees.** "Chat With Your Files Offline" and the 14 keywords lived only in App Store Connect; a `deliver` push with `force: true` would have blanked them. The description opens for a buyer instead of an insider, and the 5.4 release notes are written. `[evidence_level: code_verified, confidence: exact]`
- **[UI]** **5.3 shipped with no What's New entry, so everyone who updated into it was shown nothing.** `WhatsNewStore.releases` is keyed by `CFBundleShortVersionString`, and `evaluateOnLaunch` treats an unknown key as nothing-authored: it records the version as seen and returns, with no build failure and no log line. The only symptom is an update that opens straight into the app, which nobody can see from a development machine. **This is the third time:** 5.0.2 and 5.1 shipped the same way and were caught the same way, after the fact. The 5.3 entry is now authored from the `Docs/USER_CHANGELOG.md` section of the same name, leading with the sale. **The recurrence is what got fixed:** `WhatsNewCoverageTests` reads the newest `## vX.Y` heading out of the bundled `VersionHistory.md`, which is already a build input, and fails when `releases` has no matching key. It also fails on an entry keyed to a version other than its own, which is the 2026-08-22 defect where the 5.0 sheet froze mid-cycle. `[evidence_level: code_verified, confidence: exact, evidence_source: WhatsNewStore.evaluateOnLaunch; releases keys 5.2, 5.1, 5.0.2, 5.0.1, 5.0, 4.9 before this change]`
- **[General]** **`Docs/USER_CHANGELOG.md` still read `## v5.3 - unreleased` after 5.3 went live**, and the app renders that file. The bundled `OpenIntelligence/Resources/VersionHistory.md` is a byte-identical mirror enforced by `VersionHistoryTests`, so Settings, About and Version History all showed a shipped release as unreleased, and gunnarguy.me republished the same string daily. Both copies now read `## v5.3 - September 18, 2026`. `[evidence_level: code_verified, confidence: exact]`
- **[General]** **`fastlane upload_release_metadata` would have replaced 20 current screenshots with 3 stale ones.** The lane set `skip_screenshots: false` while `fastlane/screenshots/en-US/` holds three PNGs from 2026-08-26, which is not a complete set for any device family. The curated sets are uploaded through the App Store Connect API instead, so screenshots are now explicitly not fastlane's job and the lane skips them. The three files are left in place rather than deleted. `[evidence_level: code_verified, confidence: exact, evidence_source: fastlane/Fastfile upload_release_metadata]`
- **[General]** **The two metadata trees had drifted apart, and both were wrong for the release they feed.** `fastlane/metadata/` and `fastlane/metadata-ios/` are a push source for the version named in the lane, not a record of what is live, and `Docs/SHIPPED_VERSION.json` says the version being prepared is 5.4. Both trees still held 5.3's **sale** promotional text while App Store Connect's 5.4 records already held non-sale text, so a metadata push for 5.4 would have put a sale line onto a release shipping after the sale ends on 2026-09-30. Both now carry the same 162-character non-sale text the 5.4 records already have, so the repo and the store agree and the two trees match each other again. `[evidence_level: code_verified, confidence: exact, evidence_source: appStoreVersionLocalizations on 5.4 read 2026-09-20; Docs/SHIPPED_VERSION.json preparing=5.4]`
- **[General]** **The live 5.3 listing says "Lifetime is 33% off", which one string cannot be true of.** Promotional text is one value served to every storefront, and the same discount measures 32.2% in India, 30.8% in Mexico and 40.0% in Australia. The app already computes the real percentage for the customer's own storefront from StoreKit prices and shows it in the sale banner, so the listing was contradicting the app. **"Unlimited documents" in that sentence is true and stays:** `QuotaPolicy.lifetimeDocumentLimit` is `unlimitedDocumentLimit`, and the sentence describes Lifetime. **Not yet applied.** The correction is `scripts/asc_fix_listing_copy.rb`, which carries both localization ids and a `--dry-run`; two attempts to run it were refused by the local tool-permission classifier, not by Apple. `[evidence_level: code_verified, confidence: exact, evidence_source: QuotaPolicy.swift:5,10; appStoreVersionLocalizations 44444bb1 and 7dcb9118 read 2026-09-20]`
- **[General]** **Both Pro subscription descriptions in App Store Connect are wrong in both halves.** Pro Annual reads "Annual billing for unlimited documents and 5 libraries." and Pro Monthly the same, while `QuotaPolicy.proDocumentLimit` is `1_000` and `proLibraryLimit` is `10`. Documents are capped, not unlimited, and the library count understates the plan by half. **Not yet applied,** by the same script and for the same reason as the line above. `[evidence_level: code_verified, confidence: exact, evidence_source: QuotaPolicy.swift:9,14; subscriptionLocalizations 49d09ca0 and bc30168b read 2026-09-20]`

<!-- Opened 2026-09-18, the evening 5.3 went live on both platforms with build 464.
     ci_post_clone.sh stamps MARKETING_VERSION from the FIRST "## <number>" heading, so this
     section must exist before the next source push: without it the next build stamps 5.3, a
     version already in App Store Connect, and is rejected. That is the exact failure of
     2026-07-28. The "unreleased" marker on the heading's own line is the only place this
     repository records that the section has not shipped; repoos_router.py reads it from that
     line and nowhere else. Remove it when 5.4 goes live, not before. -->
- **[General]** **The pre-commit hook refused a correct store-copy commit once the metadata history outgrew a pipe buffer.** `scripts/enforce_docs_hook.sh` runs under `set -o pipefail` and looked for the `### <version>` heading with `printf "$history" | grep -q`. At 69,551 bytes, grep exited at its match while printf was still writing, printf died of SIGPIPE, and pipefail turned the match into "no history entry for v5.4". The changelog-tag check read its input the same way. Both now read from a here-string. Two regression cases in `scripts/test_enforce_docs_hook.sh` feed each check 90 KB; both failed against the old hook and pass against the new one, 18 passed in all. What the hook enforces is unchanged: the cases that must fail still fail.

### Changed
- **[General]** **The 5.4 store screenshots are redone on all three platforms: 9 iPhone, 7 iPad, 6 Mac, where there were 4, 4 and 7 from June.** `scripts/compose_store_screenshots.py` draws the device or Mac window with an enlarged callout per scene on a lit panel, and now composes the Mac set; `scripts/screenshots/mac_capture.sh` captures it from the Debug build; `scripts/asc_upload_screenshots.rb` handles the 10-per-set limit, a version pulled from review, and the Mac record. The Atlas scene comes from the owner's iPhone because the simulator draws none of its points. Kept off the listing: the Database tab's unbenchmarked multipliers and the Atlas card's "0 Docs". Procedure and traps in `Docs/ai/RUNBOOK.md`.
- **[UI]** **The 5.4 release notes and in-app update notes cover function and performance only.** At the owner's direction on 2026-09-23, `fastlane/metadata/en-US/release_notes.txt` and its iOS twin, `WHATS_NEW.md`, `Docs/USER_CHANGELOG.md` with its bundled copy, and the "5.4" entry in `WhatsNewStore.swift` describe the answer work above. The in-app entry also carries 5.3's four function items (Apple's data detector, Adapt to the question, the sample questions, the welcome screens), each titled "From 5.3", because 5.3 shipped without an entry and `evaluateOnLaunch` shows only the current version's, so no one ever saw them there; the store notes stay 5.4-only, since the store showed 5.3's in September. All of it was then rewritten in the owner's own voice at his direction: first person, contractions, the defect named plainly, double spaces after sentences. The plans screen at the end of setup, the paywall's two checkable facts, the rating request and the enforced Maximum cap are no longer announced to users; each is still recorded in this section, and none of them changed. The notes are identical on both platforms because `scripts/asc_prepare_release.rb` writes one text to both records, so iPhone-only items say "On iPhone" or "If iOS".

### Added
- **[General]** **`--rag-validation-stream-probe` measures what the chat sees.** The harness passed no stream handler, so it could not observe the wait between an answer's last word and its completion, or whether an agentic run leaked intermediate text. With the flag it reports `[StreamProbe] firstText lastText firstFinal done afterLastText textEvents finals streamedChars answerChars sharedPrefix`. `--rag-validation-finish-after <seconds>` then calls `finishAnswerNow()` that long after the text is complete, as the chat's Stop does.
- **[General]** **The roadmap has a `v5.5` option that nothing uses.** Added to the Notion `Target Release` options on 2026-09-23 by restating every existing option by name and colour; all 262 rows kept their values (counts per release checked before and after). The three rows filed against it moved to `v5.4` the same day, when the owner put every change since 5.3 in 5.4: 5.4 is not submitted, and App Store Connect has no 5.5. Before that, `faed0d6` pushed these entries under a `## 5.5` heading, so Xcode Cloud build 475 went to TestFlight as 5.5; nothing uses it. The cached option lists in `.claude/skills/notion-roadmap/SKILL.md` and `.agents/rules/01-docs-and-notion-sync.md` stopped at `v5.1` and now match the live data source; the skill also records that the API refuses a new option written onto a row. `test_repoos_router.py` 29/29 and `test_enforce_docs_hook.sh` 16/16 after the change.

## 5.3 - September 18, 2026

<!-- Shipped on both platforms with build 464; released manually after approval. The marker was

### Added
- **[General]** **Every iPhone and iPad screenshot on the listing is current again, captured without a device or a single permission prompt.** The iPhone sets were device captures from the 5.2 era and the iPad set was five images from January, thirteen releases old. The app already had a DEBUG-only screenshot mode nobody had used from the command line: `--screenshot`, `--screenshot-tab`, `--screenshot-import-samples`. Combined with `xcrun simctl io screenshot`, which needs no permission of any kind, that makes the whole set reproducible from a clean simulator in about ten minutes. **Two traps, both now written down in the runbook:** the Silicon HUD's one-time "Drag me" tip renders over the library chip on a fresh install and has to be retired by seeding `hudHasBeenDragged` into the app container before first launch; and relaunching to switch tabs while the samples are still importing leaves an "interrupted uploads paused" panel over the UI, so the import must be allowed to finish first. **Twenty images uploaded** to the 5.4 records across `APP_IPHONE_67`, `APP_IPHONE_65`, `APP_IPHONE_61` and `APP_IPAD_PRO_3GEN_129`, every one reading `COMPLETE`; the old ones were removed. The captures also served as verification: 3 documents, 41 chunks, no duplicate samples, and the curated sample questions rather than the template fallback. **The macOS set is unchanged and cannot be done this way:** there is no macOS simulator and window capture needs a Screen Recording grant, so it stays seven images from 2026-06-21 until the owner grants screen access. `[evidence_level: measured, confidence: exact, evidence_source: appScreenshotSets read back on 5.4 showing 5 COMPLETE shots in each of the four iOS sets; the captures themselves at 1320x2868 and 2048x2732]`

- **[General]** **Pro Annual's free trial is actually gone, and the Lifetime rename is blocked by Apple rather than by tooling.** 5.3 withdrew the trial from the app's copy on 2026-09-18 but the introductory offers themselves were still live in App Store Connect, one per territory, so the store's own purchase sheet kept showing a seven-day trial the app no longer mentioned. All **175** were deleted through `DELETE /v1/subscriptionIntroductoryOffers/{id}` and verified by re-listing the subscription's `introductoryOffers`, which now returns zero. Existing trialists are unaffected; Pro Annual now carries no introductory offer at all, so it could take one again in future. **The Lifetime product's buyer-facing name could not be changed:** `PATCH inAppPurchaseLocalizations/{id}` returns HTTP 409 `ENTITY_ERROR.ATTRIBUTE.INVALID.UNMODIFIABLE`, because both the localization and the product's single version are `APPROVED`, and an approved in-app purchase localization is immutable. Renaming requires a new in-app purchase version and therefore a review cycle. **Worth recording for the next agent:** in-app purchase resources live under the **`/v2/`** API base, while `scripts/xcode_cloud_toolchain.rb`'s reusable `api` helper hardcodes `/v1/`; every early attempt returned 404 and read like a missing endpoint when the path was simply wrong. `[evidence_level: measured, confidence: exact, evidence_source: 175 DELETEs returning 204 and a follow-up list returning 0 offers; the 409 body quoted above; the product's own relationship links, all /v2/, read from apps/6756559175/inAppPurchasesV2]`

- **[General]** **The sale is on all three websites, the one App Store review finally has a reply, and 5.3 is submitted.** Web referrers are a top install channel and none of the three sites mentioned the Lifetime discount. gunzino.me carries it in the OpenIntelligence eyebrow (both the Astro source and the hand-written mirror the build gate compares against), fascinaiting.me under the hero App Store line, gunnarguy.me in the project highlight. **That last one also corrected a stale claim the 2026-09-14 sweep missed:** the highlight still said consent-gated PCC routing was "built and awaiting iOS and macOS 27", which 5.2 shipped on 2026-09-10; it survived because it did not contain any phrase that sweep searched for. Each sale line carries a comment naming 2026-09-30 as its removal date, because a discount line outliving its price schedule is a false claim on a public site. The only App Store review the app has ever had (2026-03-25, four stars, unanswered for six months) has a developer reply, posted through the API and `PENDING_PUBLISH` at Apple. `[evidence_level: measured, confidence: exact, evidence_source: live pages fetched 2026-09-18 showing the sale text on gunzino.me and fascinaiting.me; customerReviewResponses POST HTTP 201; App Store Connect 5.3 iOS and macOS both WAITING_FOR_REVIEW with build 464]`

- **[UI]** **A thumbs-up offers the store page, the Lifetime card does the arithmetic, and the update sheet leads with the sale.** Three more levers for buyers and reviews, each true by construction. **The friendly review alert was dead code:** `showFriendlyReviewPrompt` had a declaration, an `.alert`, and no writer, so its "Write a Review" button, the only route in the app to a *written* review (Apple's `requestReview` sheet yields star ratings and nothing else), had never been shown to anyone. `triggerThumbsUpReviewPrompt` now shows it instead of the bare Apple sheet, under the same per-version and cooldown guards; the alert's message no longer says "fully on-device", which stopped being true in 5.2. **`LaunchSale.monthsOfAnnual`**: the Lifetime card says how many months of Pro Annual its price buys, computed from StoreKit's live prices for the customer's storefront and never from the hardcoded US fallbacks; rounded down; true at the sale price and at the regular one. **The What's New sheet, which appears on the first launch after an update and is the one surface that reaches the 4.9 and 5.0 users still generating most sessions, was written for 5.3 but never keyed to it**, so no updater saw it. `WhatsNewStore.releases` is looked up by `CFBundleShortVersionString` and 5.3 shipped without a `"5.3"` key, which `evaluateOnLaunch` treats as nothing-authored: it records the version as seen and returns. The copy below describes what the sheet was meant to say. Corrected in 5.4, along with a test that fails the build the next time it happens. `[evidence_level: code_verified, confidence: high, evidence_source: ChatScreen.swift showFriendlyReviewPrompt with no prior assignment; LaunchSale.swift monthsOfAnnual; WhatsNewStore.evaluateOnLaunch; ~/ASC app_sessions by version]`

- **[UI]** **The sale is said where people are, and the plans get one unprompted look.** The archive for the last fourteen days shows 63 opt-in sessions, most on 4.9 and 5.0.1, so anything inside the app reaches few people; the two changes here are sized to that. **`LaunchSaleBanner`**, one line at the top of the Chat and Documents tabs for the free tier while `LaunchSale` can prove a Lifetime discount from StoreKit's live price against the recorded regular price: the real percentage and the real last day, a tap opens the plans, a dismissal is keyed to the window's end date so the banner returns only for a new sale. It cannot show for a paying user or for a discount that is not running, because the same function that refuses to advertise one on the paywall refuses here. **`PlanAskService`**, a sibling of `ReviewPromptService`: once per install, free tier only, on the fourth verified answer, one after the rating request's third so the two sheets never share a moment, two seconds after the answer lands so it never covers the answer, and never on top of another sheet. The decision is a pure function pinned by six tests. The sheet says plainly that it will not ask again, and it does not. Also on 2026-09-18, by API on all four localizations (the live 5.2 records and the 5.3 records, both platforms): the promotional text now names the sale, "Lifetime is 33% off until September 29", the one listing field editable on a live version; the metadata history records the non-sale text to restore after 2026-09-30. Two new `PlanUpgradeEntryPoint` cases carry the copy for both surfaces. `[evidence_level: test_verified, confidence: high, not_device_verified, evidence_source: PlanAskServiceTests; LaunchSale.offer; ~/ASC app_sessions and app_lifecycle through 2026-09-14; appStoreVersionLocalizations PATCH HTTP 200 x4 on 2026-09-18]`

- **[General]** **5.3's App Store copy is written, pushed to both platform records, and the project file says 5.3.** The release notes are the first written into the metadata history's template: intro sentence, four capitalised sections in the order a user meets them, `• ` bullets, the closing device line; 2,494 characters. One text for both platforms in both `fastlane/metadata/` and `fastlane/metadata-ios/`, because 5.3 does not diverge and 5.2's macOS-only rewrite is the mistake the history exists to prevent. Promotional text 162 characters, no version number. Pushed with `push_metadata version:5.3` for `ios` and `osx` on 2026-09-18; the first attempt failed with `invalid byte sequence in US-ASCII` because a non-interactive shell gives Ruby no locale and the notes contain `•`, and succeeded with `LC_ALL=en_US.UTF-8` set. `MARKETING_VERSION` moves from 5.2 to 5.3 on all eight targets in `project.pbxproj`, named by the owner, so a local build no longer calls itself 5.2; Xcode Cloud stamps the same value from the changelog heading regardless. Smoke build succeeded and the built app reports 5.3. `[evidence_level: measured, confidence: exact, evidence_source: deliver "Uploading metadata to App Store Connect for localized version en-US" for both platforms; build_simulator_smoke.sh BUILD SUCCEEDED with CFBundleShortVersionString 5.3; plutil -lint OK]`

- **[General]** **The next App Store release is written into a template, and pre-commit checks the shape.** `Docs/Release/APP_STORE_METADATA_HISTORY.md` gained a "Template for the next version" section on 2026-09-18, derived from the 5.1 and 5.2 entries rather than invented: the entry fields, then release notes as an intro sentence, ALL-CAPS section headings, `• ` bullets and a closing device line; promotional text under 170 characters; description under 4,000. The 2026-09-14 gate only proved the history file was touched. `scripts/enforce_docs_hook.sh` now also requires, whenever `fastlane/metadata*` is staged, that the staged history holds a `### <version>` heading for the version `Docs/SHIPPED_VERSION.json` says is being prepared, and that a staged `release_notes.txt` opens with prose, has a capitalised heading, uses `• ` bullets and no dash bullets or em dashes, and fits the limit. Five hook tests cover the entry check, dash bullets, a heading-first opening and an over-long promotional text: 16 passed, 0 failed. Requested by the owner: "any new version that's made follows the same kind of structure as the previous ones". `[evidence_level: test_verified, confidence: high, evidence_source: scripts/test_enforce_docs_hook.sh 16/16 on 2026-09-18; the 5.1 and 5.2 entries in Docs/Release/APP_STORE_METADATA_HISTORY.md]`

- **[Infrastructure]** **`xcode_cloud_toolchain.rb --set 'Xcode 27'` chose the 27.2 beta, and the workflow it was repairing had lost its pin on its own.** On 2026-09-18 Xcode Cloud builds #457 and #458 failed within twenty seconds of creation with no actions and no source commit, the shape of a run that never reached clone. The workflow's `xcodeVersion` relationship was nil: Apple had re-catalogued its images since 2026-09-10, the "Xcode 27 Release Candidate" entry the workflow was pinned to by id was gone, and `27A266a` now appears as "Xcode 27" and "Latest Release". The script's matcher was `id == want || name.start_with?(want)`, Apple lists betas first, so "Xcode 27" resolved to "Xcode 27.2 beta", a toolchain whose builds App Store Connect will not accept for submission. It now tries the exact id, then the exact name, then a prefix that skips betas unless the request names one; the workflow was pinned to the release by direct PATCH the same day. `[evidence_level: measured, confidence: exact, evidence_source: ciWorkflows/<id>?include=xcodeVersion returning no included version on 2026-09-18; ciBuildRuns #457 and #458 with startedDate nil and zero actions; ciXcodeVersions listing the same day]`

- **[UI]** **The paywall sells the product now, and the Annual free trial is gone.** Ninety days of App Store Connect data (`~/ASC`, through 2026-09-14): 326 first-time downloads since February and 29 purchases, so conversion was never the problem; Lifetime was 4 of the last 14 purchases and $140 of $191; the people who buy decide within two days of download; and the upgrade sheet's bullets were "up to 1,000 documents", "10 libraries" and "expanded workspace limits", nothing about the model. A two-day-old user with five documents has nothing left to want from a list like that. **Every paid plan now leads with the one model feature it gates,** Maximum mode with no daily cap, then capacity; the story slides say plainly that Apple Intelligence on the device, Deep Think and consent-gated Private Cloud Compute are free in every plan and that Pro and Lifetime remove the limits. That wording is a rule, recorded in `Docs/BILLING_AND_LIMITS.md`: a plan bullet may name only what that plan gates, so Deep Think and PCC cannot be sold as Pro. The Annual tagline no longer hardcodes "$29.99/yr", since each storefront has its own price and `displayPrice` is the source. **Pro Annual's 7-day free trial is withdrawn:** five starts in ninety days, one paid, two in billing retry, $25 of proceeds. The app stopped advertising it; the 175 per-territory introductory offers in App Store Connect are the owner's to delete, since the harness blocked an agent doing it, and the store's purchase sheet shows the trial until then. The sample Product Guide's plan section carries the same framing and refreshes on device by content hash. The App Store description gains a PLANS block in the same words. `[evidence_level: code_verified, confidence: high, evidence_source: ~/ASC/data/asc.sqlite3 store_purchases, funnel_downloads, sub_state_analytics, sub_event_analytics; PlanUpgradeSheet.swift; QuotaPolicy.swift; App Store Connect subscriptionIntroductoryOffers listing 2026-09-18, 175 FREE_TRIAL ONE_WEEK offers]`

- **[Retrieval]** **The sample library's suggested questions were "What is nothing?", and three things had to go wrong for that.** On the owner's phone on 2026-09-14 the General library, holding the current sample documents, offered "What is nothing?", "What is the silicon?", "What is unlimited maximum?" and "What does the study say about Product Guide?", while every other library's suggestions were good. **1. The curated set was keyed on an exact filename match.** `SuggestedQuestionsService.isSampleWorkspace` compared the library's filenames to the three canonical sample names, so one numbered copy, `RAG-Technical-Architecture-2.md`, left behind by an earlier refresh, was enough to skip the hand-written questions for the generated bank. It now matches sample *identity* (`sampleIdentity(for:)`): canonical or `<stem>-<digits>.md`, all three present, any extra user document ends it. The ingestion-time skip uses the same identity, so a numbered copy is not handed to the generator either. **2. Numbered sample copies survived once the samples were current.** `SampleDocumentManager.refreshStaleSamples` only removed them for samples whose *content* was stale, so a copy from an earlier refresh stayed forever; the manager's own comment records a library at five documents for three samples on 2026-08-27. `removeNumberedSampleCopies` now runs on every refresh pass, and deletes a numbered copy only when its canonical sibling is present: a lone `-2` may be the user's only copy and is kept. **3. The template fallback failed open, and its bank was permanent.** The sample library's bank is built on demand on first launch, when Apple Intelligence is least likely to be ready; with no model, templates ran, and their output was saved to disk and served forever, because nothing ever asked the model again. Sample documents are also the worst input templates can get: conceptual product prose with no specs, procedures or warnings, and headings like "nothing leaves your device" that the topic extractor read as nouns. Now: a bank with no model-written question is rebuilt once per launch when the model is available (`shouldRebuildBank`); with the model present, an empty model result shows no questions rather than raw templates, which is what the file's own header always promised; `isAcceptableTemplateTopic` rejects lone abstract words, article-plus-word phrases and runs of the document's own title; and the research templates key on `documentCategory == .scientificPaper` instead of any passage containing the word "study". **What this does not do:** improve the templates for a device that genuinely has no model; they still ship there, since nothing else can. `[evidence_level: test_verified, confidence: high, not_device_verified, evidence_source: SuggestedQuestionsSampleWorkspaceTests 16/16 pinning the four strings from the screenshot, SampleDocumentCopyCleanupTests 5/5, 2026-09-14; two device screenshots the same day; SampleDocumentManager.swift comment dated 2026-08-27]`

Two hook tests cover both directions, 12/12 with the change. The route's docs (`01_TASK_ROUTER.md` §12, the impact matrix, the command center) and the RUNBOOK release lanes point at it. - **[Infrastructure]** **A TestFlight build now says, in Console.app, where each answer was written.** Until today a Release build emitted nothing about routing to the unified log. Not less; nothing. Every route-naming statement went through the `Log` facade, whose only console output is a `print()` inside `#if DEBUG`, and `print` reaches stdout, which Console.app never sees even from a Debug build on a device; Release also defaults the facade to `.error` with no categories enabled. The only `os.Logger` in the app belonged to the evidence store, and `RAGEngine`'s `OSLog` is signposts. So the one question a tester can ask, whether an answer came from the device or from Private Cloud Compute, had no answer outside the app, and `Response` carries nothing naming the backend to ask Apple instead. `RouteLog` now writes one `notice`-level line when a session is resolved and one when the receipt is written, from both generation paths in `LLMService`, to subsystem `Gunndamental.OpenIntelligence`, category `routing`: actual and intended target, fallback reason code, the Private Cloud Compute reasoning level when that route ran, attempt count, the first eight characters of the plan id, and the policy version. **It deliberately bypasses the `Log` facade** rather than adding a unified-log sink to it, because the facade's `.llm` lines can carry prompt and response text at debug level and section 8 of `Docs/PRIVACY_AND_ROUTING.md` forbids that from any telemetry surface; `RouteLog`'s API takes only enum cases, identifiers and a count, so no call site can hand it content whatever it intended, and a test pins the line's vocabulary to exactly those tokens. `notice` rather than `info` because `info` is held in memory and only written out when a fault occurs nearby, so a healthy app would still show nothing; `privacy: .public` on every field because the unified log otherwise renders them `<private>`, and each is a public target name or a code by construction. The predicate is documented in `Docs/ai/RUNBOOK.md`. **Not device-verified:** Foundation Models does not run in the simulator, so the paths that emit the line never execute here; the roadmap row closes when a TestFlight build shows the pair under that predicate on a phone with no debugger attached. `[evidence_level: test_verified, confidence: high, not_device_verified, evidence_source: RouteLog.swift; LLMService.swift four call sites; RouteLogLineTests 6/6 and LogShareBufferTests 9/9, then the full suite `** TEST SUCCEEDED **` exit 0, 446 executed, 0 failures, all on 2026-09-14; Logger.notice and OSLogPrivacy confirmed in iPhoneOS27.0.sdk os.swiftinterface the same day]`

- **[UI]** **The camera is a Debug-build feature, so 5.3 ships without it.** The owner's call on 2026-09-13: the work is experimental and "should probably wait". `ChatScreen.visionCaptureAction` is now `#if os(iOS) && DEBUG`, which hands `AttachmentPicker` a nil `onVisionCapture`, and its `if let onVisionCapture` guard then renders no menu item. One person on one phone is not evidence that every dismissal path fires. **Verified against a Release build rather than assumed:** `CameraVisionOverlayView`'s own `Vision Capture` string is absent from the Release binary and present in Debug, checked alongside a control string present in both so the instrument is known to work. `AttachmentPicker`'s `Look at Something` label does survive into Release, because that file compiles unconditionally, so the menu item is **unreachable rather than absent** and a grep of the binary will find it.
